[[sep-vplan-area-1]]
==== 1. Crypto and entropy

[[sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test]]
===== 1.1 `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves that one entropy source feeds the Key Manager and AES at once: both draw beats inside one window, each AES beat equals the next word on its own feed, the entropy stack matches its golden at every stage, and the AES first block equals its golden.

*Test Coverage*: One entropy source with Key Manager and AES consuming together.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production image; park OTBN, KMAC and HMAC; leave AES released.
. Bring up entropy with strict golden checking and per-consumer scoring; wait for generated bits; start draining the queue; boot the Key Manager.
. Fork: one key-generate on the Key Manager, concurrently with reseed-and-encrypt cycles on AES.
. Confirm both consumers' beat counts advanced; compare the AES first-block ciphertext against golden.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CONCUR
|Both consumers' beat counts advance within the forked window. This is an in-window advance, not same-cycle arbiter contention: the check would hold equally if one sink drained completely before the other began.
|CHK-AESKAT
|The AES first-block ciphertext equals the independent golden.
|CHK-ROUTING
|Each AES beat equals the next word on its own feed, in order; both consumers' words are members of the golden generated-bits set. This row is the per-sink entropy checker; the scoreboard reports it as `CHK5_aes` / `CHK5_km` / `CHK5_axis1`.
|CHK-NONVAC
|A non-zero number of AXI transactions was observed, so the run cannot pass having done nothing. This is a transaction floor, not a comparison count — the cited line reports zero value-verified reads, because the value comparisons for this test live in the entropy scoreboard rather than the AXI scoreboard.
|CHK1..CHK3
|The shared entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at the decorrelator, compress and seed stages.
|CHK4
|Generated bits match the golden. Generate segmentation is `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, not this `glen=32` budget.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: CHK-ROUTING is reported by the entropy scoreboard as `CHK5_aes` / `CHK5_km` / `CHK5_axis1`, and CHK-NONVAC is the AXI scoreboard's transaction floor; neither carries its own `CHK-` record.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test]]
===== 1.2 `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`target = "lsu_stub_all_live"`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`)

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves the entropy stack is alive end to end on the open-source build and that its output reaches a real consumer.

*Test Coverage*: Entropy stack seed-out to a real consumer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped.
. Release the Key Manager with a small entropy-consuming ROM image.
. Configure the source, generator and distribution blocks in order and enable them.
. Start the golden scoreboard; drain the source queue through the front door.
. Wait for generated bits, for the Key Manager to take a word, and for a non-zero word to appear in its memory. The stored word is checked for presence, not value.
. Check the generator and distribution error and alert status.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ALERTS-ZERO
|Generator and distribution error and alert status are clear. The four status registers read are the generator's `0x10915054` / `0x10915050` and the distribution block's `0x1091583c` / `0x10915838`, each required to be zero.
|CHK1
|Decorrelated output matches the golden model.
|CHK2
|Compressed output, observed at the queue front door, matches golden.
|CHK3
|The seed matches golden.
|CHK4
|Generated bits match golden. Generate segmentation is `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, not this `glen=32` budget.
|CHK5
|The Key Manager consumes an entropy word and it matches golden.
|CHK5_pool
|Mux endpoint [2] (entropy-pool adapter): each post-adapter pool beat equals the next AXIS2 word, and `pool_edn_fips_o=1` on every completed beat, so the adapter forwarded the DRBG FIPS bit. Does not drain `0x1095_0000`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Variant*: `sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test` runs this module with `+esrc_fifo_closed` and `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE=0`; see <<sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test>>.

[[esrc-noise-exception]]
*What `+esrc_noise_force` is, and what it costs*: a ring oscillator is a
free-running combinational loop — `entropy_ring_oscillator` is a NAND-plus-buffer
delay chain closed on itself — and no event-driven simulator produces physical
randomness from it. Injection is therefore the only way to exercise the stack,
and it is applied at the decorrelator noise input, upstream of every stage this
plan checks. This is the same node and the same default mode the entropy IP's own
testbench uses.

*Not claimed*: the injection point sits downstream of the entire noise source —
the ring oscillator, the metastable sampling flop, and the sample-clock-to-core-clock
synchroniser — and the oscillator tune/detune path drives that same stage. All of
that is real logic, and no test in this plan exercises it: no testlist entry runs
this stack with the injection off. Entropy quality, oscillator tuning and health
tests over real noise belong to the entropy IP's own testbench. Every stage after
the injection point is real logic and is golden-checked.

'''''

[[sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test]]
===== 1.3 `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Walks the nine-cell product of {ECB, CBC, CTR} x {128, 192, 256} in one invocation, with key, IV and plaintext randomised per cell.

*Test Coverage*: Nine AES cells: {ECB, CBC, CTR} × {128, 192, 256}.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense` and park non-AES EDN clients.
. Walk all nine {ECB, CBC, CTR} × {128, 192, 256} cells against `sep_aes_golden`.
. Score CHK1..CHK4 bit-exact and CHK5_aes routing.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CELL
|Per cell: the ciphertext equals the golden, the round trip returns the plaintext, and no alert fired. For ECB and CBC the round trip programs a real DECRYPT operation; for CTR it re-runs ENCRYPT, which is a keystream-determinism check rather than a decrypt.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Every mode x key-size cell ran in one invocation and produced a *distinct* ciphertext, so the stimulus stream is live rather than repeating one configuration. Note the key, IV and plaintext are re-drawn per cell, so this does not by itself prove the cells programmed different modes — the per-cell golden compare is what proves that.
|CHK1..CHK4
|Strict entropy scoreboard: the decorrelator, compress, seed and genbits stages each match the chained golden bit-exactly. OTBN, KMAC and HMAC are parked, so AES is the only crypto EDN client.
|CHK5_aes
|Each post-adapter AES beat equals the next AXIS1 word — the single-sink routing golden. The Key Manager is unused on this path. The minimum-evidence floor is raised to 32 scored beats, so the per-beat claim cannot be discharged by a single beat.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test]]
===== 1.4 `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Walks 17 cells: keyed HMAC over every legal key length for each of SHA-256/384/512, plus plain SHA for each variant.

*Test Coverage*: Seventeen HMAC/SHA cells across SHA-256/384/512 and legal key lengths.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`.
. Walk the 17 HMAC/SHA cells against `sep_hmac_golden`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CONV
|The engine honours the *specified* software-key register convention. `hmac.hjson` (the `KEY` multireg) defines `key[1023:0] = {KEY0, ... , KEY31}`, so `KEY_0` is the most-significant word and no word reversal is needed; `hmac.sv` implements it (`secret_key_d` from `reg2hw.key[31-i]`). The convention is pinned from the specification, not discovered by asking the engine which one it agrees with, and not transcribed from the RTL it checks — see <<audit>>. On mismatch the failure reports whether the reversed order would have matched, which separates a key word-order regression from a general digest fault.
|CHK-CELL
|Per cell: the digest equals the golden, the done interrupt sets and clears on write-one-to-clear, and the error status is clean.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Every legal cell ran, each producing a distinct digest. The matrix is derived from the generated digest-size and key-length banks minus `HMAC_ILLEGAL_KEYED`, which the sequence computes from the register specification's block-size rule (`hmac.adoc`: the key length cannot exceed the block size, 512-bit for SHA-2 256) rather than from the engine's own bound. It is the single source of truth for legality, so the sequence and this walk cannot drift apart. As on the AES sweep, the key and message are re-drawn per cell, so distinctness alone does not prove the cells programmed different variants — the per-cell golden compare is what proves that.
|SKIP-ILLEGAL-KEYED
|Each digest-size / key-length pair the engine rejects as `invalid_config` is named in the log with its reason, rather than being dropped silently from the matrix. A declaration, not a checker: no negative cell is driven, so nothing here provokes `ERR_CODE`.
|CHK-ERR
|`ERR_CODE` and the error interrupt bit read zero across the walk. This is a passive observation: nothing in this entry induces an error, so there is no positive control that the register can ever be non-zero on this path.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test]]
===== 1.5 `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Thirteen cells across SHA3-224/256/384/512, SHAKE-128/256, cSHAKE-128/256 and KMAC-128/256 spanning all five key lengths.

*Test Coverage*: Thirteen KMAC/SHA3/SHAKE/cSHAKE cells across the legal key lengths.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`.
. Walk the thirteen SHA3/SHAKE/cSHAKE/KMAC cells against `sep_kmac_golden`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CELL
|Per cell: the digest equals the golden, and the error status is clean.
|CHK-DONE-RW1C
|The done interrupt is observed *set*, written to clear, and re-read as zero — a real three-step contract rather than a single sample.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Every cell ran, each producing a distinct digest. The walk covers the complete legal SHA3 strength set (224/256/384/512) and all five KMAC key lengths. As on the AES sweep, the key and message are re-drawn per cell, so distinctness alone does not prove the cells programmed different modes — the per-cell golden compare is what proves that.
|CHK-ERR
|`ERR_CODE` and the error interrupt bit read zero across the walk. Passive observation only: nothing in this entry induces an error, so there is no positive control that the register can ever be non-zero on this path.
|CHK1..CHK4
|Strict entropy scoreboard: the decorrelator, compress, seed and genbits stages each match the chained golden bit-exactly. OTBN, AES and HMAC are parked, so KMAC is the only crypto EDN client.
|CHK5_kmac
|Each post-adapter KMAC beat equals the next AXIS1 word — the single-sink routing golden. The Key Manager is unused on this path. The minimum-evidence floor is 3 scored beats. KMAC draws entropy once per operation, not per block, so the floor sits below the beats one walk delivers.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Shared-code limit*: the SP800-185 encoders (`right_encode`, `encode_string`) are
shared between the stimulus and the golden, so an encoder error would program the
DUT and build the expectation identically. This is mitigated rather than
eliminated: the golden's import-time KATs exercise those same encoders against
NIST samples.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test]]
===== 1.6 `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Two crypto sinks draw EDN entropy concurrently and both complete correctly: each AES fork block matches its golden bit-exact and the KMAC digest matches its own, while both are shown to have taken real post-adapter EDN beats inside the contention window. An AES-alone baseline in which KMAC takes zero beats is the control that keeps the both-beats check from passing vacuously.

*Test Coverage*: AES and KMAC draw EDN together, plus an AES-alone control.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense` and `+esrc_noise_force`. Resolve the AES plaintext and the KMAC message from the seed and log them.
. Start the entropy stack in strict mode, with the AES and KMAC sinks scored against the golden. Wait for CSRNG genbits and start the FIFO drain.
. Reseed the AES masking PRNG once and configure AES-256-ECB.
. Load the AES key and encrypt one block with KMAC idle. Require the ciphertext to match the golden and the KMAC sink to have zero beats (CHK-NONVAC). Require at least one AES beat (CHK-SINK-BIND).
. Record both sink beat counts. Start two concurrent tasks: AES reloads the key and encrypts two blocks, and KMAC runs two keyed KMAC-256 operations. Require every AES block and every KMAC digest to match its golden (CHK-BOTH-COMPLETE).
. Require each sink's beat count to rise across the fork (CHK-BOTH-BEATS). Require the AES and KMAC beat time spans in the fork to overlap (CHK-OVERLAP).
. Require clean AES and KMAC status, stop the FIFO drain, and require zero CSRNG and EDN alerts.
. Call the strict `report()`. It grades CHK1..CHK4 and the per-sink routing against the AXIS1 grant stream (CHK-ROUTING, CHK-MEMBERSHIP).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|An AES-alone baseline reproduces its known-answer ciphertext while the KMAC sink takes *zero* beats — a real control showing the both-beats check below is not always true.
|CHK-SINK-BIND
|The same AES-alone baseline gives the AES sink at least one AES-named beat, so the per-sink beat attribution the later checkers use is bound to the AES client and not to the adapter order.
|CHK-BOTH-COMPLETE
|Under contention both clients complete correctly: every AES fork block matches its golden bit-exact, and the KMAC digest matches its own golden.
|CHK-BOTH-BEATS
|Both crypto sinks took real post-adapter EDN beats during the contention window.
|CHK-OVERLAP
|The two sinks' beat windows overlap in time, so one DRBG time-multiplexed two live clients. This is interval-span containment, *not* interleaving: it is satisfied when one client's entire burst falls in a gap between two of the other's beats. Same-cycle dual `edn_req` and alternating grants are <<sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test>>, not this entry.
|CHK-ROUTING
|Each AES post-adapter beat and each KMAC post-adapter beat equals the AXIS1 word the adapter granted that cycle. Unscored clients (OTBN wipe) still consume AXIS1 words; those grants are popped so AES/KMAC stay aligned.
|CHK-MEMBERSHIP
|Every word either sink received is a member of the CHK4 genbits golden set (AXIS1 is chained to genbits in `report()`).
|CHK1..CHK4
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at each stage, in strict mode, with `report()` called. Generate segmentation is `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, not this budget. CHK1's golden initial state is seeded from the live RTL shift register after reset, so CHK1 verifies the *recurrence* rather than the reset phase, and CHK2..CHK4 take the previous verified stage's DUT output as their input.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test]]
===== 1.7 `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves that the EDN fan-out `u_axis_edn_crypto_s3c_scan` (`drbg_axis_edn_adapter`), which `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` describes as a small round-robin native-protocol fan-out to AES, KMAC, OTBN RND and OTBN URND, grants and routes entropy to all four clients under full load, with the entropy pool scored as a fifth endpoint.

*Test Coverage*: EDN fan-out under its full client load. AES and OTBN URND are graded for sharing and an alternating grant prefix; round-robin order under simultaneous request is not proven, and a fixed-priority arbiter is not excluded; OTBN RND and KMAC are graded for routing and consume; the entropy pool is scored as a concurrent fifth endpoint.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped.
. Hold AES PRNG reseed and OTBN URND `edn_req` together before EDN is enabled.
. Bring up the entropy stack (noise force, seed, then enable EDN).
. Sample `crypto_edn_ack_o` for a fixed grant budget, then let the checkers decide. The collection loop does not stop on the alternation condition.
. Then load a four-read RND program into OTBN IMEM and issue EXECUTE without polling STATUS, so the blocked `crypto_edn_req[2]` is observable. RND cannot join the dual-request window above: it only requests while a program is blocked on the RND CSR, and OTBN cannot execute until its post-reset secure wipe has consumed URND, which already needs EDN enabled.
. Wait for IDLE, read `ERR_BITS`, and read the four RND words the program stored to DMEM.
. Then run a keyed KMAC-256 cell, the adapter's fourth client, and compare its digest against the Keccak golden.
. Collect the set of client bits granted anywhere in the run from the monitor.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DUAL-REQ
|At least one cycle both selected clients have `edn_req` high. Without this, CHK-GRANT-ALT is vacuous.
|CHK-GRANT-ALT
|After that dual request, a prefix of consecutive post-adapter grants strictly alternates between the two clients. A grant stream that is one client then the other, with no alternating prefix, fails. The prefix necessarily contains both clients, since only the two are recorded, so that is not scored separately. Same-cycle dual-`edn_req` grants are not scored: AES reseed pulses, so an ack-time dual sample can be AES-only. This is sharing plus an alternating prefix, not a proof of round-robin order: a fixed-priority arbiter that serves URND whenever AES has dropped `edn_req` produces the same stream and passes.
|CHK-NO-STARVE
|Each requesting client receives at least one `edn_ack` in the window.
|CHK-ROUTING
|Each AES post-adapter beat and each OTBN URND post-adapter beat equals the AXIS1 word the adapter granted that cycle.
|CHK1..CHK4
|Strict entropy scoreboard on the same bring-up that feeds the dual-request window: FIFO drain after the seed, then `report()`.
|CHK-RND-REQ
|`crypto_edn_req[2]` (OTBN RND) is high while the loaded program is blocked on the RND CSR. Without this request the adapter never grants RND and CHK5_otbn_rnd cannot fail, so this is the control for it.
|CHK-RND-CONSUME
|The RND program retires with `ERR_BITS` == 0 and the four words it stored to DMEM are all distinct — which already leaves at most one of them zero, so an all-zero stream is not scored separately. Each CSR read of RND is served from a fresh 256-bit EDN fetch, so a DUT that latched one value and replayed it, or that returned the reset value, fails here. This is the consume proof at the OTBN end; CHK5_otbn_rnd is the routing proof at the adapter, and neither implies the other.
|CHK-KMAC-CLIENT
|The fourth adapter client completes a keyed KMAC-256 whose digest equals the Keccak golden. A client that took EDN beats but produced the wrong digest is not a working client, so the digest rather than the beat count is what is graded here. The value matrix itself is walked by `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`; this cell is fixed.
|CHK-FOUR-CLIENT
|Every one of the adapter's four clients was granted at some point in the run. The alternation sampler watches only AES and OTBN URND, so this is taken from the per-client grant bits accumulated across the whole run.
|CHK5_aes / CHK5_kmac / CHK5_otbn_rnd / CHK5_otbn_urnd / CHK5_pool
|All four crypto sinks match the AXIS1 grant-order stream and the entropy pool matches the AXIS2 stream, with zero mismatches on all five. RND carries the only stimulus-derived floor: one match per 32-bit beat of every RND CSR read the program issues. The AES, OTBN URND, KMAC and entropy-pool floors are non-vacuity floors -- above zero, below the traffic any seed produces -- so they fail a sink that took nothing and do not bound one that started and then starved; the zero-mismatch requirement plus the grant-stream checkers are what is claimed for those four. Alternation is claimed for AES and OTBN URND only: RND and KMAC are granted outside that window, so this entry claims their routing and their consume, not their arbitration.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Standing rule*: any test that moves data across the DRBG runs CHK1..CHK4
(strict scoreboard, FIFO drain, `report()`) and enables CHK5 on every live sink.
CHK5 mode is the strongest the stimulus allows: golden routing when the adapter
grant order is observed from `edn_ack` (one or more crypto sinks); membership
when the pull order is firmware-driven; observe when even membership is not
scored (`rom_main` KM). This test uses golden routing.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test]]
===== 1.8 `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force +km_rom_hex=km_rom_entropy.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Phase 2 DRBG iconic feature: a CSRNG Generate command completes at the commanded length and `gen_last` asserts. The trailing CTR_DRBG Update is not observed: this vehicle completes exactly one Generate, so no golden-compared block lands after that Update.

*Test Coverage*: CSRNG Generate at the commanded length and `gen_last`. The trailing Update is out of scope for this vehicle.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped.
. Release the Key Manager with the small entropy-consuming ROM image.
. Program the boot Generate and the auto Generate to `glen=4` from the same config object that feeds the golden.
. Bring up the entropy stack, drain the source queue through the front door, and wait for generated bits and a Key Manager consume.
. Value-compare the word the KM firmware stored to KM SRAM against the word the DUT delivered on the AXIS endpoint.
. Require at least one completed Generate of exactly four blocks, then report the strict CHK1..CHK5 scoreboard.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SEGMENTATION
|At least one Generate command completed, and every completed command carried exactly the commanded `glen=4` blocks — so `gen_last` was observed and a segment of any other length would fail. *The trailing CTR_DRBG Update is not observed*: it fires after the last block of a command, and this vehicle completes exactly one command, so no golden-compared block lands on its far side. EDN issues no second Generate on this vehicle, so the poll window is not what bounds the observation.
|CHK1..CHK4
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at each stage, in strict mode, with `report()` called. Bit-exactness *across* a trailing Update boundary is not claimed — see CHK-SEGMENTATION for why this vehicle cannot reach one.
|CHK5_km
|The Key Manager consumes an entropy word on the post-mux AXIS endpoint and it matches golden bit-exactly — the same consumer close as the end-to-end smoke. It says nothing about Key Manager *memory*; `CHK5_km_sram` is the row that covers the store.
|CHK5_km_sram
|The word the Key Manager firmware stored to KM SRAM equals the word the DUT delivered on the AXIS endpoint. `km_rom_entropy.S` reads one DRBG-sampler DATA word and stores exactly that word to KM SRAM base + 0, so a wrong-word store, a dropped store, or a store to the wrong offset fails here — where a non-zero-only liveness poll would pass. The stored value is a genbits word and so differs per run; only the invariant is claimed.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: CHK5_km_sram is logged as `CHK-KM-SRAM`.

*Scope limit*: `km_rom_entropy.S` reads one DRBG-sampler DATA word and stores
exactly that word to KM SRAM base + 0, so `CHK5_km_sram` compares that one
delivered word against KM SRAM word 0; it does not claim a sequence of stores. A
second AUTO Generate after the Update is not claimed — the Key Manager ROM reads
one word and parks, which is enough for the legality check to execute.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test]]
===== 1.9 `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the per-IP software reset domains are isolated, using live crypto results rather than a poked status bit: each engine computes a golden-checked value, then one engine is reset and the other's held result must survive bit-exact. A software reset of OTBN, AES, or KMAC cancels that engine's EDN endpoints one cycle before its gated reset and leaves the other endpoints alone. The HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge domains also grade drain-before-reset. ABR grades the JTAG `IC_RESET` override on this leaf; its live-result isolation is on <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>.

*Test Coverage*: Per-IP software reset; neighbour crypto result survives bit-exact; crypto EDN endpoints cancelled ahead of an OTBN, AES, or KMAC software reset, with the held word never acknowledged afterwards; a KMAC software reset keeps the KMAC EDN response at 0 while URND completes its own beat; HMAC, KMAC and ABR drain-before-reset; ABR JTAG override.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Compute a golden-checked result on two engines.
. Reset one engine; the other's held result must survive bit-exact. Both directions.
. Hold HMAC in software reset and prove the same `DIGEST_0` read and a `CFG` write complete SLVERR, while the sibling AES port stays OKAY.
. Hold the KMAC reset and prove the gated reset waits for both its host and Key Manager paths to isolate, and that a read arriving inside the host-path drain window resolves. Accepted-traffic drain is not claimed for KMAC; see *Drain-before-reset* below.
. Hold the Adams Bridge reset and prove the gated reset waits for both the full-AXI host path and the shared Key Manager path to isolate, that in-flight host reads resolve, and that the JTAG override holds the domain while `SW_RESET_N` still shows it released.
. Before EDN is enabled, hold an OTBN URND request and an AES request. Resetting the requester drops that request with `edn_ack` low. A KMAC reset in the same window leaves the AES request high. With OTBN then held in reset, enabling EDN still grants AES, and releasing OTBN lets URND be granted.
. Once URND is being granted, re-arm that stream and step a KMAC software reset across one measured acknowledge period. On each gated-reset edge the KMAC endpoint's `edn_ack` and `edn_bus` are 0. URND may acknowledge on an edge; the word it presents is the word it had staged. A quiet edge does not drop URND `edn_req` unless the previous cycle already acknowledged, and does not replace a requested word with a different word.
. Software-reset KMAC and AES twice each while their endpoint holds the word of its last acknowledge with `edn_req` low, and OTBN six times at a different offset from a URND acknowledge each time. On the last cycle before the engine's gated reset falls, while it is still released, the engine's endpoints show `edn_ack` and `edn_bus` at 0, and they stay 0 until the gated reset releases.
. Step a KMAC software reset across the phase at which URND holds a staged word, and require that word to survive the edge and be the word URND is acknowledged with.
. Record every crypto EDN acknowledge through these steps. No word an endpoint held when its engine was reset is acknowledged to any client afterwards.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|Both held results are golden-matched and survive a *second* independent read, so the survives/cleared checks below are observations rather than restatements of one sample.
|CHK-SELF-RESET
|The AES reset pulse perturbs its own output. This is an inequality, not a pinned value — the masking-presented `DATA_OUT` after reset is a secure-wipe PRNG pattern rather than a fixed constant. The compensating evidence is that the entry re-derives the exact golden ciphertext afterwards, so the domain provably reset *and* recovered.
|CHK-NEIGHBOR-SURVIVES
|The HMAC digest is bit-exact intact across the AES reset.
|CHK-REVERSE
|The reverse direction: the HMAC reset clears its own digest to the exact reset value, and the AES output is bit-exact intact.
|CHK-KMAC-SELF
|KMAC `SW_RESET_N` restores `STATUS` to its register-map reset on the fields the RDL resets (`sha3_idle`, `fifo_empty`, the two alert bits) and to the idle 0 on the fields with no RDL reset (`sha3_absorb`, `sha3_squeeze`, `fifo_depth`, `fifo_full`, per their field descriptions), and STATE (share0^share1) reads 0, while HMAC DIGEST stays bit-exact. `sha3_squeeze` reads 1 before the pulse.
|CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ORDER
|The Adams Bridge reset does not assert until both paths its domain depends on
report isolated: the full-AXI SEP host path and the shared Key Manager path. The
host isolate bit is 0 when the request is issued. The Key Manager path may
already be isolated (the request is the OR of this engine and the held Key
Manager isolate). The two are sampled every cycle from the reset request, so the
order is observed at the assert edge rather than read off a settled host path. A
design that never isolates the host path never releases the sequencer and fails
here.
|CHK-ABR-HOST-DRAIN
|Host reads issued in the same cycle as the ABR reset request resolve OKAY or
SLVERR, never DECERR and never a hang. The test records the event identities
still unretired at the cycle the isolate request rises, requires that set to be
non-empty, and requires at least one event from that exact set to drain OKAY.
Thus an earlier completed read cannot satisfy the drain verdict, and the leg
grades traffic the isolate had to drain rather than beats that had already
completed.
|CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ARRIVAL
|The `CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL` contract on the ABR full-AXI isolate: the arrival AR
is accepted on `s_axi` while the isolate request is up and the gated reset is
still released, and the read then resolves. It may drain or terminate; it may
not hang.
|CHK-ABR-JTAG-OVERRIDE
|The JTAG `IC_RESET` override holds the ABR domain while `SW_RESET_N`, read back
inside the window, still reports it released -- so the hold is attributable to
JTAG rather than to software -- and the domain returns when the override
releases. Both mux directions are graded.
|CHK-KMAC-NEIGHBOR
|HMAC `SW_RESET_N` leaves KMAC STATE bit-exact intact.
|CHK-OTBN
|OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM` (rst_ni CSR) survives an AES reset and clears to its register-map reset on OTBN's own pulse. DMEM holds the staged mark across the AES neighbour pulse (cross-domain leak). OTBN's own reset runs a secure wipe, so DMEM retention across that pulse is not claimed.
|CHK-ISOLATE-PRE
|HMAC `DIGEST_0` answers OKAY with the live golden digest before the isolate request, so the later SLVERR is at a live mapped address.
|CHK-ISOLATE-LANDED
|`SW_RESET_N` readback shows the HMAC bit held, so a refusal cannot be a missed write.
|CHK-ISOLATE-SLVERR
|During the window a read of the same `DIGEST_0` completes SLVERR, not OKAY, DECERR, or hang, and the beat does not carry the live digest. `reset_controller.adoc` names SLVERR for a transaction presented while isolated; it does not name the error data.
|CHK-ISOLATE-WR
|During the window a write to HMAC `CFG` completes SLVERR. The write target is the RW `CFG` register, not the RO digest.
|CHK-ISOLATE-SIBLING
|AES `DATA_OUT_0` answers OKAY with the held ciphertext in the same HMAC window (port-scoped, not a global bus kill).
|CHK-DRAIN-ORDER
|The HMAC gated reset does not assert until both AXI-Lite paths that domain depends on — the SEP host path and the Key Manager path — report isolated. The host isolate bit is 0 when the request is issued. The Key Manager path may already be isolated: that request is the OR of this engine and the Key Manager isolate, and the Key Manager stays at its reset default on this leaf. Read from the two named TB probe bits, sampled every cycle so the assert edge cannot be missed.
|CHK-HOST-DRAIN
|Host reads issued in the same cycle as the reset request resolve OKAY or SLVERR — never DECERR and never a hang. The event identities still unretired when the isolate request rises are captured, and at least one event from that exact set must resolve OKAY, so a read completed before the drain window cannot satisfy the verdict.
|CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL
|A read *accepted inside an observed drain window* also resolves. The window is the cycles in which the HMAC host isolate request probe is high and the gated reset is still released. The arrival read is issued on the first such cycle, with an empty AR queue and its own AXI ID, and its `s_axi` AR handshake must fall inside the window. The log records the handshake time, the probe values at it, and the reset edge. It may drain (OKAY) or be terminated (SLVERR); it may not hang.
|CHK-ISOLATE-REOPEN
|After release the same `DIGEST_0` answers OKAY again. This is also what shows the drain did not leave the path wedged.
|CHK-ISOLATE-RESET-DEFAULT
|After release `DIGEST_0` reads its reset value 0.
|CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER
|The KMAC gated reset does not assert until both the host and Key Manager AXI-Lite paths report isolated, sampled in the same cycle as the observed assert edge. The host isolate bit and the gated reset are 0 / released when the request is issued. The Key Manager path may already be isolated (same OR as HMAC; Key Manager held). A domain that asserted with no host-path drain window fails rather than scoring a steady state.
|CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ARRIVAL
|The `CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL` contract on the KMAC host path: a read issued on the first cycle the KMAC host isolate request is seen has its AR accepted on `s_axi` while the request is up and the gated reset is still released, and it resolves OKAY or SLVERR. Fails explicitly if no window was ever observed or the handshake falls after the reset edge.
|CHK-TRNG-NEIGHBORS
|Parking the shared TRNG reset holds the TRNG `SW_RESET_N` bit and leaves the five accelerator bits released (OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC, ABR). HMAC DIGEST and AES DATA_OUT stay unchanged in that window. KMAC STATE, OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM` and the ABR datapath are not sampled here.
|CHK-EDN-PEND
|Before EDN is enabled, OTBN URND `edn_req` is high and `edn_ack` is low. `CHK-EDN-PEND-AES` is the same contract for the AES client, armed by a PRNG reseed that cannot yet be granted.
|CHK-EDN-FLUSH
|Resetting OTBN drops URND `edn_req` and URND `edn_ack` stays low. `CHK-EDN-FLUSH-AES` is the same contract for AES. `CHK-EDN-FLUSH-KMAC` resets KMAC while AES is still requesting: AES `edn_req` stays high and AES `edn_ack` stays low, so resetting one endpoint does not cancel or acknowledge a sibling.
|CHK-EDN-SIBLING
|With OTBN held in reset after that flush, and EDN enabled, AES receives `edn_ack`. URND `edn_req` and `edn_ack` stay low for the whole wait, so the cancelled request is not acknowledged once entropy is flowing.
|CHK-EDN-REARM
|Releasing OTBN lets URND assert `edn_req` again and receive `edn_ack`.
|CHK-EDN-EDGE
|After that grant stream is up, OTBN is re-armed and a KMAC software reset is stepped one cycle at a time across one measured URND acknowledge period. On each falling edge of the KMAC gated reset, KMAC `edn_ack` and `edn_bus` are 0. The walk includes at least one edge where URND `edn_ack` is high, and the bus on that edge is URND's own staged word. An edge with URND `edn_ack` low does not drop URND `edn_req` unless the previous cycle already acknowledged, and does not replace a requested word with a different word. The software-reset write is an AXI transaction and outlasts one KMAC seed, so an outstanding KMAC request on the edge is graded only when one occurs; `CHK-EDN-LEAD` grades the cancel ahead of the reset. The armed staged-word hold is `CHK-EDN-HOLD`.
|CHK-EDN-LEAD
|A software reset of KMAC, AES or OTBN cancels that engine's crypto EDN endpoints before its gated reset asserts. Each reset is requested while the endpoint holds a word with `edn_ack` low. For KMAC and AES the engine's `edn_req` is also low, so the acknowledge state machine has no request to pop that word for and only the cancel can clear it. On the last cycle before the gated reset falls, sampled from the gated-reset TB probe while it is still released, `edn_ack` and `edn_bus` are 0 on every endpoint of the engine (OTBN RND and URND together), and they stay 0 on every cycle the gated reset is asserted. OTBN URND requests continuously and its endpoint reads 0 on the cycle a word is popped, so its six trials start at a different offset from a URND acknowledge and every one must show the quiet cycle. A design that clears the endpoint with its reset, instead of ahead of it, fails here.
|CHK-EDN-DISCARD
|Every crypto EDN acknowledge through the EDN steps is recorded as client and word. No word an endpoint held when its engine's reset was requested is acknowledged to any client after that request. Fails if no reset endpoint held a word, so it cannot pass on an empty set.
|CHK-EDN-HOLD
|A software reset of KMAC must not rewrite a sibling's in-flight EDN data. Armed on an edge where URND held `edn_req` with a word on `edn_bus` and no acknowledge yet, and that same word is still present on the edge. The acknowledge state machine pops its endpoint FIFO on some cycles where `edn_ack` is already low; the next cycle reads 0 because the FIFO is empty, and that gap is not the arm. An edge that presents a different word fails. If every candidate edge zeroes the staged word, the check fails. It also fails if no attempt arms, so it cannot pass on an idle bus. `prim_edn_req` feeds `{edn_fips, edn_bus}` into `prim_sync_reqack_data` with `DataSrc2Dst=0`, whose `SyncReqAckDataHoldDst2Src` contract requires that data to hold across the handshake window.
|CHK-EDN-HOLD-KEEP
|The word URND had staged when the unrelated engine was reset must be the word URND is acknowledged with, within a bounded recovery window. The acknowledge may be the reset-edge cycle itself.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: `CHK-HMAC-DRAIN-OPEN`, `CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-OPEN` and `CHK-ABR-DRAIN-OPEN` record the opening clause of CHK-DRAIN-ORDER, CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER and CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ORDER: when the reset request is issued, the host isolate bit is 0 and the gated reset is still released.

*Not claimed*: the JTAG `IC_RESET` override of OTBN, AES or KMAC. It bypasses the endpoint cancel along with the isolation sequencing, so the EDN checks above take their resets from `SW_RESET_N` only. A word committed to an endpoint in the cycle its cancel rises is not targeted at SoC level: the software-reset write outlasts one engine seed. The adapter waives the arbiter request checks only on the first cycle of an endpoint flush; `AxisEdnCancelledEndpointIdle_A` and `AxisEdnReqStableUnlessEndpointCancelled_A` are the per-endpoint cancel and request-stability checks, enabled for all four crypto endpoints. Every assertion-based contract here needs VCS -- `prim_assert.sv` withholds `OCAH_OT_INC_ASSERT` when `VERILATOR` is defined, so the arbiter and adapter assertions do not exist in the default Verilator build.

*Scope limit*: live-result isolation walks AES, HMAC, KMAC and OTBN. Drain-before-reset and the JTAG override walk Adams Bridge on this leaf; ABR live-result isolation is on <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>. The Key Manager bit stays at its reset default (a different mechanism) and is not claimed.

*Drain-before-reset*: claimed for the HMAC domain by `CHK-DRAIN-ORDER`,
`CHK-HOST-DRAIN` and `CHK-DRAIN-ARRIVAL`, for the Adams Bridge domain by
`CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ORDER`, `CHK-ABR-HOST-DRAIN` and `CHK-ABR-DRAIN-ARRIVAL`,
and for the KMAC domain by `CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ORDER` and `CHK-KMAC-DRAIN-ARRIVAL`.
The KMAC leg does not claim accepted-traffic drain: its drain reads go out with
the request only to give the isolate traffic, and their outcome is not graded. Adams Bridge does carry that property, on a full-AXI isolate. HMAC
drain runs against a live golden-checked result on a path this entry isolates.
ABR drain counts the host reads still outstanding when the isolate request
rises, so it grades traffic the isolate has to retire. The shared TRNG domain grades drain-before-reset in
<<sep_trng_reset_recovery_test>>.

*Not claimed*: drain-before-reset for the AES and OTBN domains — the
mechanism is shared, but a per-domain demonstration is not made here. Key
Manager isolate termination is not claimed here: `CHK-DRAIN-ORDER` reads the
`km_hmac` isolate bit, which is already isolated while the Key Manager is held
at its reset default, so it grades neither the KM path reaching isolation nor
what a KM transaction receives. That response is graded for `km_hmac` in
<<sep_km_isolate_termination_test>>, which runs a KM CPU image so the
transaction comes from the master that owns the path. SMC CPU-MMIO isolate is
out of this subsystem.

'''''

[[sep_trng_reset_recovery_test]]
===== 1.10 `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves the one `SW_RESET_N` bit that resets ESRC, CSRNG and EDN
together behaves as a single coordinated domain: the reset waits for all three
AXI ports to isolate, its downstream clear changes only after two `clk_i`
synchronizer edges, the entropy pool scrubs whatever it held, the external source
path stays outside the domain, and recovery admits only fresh entropy.

*Test Coverage*: Shared TRNG `SW_RESET_N` drain-before-reset and ordered recovery.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the internal chain up and fill the fabric entropy pool through
  ESRC → CSRNG → EDN, recording the pool level and that `FIPS_LOCK` is set.
. Write a source-select value that is not the RDL reset and confirm the write
  took. That non-reset value is the pre-reset baseline the later check must
  still see.
. Hold every entropy consumer (KM, OTBN, AES, KMAC), then request the TRNG reset
  while concurrent reads are in flight on all three internal CSR ports.
. Through the reset, observe the isolate probe, the fate of the in-flight
  accesses, the two-stage clear synchronizer, and the pool's packer depth.
. With the domain in reset, read the three internal ports and the
  source-select register; then exercise the JTAG reset pair to hold and
  release the same coordinated reset.
. Reinitialize ESRC, CSRNG and EDN with consumers still held, require a fresh
  seed and fresh pool progress, and only then restore the consumer resets.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TRNG-FILL
|The initial DRBG produces genbits and `FIPS_LOCK` reads set, so the pool level the later checks compare against is real entropy rather than a reset default.
|CHK-TRNG-ISOLATE-ALL
|While the reset is requested the isolate probe reads all three ports isolated. This is the drain-before-reset evidence: the reset does not assert until ESRC, CSRNG and EDN have all reported isolated.
|CHK-TRNG-DRAIN
|Every in-flight pre-reset access completes either OKAY or SLVERR — never DECERR and never a hang — and at least one completes OKAY, so accepted traffic provably drained rather than being dropped.
|CHK-TRNG-CLEAR-SYNC
|The reset-derived clear remains inactive when the asynchronous TRNG reset is first observed and asserts within the following two observed `clk_i` edges.
|CHK-TRNG-PACKER
|The entropy pool's packer depth is zero, so a half-packed word does not survive the reset.
|CHK-TRNG-SLVERR
|All three internal CSR ports answer SLVERR while isolated.
|CHK-TRNG-EXTERNAL
|After the packer is parked the test writes a source-select value that is not the RDL reset of `sel[2:0]` (`0x7` in `sep_cpu_ctrl.rdl`), confirms the write took, then asserts the same value after the internal reset. A match on the reset value would be vacuous. The `0x1091_7000` passthrough is an adopter aperture; intra-window decode is not graded.
|CHK-TRNG-JTAG
|The JTAG override holds the coordinated reset asserted while software releases it. Clearing the override leaves the gated reset low until `clk_i` has sampled it: still low a picosecond later and on the first edge, released on the second or third edge. The downstream clear remains asserted through the first `clk_i` edge after that release and releases after the second.
|CHK-TRNG-CONSUMERS-HELD
|The `SW_RESET_N` readback shows the four consumers still held and the TRNG bit released, so the recovery ordering is observed rather than assumed.
|CHK-TRNG-CSR-RESET
|After the reset `FIPS_LOCK` is clear, the CSRNG and EDN interrupt enables return to zero from the non-zero masks written before the reset, and reserved ESRC `CTRL[0]` reads zero before a write of 1 and still reads zero after — RAZ and WI, not write-ignore alone. The interrupt enables are driven off reset first, so this is a 1->0 traversal rather than a read of two registers that were never written.
|CHK-TRNG-STALE
|A pop from the empty pool answers SLVERR with zero data, so pre-reset entropy cannot be read back. A DECERR here would mean the aperture had fallen into the reset domain, which is the opposite of what this proves, so the response is pinned rather than tested as any non-OKAY.
|CHK-TRNG-FRESH
|ESRC produces a fresh seed, `FIPS_LOCK` sets again, and a pool read succeeds, all before the consumer resets are restored to their saved value.
|CHK-TRNG-RECOVERY
|Closing summary line over the twelve rows above: the pool went full, emptied and refilled, the stale read was rejected, fresh data was delivered, and the consumer resets were restored to their saved value. It carries no contract of its own.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Why the pool is checked from outside the domain*: the entropy pool's AXI
responder is not in the TRNG reset domain, which is what makes the scrub
observable at all — the port stays live while its contents are invalidated.

*The RTL clear and isolate assertions are VCS-only*: the six
`ClearDropsRequest_A` / `ClearScrubs*` / `ClearResetsStall_A` /
`NoEntropyAcceptedDuringClear_A` / `ClearScrubsPendingRead_A` assertions in
`sep_entropy_fifo.sv`, the two `AxisEdnNo*DuringClear_A` in
`drbg_axis_edn_adapter.sv` (re-armed by name in `tb/tb_top.sv` for both the
crypto and the pool adapter, after the adapter-scope `$assertoff`), and `ResetOnlyWhenIsolated_A` /
`IsolateHeldThroughReset_A` in `sep_isolate_rst_seq.sv` all expand to nothing
under Verilator, because `prim_assert.sv` and `ocah_assert.svh` both gate on
`ifndef VERILATOR`. Verilator is the acceptance backend, so those ten assertions
give no evidence there. CHK-TRNG-ISOLATE-ALL and CHK-TRNG-PACKER grade the
drain-order and packer-scrub contracts from Python probes on Verilator.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test]]
===== 1.11 `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the ML-DSA-87 key-generation path through the ABR register aperture: reset status, an ACVP keyGen known-answer public key, completion status, the PIC error and notification legs, `ZEROIZE`, and single-bit seed sensitivity. Randomization is RANDCFG — the masking entropy words, and the seed word and bit flipped in the sensitivity leg, come from the run seed (`SepAbrKeygenCfg`).

*Test Coverage*: ML-DSA-87 keyGen known-answer, PIC legs, `ZEROIZE`, and seed sensitivity.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Read the ML-DSA `NAME` and `VERSION` words and log them.
. Read `MLDSA_STATUS` and require READY, not VALID and not ERROR (CHK-RESET-STATUS).
. Enable the notifications and require PIC `[34]` and `[35]` low. Raise the error interrupt with `error_intr_trig` and require `[34]` high with `error_internal_sts` set. Clear the field by `W1C` and require it 0 and `[34]` low (CHK-PIC-ERROR).
. Wait for READY. Write the ACVP seed and the seeded entropy words over the bus and issue KEYGEN. Wait for VALID with ERROR=0 (CHK-STATUS-VALID). Read the 648-word public key and compare it to the vector (CHK-NIST-PK).
. Require `[34]` low and `[35]` high (CHK-PIC-NOTIF). Require `notif_cmd_done_sts` set, clear it by `W1C`, and require the field 0 and `[35]` low (CHK-PIC-NOTIF-W1C).
. Require VALID=1, write `ZEROIZE`, wait for VALID to drop, and require every public-key word to read zero (CHK-ZEROIZE).
. Flip the seeded seed bit and run KEYGEN again. Require a public key that differs from the vector and is not all zero (CHK-SENSITIVITY).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET-STATUS
|Reset STATUS is READY, not VALID, not ERROR.
|CHK-NIST-PK
|648-word public key matches the vendored ACVP keyGen vector.
|CHK-STATUS-VALID
|Completion STATUS is VALID with ERROR=0.
|CHK-PIC-ERROR
|Aggregate `[34]` traverses `0 -> 1` via `error_intr_trig` with `error_internal_sts` set, then the `W1C` reads that field back 0 and drops `[34]`, before the KAT. The field masks come from `abr_reg.rdl`. A stuck-low probe fails this row; the later `[34]=0` at completion is then a live observation.
|CHK-PIC-NOTIF
|Aggregate `[35]` high and `[34]` low at completion.
|CHK-PIC-NOTIF-W1C
|`notif_cmd_done_sts` reads set after keyGen; its `W1C` reads that field back 0 and clears `[35]`. The field mask comes from `abr_reg.rdl`.
|CHK-ZEROIZE
|STATUS reads VALID=1 just before the `ZEROIZE` write, `ZEROIZE` then drops VALID, and every word of the pubkey window then reads zero. `MLDSA_STATUS.READY` is low through the `ABR_ZEROIZE` walk (`abr_ready = (abr_prog_cntr == ABR_RESET)`), so a READY-high wait would hang rather than pass vacuously. Scope: the pubkey read port is gated on `mldsa_valid_reg`, so a zero window proves the key is not readable, *not* that the RAM was wiped. The memory walk is not claimed; proving it needs a probe on the pubkey RAM or on `zeroize_mem_done`.
|CHK-SENSITIVITY
|Flipping one seed bit yields a different public key, and that key is not an all-zero window — an inequality against the vector alone would be satisfied by a cleared window.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The aperture is a `sep_crypto` demux rule at `AbrRegMapBaseAddr` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_pkg.sv`), reachable from the CPU-LSU master the way the OTBN tests reach their block. It is *not* behind an `axi_lite_isolate` unit: unlike the ESRC, CSRNG and EDN legs, the external TRNG passthrough in `hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_axi_interconnect.sv` carries none. Accesses are 32-bit beats (`AxSIZE=2`), one register each. Other access sizes and strobes are graded by <<sep_abr_access_size_test>>. The Key-Manager-side key CSR is the generated PeakRDL `abr_wrapper_key_reg`. The ABR register block behind this aperture is real RTL. This leaf writes the seed window over the bus. The key-vault seed path is not claimed here; <<sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test>> applies the same ACVP vector over it. The ML-DSA `NAME` and `VERSION` words are information only: `abr_reg.rdl` declares them `sw = r` with no reset and no SEP document gives their values, so this leaf does not grade them. The bus-sweep leaves use them as read targets against a reference captured from the DUT (`CHK-ABR-ID-REF`).

*Anti-vacuity*: the public key is a cryptographic function of the seed words
written over the bus. The sensitivity leg flips one seed bit after the NIST
match, so a cached or constant engine passes the first leg and fails the second.
`[34]` is driven high through `error_intr_trig` and cleared before the KAT, so a
stuck-low aggregator cannot satisfy both `CHK-PIC-ERROR` and `CHK-PIC-NOTIF`.

*Not claimed*: the key-vault seed path — a green keyGen does not close it, and
this leaf writes the seed window over the bus. It is owned by
<<sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test>>. ML-KEM-1024 is equally not closed
by a green keyGen, and is owned by <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>; sign and verify by
<<sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test>>.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test]]
===== 1.12 `sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mldsa_sign_verify_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the two ML-DSA-87 consume commands the keyGen leaf does not
reach. `MLDSA_CTRL.CTRL` encodes SIGNING (0x2) and VERIFYING (0x3) beside KEYGEN
(0x1), and this leaf owns both, together with the signature and verify-result
windows they drive. Randomization is RANDCFG — the masking entropy words come from the run
seed (`SepAbrKeygenCfg`); the vectors are fixed.

*Test Coverage*: ML-DSA-87 sigGen known-answer, sigVer accept and reject, and
`ZEROIZE` between commands.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Read the ML-DSA `NAME` words and log them.
. Before the first command, enable the notifications and require PIC `[34]` low. Raise the error interrupt with `error_intr_trig` and require `[34]` high with `error_internal_sts` set. Clear `error_internal_sts` by W1C and require `[34]` low (CHK-PIC-ERROR).
. Wait for READY. Write the private key, mu to `MLDSA_EXTERNAL_MU`, and the seeded entropy words over the bus. Issue SIGNING with `MLDSA_CTRL.EXTERNAL_MU` set, and wait for VALID with ERROR=0.
. Read the 1157-word `MLDSA_SIGNATURE` window and compare it word-for-word to the vector. Require `[34]` low (CHK-SIGN).
. Write `ZEROIZE`, wait for VALID to drop and READY to rise, and require every signature word to read zero (CHK-ZEROIZE).
. For the accepting case, then the rejecting case: wait for READY, write the public key, mu and the signature, and issue VERIFYING with `MLDSA_CTRL.EXTERNAL_MU` set. Wait for VALID with ERROR=0 and compare `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` to the signature's c~ (CHK-VERIFY-ACCEPT, CHK-VERIFY-REJECT). Write `ZEROIZE` and wait for VALID to drop and READY to rise.
. Require the two `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` values to differ (CHK-VERIFY-REJECT).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PIC-ERROR
|PIC `[34]` reads 0 before the trigger, 1 after `error_intr_trig` with the `error_internal_sts` field set, and 0 again after the W1C with that field clear. The field mask comes from `abr_reg.rdl`. An X or Z on the bit is a failure, not a low. This is the positive control for the PIC `[34]`-low clause in CHK-SIGN.
|CHK-SIGN
|The 1157-word `MLDSA_SIGNATURE` window matches the vendored ACVP sigGen vector
word-for-word, with STATUS VALID and ERROR=0, and PIC `[34]` low.
|CHK-VERIFY-ACCEPT
|The ACVP case that must verify completes VALID and `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` (the RDL array) equals the first part of the submitted signature, i.e. c~. That equality is the RDL pass condition.
|CHK-VERIFY-REJECT
|The ACVP case that must not verify completes VALID and `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` is not equal to that signature's c~. An engine that copies the submitted c~ into the result register -- unconditional success -- fails this checker.
|CHK-ZEROIZE
|The `ZEROIZE` after SIGN drops VALID, and every word of the signature window
that CHK-SIGN just read live then reads zero, so the open read and the closed
read are on the same signature. Same scope limit as the keyGen leaf: the read
port is gated on the valid register, so a zero window proves the signature is
not readable, *not* that the RAM was wiped. The `ZEROIZE` after each
verify command must drop VALID and raise READY; its window is not read.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The aperture is the same `sep_crypto` demux rule at `AbrRegMapBaseAddr` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_pkg.sv`) the keyGen leaf uses, with the same 32-bit beats and no `axi_lite_isolate` in front of it. Both vectors are ACVP external-mu groups, so mu is written to `MLDSA_EXTERNAL_MU` rather than a raw message being hashed on the way in. Signing is the deterministic variant, so the signature is a fixed function of the private key and mu and is compared word-for-word. A randomised signature could only be checked by verifying it, which is the weaker claim. `MLDSA_STATUS.VALID` is sticky and READY does not re-assert until a `ZEROIZE`, so every command is separated by one. The private key and public key are written over the bus. The key-vault seed path is not claimed. The ML-DSA `NAME` words are information only, not graded: `abr_reg.rdl` gives them no reset and no SEP document gives their values. A dead aperture fails `CHK-SIGN`, whose window is compared against the published vector.

*Anti-vacuity*: the rejecting sigVer case is the load-bearing half. An engine
that copies each submitted signature's c~ into `MLDSA_VERIFY_RES` (success
unconditionally, in the RDL's terms) passes the accepting compare and fails
`CHK-VERIFY-REJECT`. On the signing side, deterministic external-mu signing
makes the signature a fixed function of inputs written over the bus, so a
cached, constant or non-running engine cannot match 1157 words.

*Not claimed*: `KEYGEN_SIGN` (0x4), the key-vault seed path, and
`CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` consume after a live ABR capture. ML-KEM is a
separate vehicle, <<sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test>>.

'''''

[[sep_abr_pipelined_read_test]]
===== 1.13 `sep_abr_pipelined_read_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_pipelined_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: ABR is the only crypto block reached over AHB rather than AXI, through `axi_lite_to_ahb` in `sep_crypto_abr_wrapper.sv`. A bridge that streams pipelined reads can present the later transfers of a stream with a truncated address, and every such read still answers OKAY. A master on the SoC-facing inbound port may legitimately hold several single-beat reads outstanding with distinct `ARID`s -- AXI places no restriction on that (IHI 0022 A5.3), and no SEP document places one on this aperture -- and each must be answered with its own register's value. The KAT leaves read one word at a time and wait, so they carry no streaming evidence.

*Test Coverage*: The ABR register aperture, read from the SMN inbound master at 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 reads in flight.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Open inbound filter entry 0 for the ABR page. The filter denies by default; the rule is programmed from the CPU-LSU side, as every inbound test reaches these CSRs.
. Read `MLDSA_VERSION1` once on its own through the sequencer, establishing the value the aperture holds and that it is readable at all.
. For each depth in 1, 2, 3, 4, 8: issue that many single-beat reads of THAT SAME address with distinct `ARID`s, presenting every `AR` before consuming any `R`, then collect each response. A bus watch records every `AR` and `R` handshake on the `m_axi` pins during the step.
. Require every response at every depth to equal the control value and to carry an OKAY response.
. Require, at every depth, that the `m_axi` pins accept all of that depth's `AR`s before the first `R` handshake, and that the recorded read count in flight reaches the depth.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-CONTROL
|The control read of `MLDSA_VERSION1` answers OKAY with a known (no X/Z, enforced by the bus monitor) and nonzero value. `abr_reg.rdl` gives the register no reset and no SEP document gives its value, so the control read alone is the comparand for the whole sweep; a control that answered zero, or errored, would let every pipelined read compare equal to it and this leaf would be green over a dead aperture. The address comes from the RDL, never a literal.
|CHK-ABR-PIPELINED-READ
|Every read, at every depth, returns the value the register returns when read alone, with an OKAY response. `MLDSA_VERSION1` is read-only with unconditional combinational readback, so it holds one value and any other response is wrong. An error response is recorded separately from silently wrong data. A timeout counts as a failure, not a skip.
|CHK-ABR-PIPELINE-OVERLAP
|At every depth N, the `AR` and `R` handshakes recorded on the `m_axi` pins show exactly N `AR`s accepted, all N before the first `R`, and N reads outstanding at once. A path that serialised the reads would still return the right values and pass CHK-ABR-PIPELINED-READ; this row fails it, so the depth sweep is a measured overlap and not only the order in which the test presented the reads.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Testlist*: `crypto.toml`; `all` and `cpu_stub` in `all.toml`.

*Why the inbound master*: `m_axi` is the SoC-facing port
(`smn_inbound_axi_req_i` of `hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep.sv`, which reaches the fabric on
`.ext_axi_req_i`) and a real master. `s_axi` presents what the VeeR LSU
presents, and the LSU serialises MMIO loads, so a pipelined read on that port is
stimulus the real design cannot carry.

*Why the comparison cannot be explained away*: every read targets one address with one expected value, so reordering, id-to-event mis-association or per-id state reuse in the VIP could only redistribute correct values among the responses -- none of them can turn the register's value into zero. Single-beat only (`ARLEN = 0`): the crypto demux routes any `AxLEN != 0` to its error slave (`aw_is_burst` / `ar_is_burst` in `hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_axi_interconnect.sv`), which `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` states to software, so no burst is involved or possible.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test]]
===== 1.14 `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mlkem_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the ML-KEM-1024 commands. ML-KEM is a second register block
inside the Adams Bridge aperture, with its own `MLKEM_CTRL` and `MLKEM_STATUS`
beside the ML-DSA pair, and this leaf owns it: the encapsulation key,
decapsulation key, ciphertext and shared-key windows are driven and read here.
Not randomized -- every input is a published vector.

*Test Coverage*: ML-KEM-1024 keyGen, encapsulation, and decapsulation known
answers, decapsulation walked as both the valid and the implicit-rejection case,
`ZEROIZE` between commands, and the Adams Bridge per-IP `SW_RESET_N` domain
graded on a live encaps result.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Read the ML-KEM `NAME` and `VERSION` words and log them.
. For every command below, wait for `MLKEM_STATUS.READY`, write `MLKEM_CTRL`, then wait for VALID with ERROR=0.
. Write the ACVP seeds d and z over the bus and issue keyGen. Read the 392-word encapsulation key and the 792-word decapsulation key and compare both to the vector (CHK-KEM-KEYGEN). Write `ZEROIZE`.
. Write the encapsulation key and the message randomness m over the bus and issue encapsulation. Read the ciphertext and the shared key and compare both to the vector (CHK-KEM-ENCAPS).
. Write `ZEROIZE`. Require VALID to drop and the shared-key window to read zero (CHK-KEM-ZEROIZE).
. For the ACVP case that decapsulates normally, then the case whose ciphertext was modified: write the decapsulation key and the ciphertext, issue decapsulation, read the shared key and compare it to the vector (CHK-KEM-DECAPS, CHK-KEM-DECAPS-REJECT). Write `ZEROIZE` after each case.
. Require the two decapsulation shared keys to differ (CHK-KEM-DECAPS-REJECT).
. Run a final encapsulation. Require a vector-matched shared key and `MLKEM_STATUS.VALID` set.
. Pulse the HMAC bit of `SW_RESET_N` and wait for the HMAC gated reset to assert. After release, require VALID still set and the shared key still bit-exact (CHK-ABR-NEIGHBOR-SURVIVES).
. Assert `SW_RESET_N.abr_sw_rst_n` and require a read of `MLKEM_STATUS` to answer SLVERR (CHK-ABR-ISOLATE).
. Release the ABR reset. Wait for READY and require VALID clear (CHK-ABR-SELF-RESET).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KEM-KEYGEN
|For the ACVP seeds, the 392-word encapsulation key and the 792-word
decapsulation key both match the published vector word-for-word.
|CHK-KEM-ENCAPS
|For that encapsulation key and the published message randomness, the 392-word
ciphertext and the shared key both match the vector. Because the randomness is
supplied over the bus this is an absolute value, not a self-consistency check.
|CHK-KEM-DECAPS
|The valid ACVP decapsulation case recovers the published shared key.
|CHK-KEM-DECAPS-REJECT
|The modified-ciphertext case returns the published *implicit-rejection* key --
a different value, still exact. An engine that reported an error, returned
zeros, skipped implicit rejection, or reused the previous shared key fails here
while passing the accepting case.
|CHK-ABR-NEIGHBOR-SURVIVES
|A live encaps result -- `STATUS.VALID` set and the shared key bit-exact against
the ACVP vector -- is intact after a sibling HMAC `SW_RESET_N` pulse. The
pre-pulse state is asserted first, so a run in which no result was live fails
rather than passing on an empty compare.
|CHK-ABR-ISOLATE
|The `MLKEM_STATUS` address that just returned OKAY with a live result returns
SLVERR while the ABR `SW_RESET_N` bit is held. This is the full-AXI host isolate;
every other accelerator host path is AXI-Lite.
|CHK-ABR-SELF-RESET
|The live result does not survive ABR's own reset: after release the engine
returns READY with `VALID` clear. `VALID` is hardware-driven, so a reset that
never reached the datapath leaves it set. The shared-key window is read-gated on
`VALID`, so its post-reset zeros are *not* offered as evidence of a wipe.
|CHK-KEM-ZEROIZE
|`ZEROIZE` between commands drops `VALID` and the shared-key window then reads
zero. Same scope limit as the ML-DSA leaves: the read port is gated on the valid
register, so a zero window proves the key is not readable, *not* that the
RAM was wiped.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The aperture is the same `sep_crypto` demux rule at `AbrRegMapBaseAddr` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_pkg.sv`) the ML-DSA leaves use. The ML-KEM windows sit higher in the same 64 KB region; offsets resolve from the vendor `abr_reg.rdl` by symbol. `MLKEM_CTRL` is writable only while `MLKEM_STATUS.READY` is set. A write to a busy engine is dropped with no error, and the previous `VALID` would otherwise be read as this command's completion. keyGen takes the two seeds d and z over the bus and produces both keys. Encapsulation takes the message randomness m over the bus as well as the encapsulation key, which is what makes the ciphertext a fixed function of published inputs rather than a value only a round trip could check. Under FIPS-203 implicit rejection the modified ciphertext produces a different shared key rather than an error, and ACVP publishes that key. `MLKEM_STATUS.VALID` is sticky, so every command is separated by a `ZEROIZE`. The ML-KEM `NAME` and `VERSION` words are information only, not graded: `abr_reg.rdl` gives them no reset and no SEP document gives their values. A dead decode fails every window compare against the published vectors.

*Anti-vacuity*: the ML-KEM read ports return zero before a command completes, so
a compare against zero would pass on an engine that never ran -- every expected
value here is non-zero, and the vector loader refuses an all-zero expectation at
import. The rejecting decapsulation case is the load-bearing one: implicit
rejection has no error to observe, so the only thing that can fail is the value.

*Not claimed*: `KEYGEN+DECAPS` (0x4), the key-vault seed and message read lanes,
the shared-key write lane to the key vault, and ML-DSA.

'''''

[[sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test]]
===== 1.15 `sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, both TB masters

*Randomization*: none. The depths and orders are the cells, and the expected
words are the identity reference captured by `CHK-ABR-ID-REF`.

*Objective*: Proves that reads held outstanding together on the Adams Bridge
aperture each return the value of the address they name. The ABR host path ends
in a bridge that performs one AHB transfer at a time behind a fabric that holds
several accesses outstanding. A bridge that streams reads and drops
`HADDR[2:0]` on the streamed transfers answers every read OKAY and returns the
word at the wrong address from the third read of a pipeline on, so a lone read
cannot show it.

*Test Coverage*: pipelines of depth 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 on `m_axi` and on `s_axi`;
four address orders over the ML-DSA identity words; one order on a single ARID;
both masters pipelining at once.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Read each identity word alone on `s_axi` and keep the values as the reference for every read below (CHK-ABR-ID-REF).
. On `s_axi`, program one read window over the whole ABR aperture (`0x1094_0000`..`0x1094_FFFF`) before `m_axi` reads.
. Read `MLDSA_VERSION1` alone on each master as the control (CHK-ABR-PIPE-CTRL).
. In each leg below, issue all of the leg's reads in the same cycle by driving the VIP master sequence directly. Record every AR and R handshake on the TB pins (CHK-ABR-PIPE-RID, CHK-ABR-PIPE-OVERLAP).
. On each master, issue pipelines of depth 1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 of `MLDSA_VERSION1` (CHK-ABR-PIPE-DEPTH-M-AXI, CHK-ABR-PIPE-DEPTH-S-AXI).
. On each master, issue the mixed address orders over the identity words (CHK-ABR-PIPE-MIXED).
. On each master, issue four reads on one ARID (CHK-ABR-PIPE-SAME-ID).
. Issue eight interleaved reads on `m_axi` and eight on `s_axi` at the same time (CHK-ABR-PIPE-DUAL).
. Run every leg before the verdict, so that one run lists every failing leg with the RID, address and data of each read.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-ID-REF
|Before any other ABR read, each of the eight identity words (`MLDSA_NAME`, `MLDSA_VERSION`, `MLKEM_NAME`, `MLKEM_VERSION`) is read twice alone on `s_axi` at AxSIZE=2. Each read answers OKAY, the two reads agree, the value is nonzero, and each word differs from the other word of its 8-byte granule, so a lane or half swap is detectable. The bus monitor fails an OKAY beat with X/Z in the lanes read. `abr_reg.rdl` declares these words `sw = r` with no reset and no SEP document gives their values, so this capture, not a DV constant, is the reference for every later identity read. The values themselves are not claimed.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-CTRL
|A lone read of `MLDSA_VERSION1` returns its captured reference, OKAY, on each master. The pipelined legs assume a working single read, so a failure here is a path fault rather than a pipelining one.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-DEPTH-M-AXI
|On `m_axi`, every read of each depth-1, 2, 3, 4 and 8 pipeline of `MLDSA_VERSION1` returns its captured reference, OKAY, both through the VIP result and as the lane of the R beat carrying its RID.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-DEPTH-S-AXI
|The same on `s_axi`.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-MIXED
|On each master, pipelines over `NAME0`, `NAME1`, `VERSION0` and `VERSION1` in ascending, descending and even-words-first order, and an eight-read interleaving with repeats, each return the word they name. `CHK-ABR-ID-REF` requires the two words of each granule to differ, so a response for the wrong half of a granule is a mismatch.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-SAME-ID
|Four reads on one ARID return their own words in request order, so a path that reorders same-ID reads hands a request its neighbour's word.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-DUAL
|`m_axi` and `s_axi` each pipelining eight reads at the same time get their own words, with both masters outstanding in the same cycles.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-RID
|On every leg the multiset of RIDs on the R beats equals the ARIDs issued and accepted. The VIP's own ID capture samples the first response after issue, which under concurrency may belong to another request, so the grade comes from the pin record.
|CHK-ABR-PIPE-OVERLAP
|On every distinct-ID leg all reads were accepted before the first response, and on the single-ARID legs at least three. Without the overlap the data compare proves nothing about pipelining.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The inbound filter denies by default, so `s_axi` must open a read window before `m_axi` can reach the aperture. The SEP AXI sequencer awaits each item to completion, so nothing overlaps through it; the pipelined legs therefore drive the VIP master sequence directly.

*Anti-vacuity*: the overlap is measured, not assumed, and graded alongside the
data. `CHK-ABR-ID-REF` requires the reference words to be nonzero and to differ
within each granule, so a zero or the granule neighbour's word is a mismatch
rather than a coincidence.

*Not claimed*: writes, which <<sep_abr_access_size_test>> and
<<sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test>> own, and the AHB transfer sequence itself.

'''''

[[sep_abr_access_size_test]]
===== 1.16 `sep_abr_access_size_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_access_size_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- the data values and two dead word offsets past the
register map come from the run seed (`SepAbrAccessSizeCfg`). Every size, offset
and strobe pattern, and every directed dead word, is walked on every seed.

*Objective*: Proves the ABR access-size rules in
`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`. Every ABR register is 32 bits wide: an
8-byte-aligned 64-bit access is two 32-bit accesses, and one
at `addr[2]=1` reaches only the upper register; a write whose strobe covers
only one of the two words writes that word alone; a word whose strobe is partial
is not written and the access answers SLVERR, while the other word of a 64-bit
write is still written when its strobe is full; a narrow read reads the whole
register and returns only the bytes it names, with the other byte lanes zero.
`abr_top` ties the register block's byte enables high and the AHB slave
zero-extends a narrow write, so a partial write that got through would clear the
rest of the register.

*Test Coverage*: 32-bit, 64-bit, AxSIZE=3 at `addr[2]=1`, byte and halfword
reads; 32-bit, 64-bit full-strobe, `WSTRB=0x0F`, `WSTRB=0xF0` and partial-word
writes, including 64-bit beats that cover one word whole and the other in part;
32-bit reads, word writes and partial-word writes at unpopulated offsets past
the register map.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. `s_axi`, one access at a time through the SEP AXI sequencer, so the SEP
scoreboard also fails any response the test did not mark as refused.
. Reads are graded on the eight ML-DSA and ML-KEM identity words, against the
reference `CHK-ABR-ID-REF` captures from 32-bit reads alone at the start.
Neither `abr_reg.rdl` nor any SEP document gives their values.
. Writes use the `global_intr_en_r` / `error_intr_en_r` pair (`+0x8100` /
`+0x8104`), the one 8-byte granule with a read-write register in each half, and
the 32-bit `error_internal_intr_count_r` counter (`+0x8200`), whose upper
neighbour owns no register and reads zero.
. Every refused write targets a register primed with a set bit in every byte it
holds and writes the complement, so a write that landed on any byte moves the
readback.
. The AR, R, AW and B handshakes on the `s_axi` pins are recorded, so narrow reads and
AxSIZE=3 reads at `addr[2]=1` are graded on the whole 64-bit R beat as well as
through the VIP result.
. Past the register map, the five read-write registers are primed with values
that have a set bit in every byte, then each dead word is read, word-written and
partial-written. The map end is 4 bytes past the highest register
`abr_reg.rdl` places, cross-checked against the ABR extent the SEP memory map
states. Each expected response is the one the SEP memory map states for that
word (`env/sep_decode_resp.py`). The dead words are the first two past the end,
the last word of the window, the `+0x4000` mirror of every graded register that
lands past the end, and two seeded words.
. Every register written is restored to its entry value.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-ID-REF
|As <<sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test>>.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD32
|Each identity word reads its captured reference at AxSIZE=2.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD64
|A 64-bit read at each identity pair returns `{high word, low word}`.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD64-UNALIGNED
|An AxSIZE=3 read starting at the upper word of a granule returns that word, and the lower four byte lanes of its R beat read zero.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD-NARROW
|Every byte and aligned halfword of the ML-DSA NAME and VERSION granules reads back the addressed bytes of the captured reference, and every other byte lane of its R beat reads zero.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR32
|32-bit writes land on each write target, walked to all-ones and back to zero on the enables and through seeded values on the counter.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR64-FULL
|A full-strobe 64-bit write lands both words of the pair, and all 32 bits of the low word on the counter while the unpopulated upper word still reads zero.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR64-LO
|AxSIZE=3 with `WSTRB=0x0F` writes the low word alone, OKAY, the upper register unchanged.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR64-HI
|AxSIZE=3 at `addr[2]=1` with `WSTRB=0xF0` writes the upper word alone, OKAY.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR32-HI
|AxSIZE=2 at `addr[2]=1` (`WSTRB=0xF0`) sets and clears the upper register, OKAY, the lower unchanged.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR-PARTIAL
|Each of the eight partial-word strobes (single bytes, both halfwords, both three-byte runs) on the low word, the upper word and the counter answers SLVERR and leaves the register unchanged.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-WR64-MIXED
|A 64-bit beat with `WSTRB=0x7F` writes the low word, leaves the upper one, and answers SLVERR; `WSTRB=0xFC` does the reverse.
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RD-NARROW-RW
