|Byte and halfword reads of the counter return the addressed bytes of its seeded value, and every other byte lane of the R beat reads zero.
|CHK-ABR-UNPOP-RD
|A 32-bit read at every dead word past the register map answers the response code and read data the SEP memory map's Past Extent column states, while every read-write register holds a nonzero value, so an aliased read returns data. Where the map states OKAY, every byte lane of the R beat also reads zero. `adams_bridge.adoc` (aperture-decode paragraph): the crypto demux refuses every access past the register map.
|CHK-ABR-UNPOP-WR
|An all-ones word write at every dead word answers the map's write code, the word reads back as the map states, and all five read-write registers and eight identity words read their primed or captured values afterwards.
|CHK-ABR-UNPOP-PARTIAL
|A byte, a halfword and a three-byte write at the first dead word past the map and at the last word of the window are each refused with the map's write code and leave every graded register unchanged. Where the map answers a word write OKAY, the write reaches ABR and the expected refusal is SLVERR (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`, "ABR access size").
|CHK-ABR-SIZE-RESTORE
|Every register written reads back its entry value.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: each write target is driven through values it does not hold
before its compare, and the counter carries a full 32-bit seeded value, so a
dropped write, a write on the wrong lane or a zero-extended partial write each
change a readback.

*Not claimed*: concurrency, which <<sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test>> and
<<sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test>> own, and `m_axi`. The order of the two 32-bit
accesses of a 64-bit access (`adams_bridge.adoc`: low address first): the test
records only the host `s_axi` pins, and every outcome it grades is the same in
either order. Unpopulated offsets between
populated objects below `0xC018` are not walked; the no-alias compare covers
the graded registers only, not every populated ABR register.

'''''

[[sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test]]
===== 1.17 `sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_concurrent_rw_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, both TB masters

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- the op mix, addresses, data, per-master outstanding
depth (3 to 6), AWIDs and the RREADY / BREADY throttle patterns come from the
run seed (`SepAbrConcurrentRwCfg`); `reseed = 3`.

*Objective*: Proves the statement in `adams_bridge.adoc` that any number of
reads and writes may be outstanding on the aperture from any mix of masters,
and that each returns the value of the address it names.

*Test Coverage*: 32-bit and 64-bit reads of identity and read-write registers,
and 32-bit writes and refused partial-word writes, from `s_axi` and `m_axi` at
the same time with both response channels throttled; 64-bit writes from `s_axi`
alone, which owns the one read-write register pair in an 8-byte granule.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. `s_axi` opens a read-write inbound-filter window over the ABR aperture for
`m_axi`, then sets each read-write register to a seeded start value.
. Each read-write register is written by one master only, and that master
issues all its writes on one AWID, so its writes to the register land in issue
order. `s_axi` owns the interrupt-enable pair and `error_internal_intr_count_r`;
`m_axi` owns `notif_intr_en_r` and `notif_cmd_done_intr_count_r`.
. `CHK-ABR-ID-REF` first captures every identity word from the DUT, and the
seeded streams are built after it. Identity reads name only words whose
captured value no other identity word read holds, so a response swapped
between two identity reads is a mismatch.
. Both masters run their seeded streams at once, each with several accesses
outstanding, while seeded pause patterns hold RREADY and BREADY low.
. After both drain, every register is read from both masters, then restored.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ABR-ID-REF
|As <<sep_abr_pipelined_read_matrix_test>>.
|CHK-ABR-CRW-WRITE-RESP
|Every full-word write answers OKAY and every partial-word write SLVERR.
|CHK-ABR-CRW-READ
|Every read answers OKAY. An identity word returns its captured reference exactly. A read-write word returns a value inside its owner's write window: the last write whose response came back before the read was issued, or any later write issued before the read returned. At least one read-write word had a single legal value and at least one cross-master read observed a value the other master wrote.
|CHK-ABR-CRW-STRESS
|The pins show one AR per read and one AW and B per write, at least three accesses outstanding at once on each master, cycles with both masters outstanding, and RREADY and BREADY each held low while a response was valid.
|CHK-ABR-CRW-FINAL
|After both streams drain, each read-write register reads its owner's last written value from both masters.
|CHK-ABR-CRW-RESTORE
|Every register reads back its entry value.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the window is computed from each access's issue and completion
times rather than widened to every value ever written, and the strict final
check does not depend on timing at all. The stress floors are measured on the
pins, so a run whose streams serialized fails rather than passing on sequential
traffic.

*Not claimed*: the order in which the bridge chooses between a waiting read and
a waiting write, and two masters writing the same register.

'''''

[[sep_abr_absent_decerr_test]]
===== 1.18 `sep_abr_absent_decerr_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_abr_absent.parhex`, target `lsu_stub_abr_absent`. Verilator only (`tools = ["verilator"]` in `testlists/crypto.toml`): VCS coverage runs elaborate the single `default` target, which keeps Adams Bridge.

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_absent_decerr_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE.

*Objective*: The Adams Bridge build option. `doc/integrator/src/defines.adoc` (Adams Bridge defines) says that without `SEP_ABR_EN`, `sep_crypto` terminates the ABR AXI aperture and the Key Manager's ABR key bus with DECERR subordinates. The `lsu_stub_abr_absent` target in `sep_sim_cfg.toml` is the `lsu_stub_all_live` model with the `+define+SEP_ABR_EN` line dropped from its generated bender filelist; every other target keeps the define.

*Test Coverage*: host read and write of the ABR aperture; KM CPU store and load of the ABR wrapper key aperture.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring-up. Require the ABR reset released and the host ABR path not isolated, so a DECERR is not the isolation answer.
. Read an HMAC register over the same crypto interconnect (control), then read `MLDSA_NAME` and write `MLDSA_CTRL`.
. Release the KM and wait for the KM ABR path to open. The image `km_rom_abr_absent` W1Cs the `IRQ_STATUS` AXI error bits before each access and reports which bits the access set: a store to the HMAC wrapper key aperture (control), then a store and a load at the ABR wrapper key aperture.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,6",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |What it proves
|CHK-ABR-ABSENT-OPEN
|ABR reset released; the host ABR path is not isolated during the host legs and the KM ABR path is not isolated before the KM legs.
|CHK-ABR-ABSENT-CONTROL
|An HMAC `STATUS` read answers OKAY on the crypto interconnect.
|CHK-ABR-ABSENT-HOST-RD
|A read of `MLDSA_NAME` answers DECERR.
|CHK-ABR-ABSENT-HOST-WR
|A write of `MLDSA_CTRL` answers DECERR.
|CHK-KM-ABR-ABSENT-PRECOND
|Every KM leg started with both AXI error bits clear.
|CHK-KM-ABR-ABSENT-CONTROL
|The KM store to the HMAC wrapper key aperture sets neither error bit.
|CHK-KM-ABR-ABSENT-WR
|The KM store to the ABR wrapper key aperture sets AXI_DECERR and not AXI_SLVERR.
|CHK-KM-ABR-ABSENT-RD
|The KM load from the same address sets AXI_DECERR and not AXI_SLVERR.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the same leaf run on the `lsu_stub_all_live` model (ABR present) fails `CHK-ABR-ABSENT-HOST-RD` with OKAY, so the host checkers discriminate the two builds. The HMAC controls on both paths answer clean in the same run.

*Not claimed*: the ABR memory tie-off and the held-low ABR interrupts of the ABR-absent build. The image also loads the first KMCSR address past the generated block; the answer is logged and not graded (see Known Limitations, "KMCSR extent").

'''''

[[sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test]]
===== 1.19 `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — extra accepted pops come from the run seed (`SepEntropyPoolCfg`). The alias set and all three unique-dead offsets are walked every seed, so the decode coverage is seed-independent.

*Objective*: The fabric entropy pool answers only its three live registers. Occupancy, the live `pool_low` flag and fill-stall status reach software and the interrupt aggregator, a pop drains one word, and an empty pop is refused. Writes to each live offset and a representative unmapped offset return SLVERR without changing occupancy. Every mapped high-bit mirror and unique-dead offset walked by the test is refused with zero read data. A 32-bit read of a register's upper word is refused with zero read data and does not pop or disturb the FIFO.

*Test Coverage*: Three live pool registers; pop, empty-refuse, unmapped refuse, writes to STATUS, IRQ_CAUSE, DATA and one unmapped offset, plus the shared write refusal under all three AW/W arrival orders; 32-bit reads of the upper word of each register.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. With the pool empty, require level 0, aggregate `[36]` high (CHK-POOL-LOW-HIGH) and `0x08` = `0x1` (CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-LOW).
. Start the pool beat capture and the shared entropy bring-up, with the pool sink in observe mode. Wait for genbits and start the FIFO drain.
. Wait until the live `pool_low` flag clears. Require `fifo_level` greater than 0 and `[36]` low (CHK-POOL-FILL, CHK-POOL-LOW-LOW, CHK-POOL-LOW-EDGE), and `0x08` = `0x0` (CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-IDLE).
. Disable ESRC. Read every high-bit mirror of the three live registers and all three unique-dead offsets, and require SLVERR with RDATA=0 (CHK-UNMAPPED).
. Disable EDN and pop until the pool is not full with a request outstanding. Write STATUS, IRQ_CAUSE, DATA and one unmapped offset with AW and W in the same cycle; require SLVERR and unchanged occupancy (CHK-WRITE-SLVERR).
. Pause the FIFO drain. Drive the shared write refusal two more ways at STATUS: AW leading and W leading, by arming an `AxiTimingProfile` on the master (CHK-WRITE-ORDER). Restart the drain.
. Wait `STALL_THRESH` cycles. Require `[37]` high (CHK-STALL-ASSERT) and `0x08` = `0x2` (CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-STALL). Hold the stall for three times `STALL_THRESH` more and require both unchanged (CHK-STALL-DURATION).
. Re-enable EDN and ESRC and wait for `[37]` low (CHK-STALL-CLEAR).
. Disable EDN and ESRC and make the pool not full again. Pop once, issue the three 32-bit upper-word reads, and pop again (CHK-HALF-READ).
. Pop the seeded count of extra words. Require OKAY, level minus one, and nonzero distinct words (CHK-POP-DATA).
. Drain to empty and compare `[36]` to `status[6]` on each status read (CHK-POOL-LOW-EDGE). Require `[36]` high (CHK-POOL-LOW-REHIGH). Pop once more and require SLVERR (CHK-EMPTY-SLVERR).
. Stop the drain, require the CSRNG and EDN alerts zero (CHK-ALERTS-ZERO), and call `report()` (CHK5_pool).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-POOL-LOW-HIGH
|Aggregate `[36]` high on empty (level 0).
|CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-LOW
|`0x08=0x1` at empty (`pool_low`), not a copy of status `0x40`.
|CHK-POOL-FILL
|`pool_low` clears after fill and `fifo_level` is greater than 0. `pool_err` reads 0. `interrupts.adoc` source 39 says the pointer-integrity check is not built in the current configuration, so that source is tied to zero; the zero is not a fault-free claim and no pool integrity fault is claimed.
|CHK-POOL-LOW-LOW
|Aggregate `[36]` low after fill.
|CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-IDLE
|`0x08` traverses `0x1 -> 0x0` after fill, not a copy of status.
|CHK-STALL-DURATION
|Holding the same stall three times past `STALL_THRESH` leaves `[37]` asserted and the irq-cause code still reading the fill-stall value. The counter saturates and the flag clears only on forward progress, so a counter that wrapped or a flag that self-cleared on saturation would drop the fault while the outage continued.
|CHK-WRITE-ORDER
|A write whose AW leads W, and one whose W leads AW, each answer a single BRESP=SLVERR and leave `fifo_level` unchanged. AW and W carry no ordering requirement between them (AMBA IHI 0022 A3.3). The backend presents both in the same cycle, so the split-arrival arms of that handshake need the master armed with an `AxiTimingProfile`; the profile is only a request, so each arm is credited from the order the `s_axi` monitor observed (first AWVALID and WVALID cycle), and the background FIFO drain on the same master is paused while the profile is armed. A slave that assumed same-cycle arrival would wedge or answer twice.
|CHK-WRITE-SLVERR
|Writes to STATUS, IRQ_CAUSE, DATA and one unmapped offset return SLVERR and do not change `fifo_level` while the pool is not full (a full FIFO cannot show a refused fill).
|CHK-UNMAPPED
|Every high-bit mirror of a live register, plus all three unique-dead offsets, returns SLVERR with RDATA=0. All three are walked every seed: `0x18` is the unused `[4:3]=11` code and catches a decode class the other two do not, so a seeded pick of one could miss it.
|CHK-STALL-ASSERT
|`[37]` high after `STALL_THRESH` with the pool not full.
|CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-STALL
|`0x08=0x2` at stall (`fill_stall`, `pool_low` still clear after the fill). Must not copy the raw status word.
|CHK-STALL-CLEAR
|`[37]` low after EDN and ESRC re-enable.
|CHK-HALF-READ
|`sep_entropy_pool.rdl` declares every register `accesswidth = 64`. A 32-bit read (AxSIZE=2) at `+0x4`, `+0xC` and `+0x14` answers SLVERR with RDATA=0 (X/Z-checked in the error-RDATA window) and leaves `fifo_level` unchanged. The 64-bit pop before the three reads and the pop after them return two consecutive words of the pool's EDN beat stream, which the test records from the `pool_edn_*` observation ports; the popped word must match exactly one adjacent beat pair, and the next pop must be the pair after it in the same packing order. A half read that popped, or that disturbed the head of the FIFO, fails the level or the word compare.
|CHK-POP-DATA
|Accepted pops OKAY, level minus one, nonzero distinct words.
|CHK-POOL-LOW-REHIGH
|`[36]` high again after drain (high-low-high).
|CHK-POOL-LOW-EDGE
|On the fill read and on each drain-leg status read down to empty, the `[36]` aggregate agrees with `status[6]`. Other status reads (poll, refusal and stall legs) are not compared to `[36]`. Occupancy is not compared against a FIFO watermark. The high-low-high walk of `[36]` is the live-flag evidence.
|CHK-EMPTY-SLVERR
|Pop at `0x10` on empty returns SLVERR, after an accepted pop.
|CHK5_pool
|Native EDN beats reached the pool adapter (observe-mode floor). Bit-exact per-beat routing stays with `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, not this row.
|CHK-ALERTS-ZERO
|After the aperture walk, CSRNG `ERR_CODE` and `RECOV_ALERT_STS` and EDN `ERR_CODE` and `RECOV_ALERT_STS` each read zero. The shared base-test step `check_entropy_alerts_zero` (`cocotb/tests/sep_base_test.py`) reads and grades them.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The block is a 64 KB crossbar target at `entropy_fifo.main` (`0x1095_0000`). It fills from the native EDN endpoint after the shared entropy bring-up. Every seed walks the high-bit mirrors of the three live registers, plus all three unique-dead offsets, so a truncated decode cannot hide behind a seeded pick. Fill waits on the live `pool_low` flag, not on an occupancy watermark. `STALL_THRESH` is the wait bound for the fill-stall flag.

*Anti-vacuity*: fill is observed as `pool_low` clear and `fifo_level` greater than 0 before any refusal is
scored. An accepted pop happens before the empty-pop SLVERR. `[36]` is seen
high, then low, then high. `[37]` is the first fill-stall after the fill path is
starved for `STALL_THRESH` with the pool not full. At least one popped word is
nonzero and two popped words differ.

*RTL fact that arms `[37]`*: ESRC CTRL `MODULE_ENABLE=0` does not drop AUTO-mode
EDN acks while CSRNG still has a seed, and after that disable the pool runs to
full (level 32). `fill_stall` needs `req_pending && !edn_ack` for `STALL_THRESH`,
and a full pool holds `req_pending` low. The stall wait therefore also writes
EDN_CTRL `EDN_ENABLE=False` (legal mubi4, other fields unchanged) and pops
leftover words until the pool has room and acks have stopped.

*Not claimed*: full PIC delivery (claim / mask / ISR). Drain-under-fill and
recovery stress are not exercised here. Stall duration is graded by
`CHK-STALL-DURATION`; what remains unclaimed is a stall that outlasts a
recovery attempt.

'''''

[[sep_otbn_compute_rand_test]]
===== 1.20 `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden (strict CHK1..CHK4 plus `CHK5_otbn_urnd` in observe mode)

*Objective*: Loads a tiny RV32I program into OTBN IMEM, stages two seeded DMEM operands, issues EXECUTE, and checks `ERR_BITS==0` plus the DMEM result against an independent Python golden, while the strict entropy scoreboard runs against OTBN's live URND draw.

*Test Coverage*: OTBN ADD/XOR/AND vs an independent golden, with URND observed.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up `no_cpu`, park AES/HMAC/KMAC, bring up entropy for OTBN URND wipe.
. Walk ADD, XOR, AND against the independent golden.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ERRBITS
|Every compute cell retires with `ERR_BITS==0`.
|CHK-DMEM
|Every compute cell's DMEM result equals the Python golden for that op and those operands. The result word is pre-seeded with the golden's exact complement, so a program that never stores its result fails here rather than passing on a seed whose golden happens to be zero.
|CHK1..CHK4 / CHK5_otbn_urnd
|This entry also runs the strict entropy scoreboard against the chained golden, with `CHK5_otbn_urnd` in observe mode, because OTBN draws real URND entropy while it computes.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|ADD, XOR, and AND ran in one invocation. Operands come from the run seed.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Evidence*: one `CHK-CELL` record per compute cell (`cocotb/tests/otbn/sep_otbn_compute_rand_test.py`) covers CHK-ERRBITS and CHK-DMEM for that cell. A cell that fails either one fails under its own checker name.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test]]
===== 1.21 `sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — all five `HT_WATERMARK_NUM` encodings and both
low-mode falls walk every seed; the illegal selector comes from the run seed.

*Objective*: Health-test watermark arming: each supported selector arms to `0x0000` high or `0xFFFF` low on `MODULE_ENABLE` 0->1, a selector write leaves `HT_WATERMARK` at the entry value, and an illegal selector resolves to `REPCNT_HI`. Scope: the ESRC → DRBG → EDN datapath row owns CHK1–CHK4 and stays where it is.

*Test Coverage*: Health-test watermark arming and the matching status.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Every supported selector arms on MODULE_ENABLE 0→1.
. Changing the selector does not re-arm. An unsupported value becomes `REPCNT_HI`.
. A low watermark falls once `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE` is allowed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ARM-MEN
|Each supported selector arms to `0x0000` (high) or `0xFFFF` (low) on MODULE_ENABLE 0→1.
|CHK-NO-REARM
|A selector write leaves `HT_WATERMARK` at the entry value.
|CHK-UNSUPPORTED
|An illegal selector resolves to `REPCNT_HI` and arms `0x0000`.
|CHK-LOW-FALL
|After arming a low mode, enabling health tests drops `HT_WATERMARK` below
`0xFFFF`. Both `APT_LO` and `MARKOV_LO` take this path on every seed: they drive
separate event counters in `entropy_source.sv`, so choosing one per seed would
leave the other permanently unproven.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The number of selectors whose DUT readback matched its documented arm value
equals the size of `enum WATERMARK_TEST`, read from `entropy_source.rdl` at
import. The bound comes from the RDL rather than from the walk generator, so a
skipped mode fails here. The same invocation covers the seed-selected illegal
selector and both low-mode falls.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the shared TRNG reset path. It clears the selector and the
watermark together; no vehicle asserts that pair.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_fips_lock_test]]
===== 1.22 `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every certified-configuration class every seed; pre-lock value and rejected poke come from the run seed, and each field's poke is selected to differ from that field's pre-lock value. The seed also picks which observe tap is held at 1 and which at 0 across the lock, and the `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.LANE_SEL` values before and under the lock.

*Objective*: The FIPS lock makes ESRC configuration write-once: a pre-lock write moves the field off reset, the same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`, write-0 cannot clear the lock, and `rst_ni` is the only way back. The lock also freezes the debug-pin mux (`DEBUG_CTRL`) and both observe-tap enables (`BIW_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE`, `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE`), so a locked part cannot capture or route pre-conditioning entropy. Each conditioned word goes to the DRBG and into the main FIFO in parallel, so `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE` gates the software copy of the DRBG seed words: the walk clears it before the lock, as a certified configuration does, and the lock must hold it at 0. The advisory RCT/APT cutoffs derived from `MIN_ENTROPY_H` are graded against an SP 800-90B oracle. Scope: health-test *quality* is not claimed.

*Test Coverage*: FIPS lock and the matching entropy-source status.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`. Walk every field `entropy_source.rdl` marks `swwel`, one target per register, except `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`. Write each target's seeded pre-lock value and require the field to move off reset (CHK-PRE-LOCK).
. Write `CTRL.RSVD0` and require it to read 0 with `CTRL` unchanged (CHK-CTRL-RSVD).
. Sweep `MIN_ENTROPY_H` over eight Q4.4 points and read `RECOMMENDED_THRESHOLDS` at each point (CHK-REC-THRESH). Restore the `MIN_ENTROPY_H` pre-lock value.
. Write each observe-tap enable to the opposite of its seeded held value, then to the held value, with the pre-lock `LANE_SEL`. Read back after each write (CHK-OBS-PRE-LOCK).
. Write `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1` and read 1 (CHK-LOCK-SET). Write 0 and read 1 (CHK-LOCK-W1S).
. Poke each target and require the pre-lock value to hold (CHK-POST-LOCK).
. Write each tap enable to the opposite of its held value, with the post-lock `LANE_SEL`. Require the enable to hold (CHK-OBS-ENABLE-LOCKED) and `LANE_SEL` to move (CHK-OBS-LANE-SEL).
. Write `CTRL.RSVD0` again and require `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1` (CHK-CTRL-RSVD).
. Pulse `rst_ni` and require `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=0` (CHK-RST-NI).
. Read the released target at its reset, then poke it and read the poke (CHK-POST-UNLOCK). Read the tap that held 0, set it and read 1, then clear it (CHK-OBS-POST-UNLOCK).
. Log the walked classes (CHK-RANDCFG).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PRE-LOCK
|A pre-lock write moves the field off reset.
|CHK-POST-LOCK
|The same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`.
|CHK-LOCK-SET
|`FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` reads 1 after the arming write.
|CHK-LOCK-W1S
|Write-0 leaves `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`.
|CHK-CTRL-RSVD
|Reserved `CTRL.RSVD0` reads 0, and writing it before the lock leaves `CTRL`
unchanged, where the other `CTRL` fields are demonstrably writable so an
unchanged register isolates `RSVD0`. Repeated after the lock, the same write
leaves `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`.
|CHK-RST-NI
|`rst_ni` clears `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` to 0.
|CHK-POST-UNLOCK
|After `rst_ni` the released target reads its register reset, then takes the
poke that the lock rejected. The target is chosen so its poke differs from the
reset, otherwise the reset value alone would satisfy the readback.
|CHK-OBS-PRE-LOCK
|Before the lock, `BIW_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE` and `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE`
each take both values on readback, so a bit stuck at either value fails here.
|CHK-OBS-ENABLE-LOCKED
|Under the lock each tap enable rejects a write of the opposite value. One tap
is held at 1 and rejects a clear, the other is held at 0 and rejects a set,
picked by the seed, so every seed grades both directions on both taps over
the reseeds.
|CHK-OBS-LANE-SEL
|The same `NOISE_OBS_CTRL` write under the lock moves `LANE_SEL`, which is not
locked. The lane moving while `RAW_ENABLE` holds shows the write reached the
register and the lock is per field.
|CHK-OBS-POST-UNLOCK
|After `rst_ni` the tap that held 0 reads 0 and takes the set the lock rejected.
|CHK-REC-THRESH
|`RECOMMENDED_THRESHOLDS.RCT_LIMIT` tracks `MIN_ENTROPY_H` across a sweep of
eight Q4.4 points, compared against SP 800-90B 4.4.1 `C = 1 + ceil(20/H)`
re-derived in the test. The oracle is the standard, not
`entropy_source_rec_thresh_lut.sv`, which the checker never reads. `APT_LIMIT` is
an exact binomial cutoff with no closed form; what is claimed for it is that it
stays inside the 1024-sample window, never rises between sweep points, and is
lower at the Q4.4 maximum than at `MIN_ENTROPY_H=0x00`.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|Every locked field class of `entropy_source.rdl` is walked in one invocation,
and the PASS line names each class it walked. The two observe-tap enables are
graded by the `CHK-OBS-*` checkers. Every register that locks two
fields moves both off reset in the pre-lock write, so a field inside the compare
mask is never left unwritten.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: This leaf is one of two vehicles for the ESRC lock-and-alert contract; the other is <<sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test>>. `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` is write-1-only. The observe-tap enables and the debug-pin mux are locked; `NOISE_OBS_CTRL.LANE_SEL` is not. The walk sequence declares its coverage and reconciles it against the `swwel` inventory in `entropy_source.rdl` at import, so a lock added or dropped there fails the test instead of leaving the walk quietly short.

*Not claimed*: `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`. It is locked too, but clearing it stops the
block for the rest of the walk, so its lock belongs to the reset-recovery
vehicle. The observe FIFOs' data path under the lock: `BIW_OBS_STATUS`,
`NOISE_OBS_STATUS` and the `*_OBS_RDATA` ports are not read, so a locked-off
tap is graded at the control bit only. The main-FIFO data path with
`FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE` locked off is graded by <<sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test>>.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test]]
===== 1.23 `sep_esrc_e2e_fifo_closed_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force +km_rom_hex=km_rom_entropy.parhex +esrc_fifo_closed`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, selected by `+esrc_fifo_closed`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE -- directed, with the same deterministic noise as `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`.

*Objective*: `hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc` gives `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE` one job: it gates the main-FIFO write. The words offered to the FIFO also seed the DRBG, and that seed stream does not depend on ENABLE. With ENABLE=0 from the ESRC configuration on, the DRBG must still seed and generate bit-exact against the golden, no word may enter the FIFO, and under `FIPS_LOCK` software must not be able to set ENABLE again.

*Test Coverage*: DRBG seeding with the `FIFO_RDATA` read path closed, and the lock on that read path.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Same bring-up as `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, with `SepEntropyCfg.fifo_enable=0`. The ESRC configuration writes `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE=0` before the generators start and before `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK`.
. Wait for a seed, set the lock, enable EDN, release the Key Manager, and wait for genbits and the Key Manager consume, as in the smoke.
. Over that phase, read `FIFO_STATUS` and pop `FIFO_RDATA` twice per sample. Keep sampling until a full seed of words (12) crosses the seed port after the lock and the DRBG packs a new seed.
. Under the lock, write `FIFO_CTRL.ENABLE=1` and read it back. Wait for 12 more seed-port words, then read `FIFO_STATUS`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK1
|Decorrelated output matches the golden model.
|CHK2
|Compressed output matches golden, scored at the FIFO input tap (`entropy_stream_data_o`, the DRBG seed port). The FIFO stays empty, so the front-door drain of the smoke has nothing to read.
|CHK3
|The seed that CSRNG accepts matches golden. Each word in it crossed the seed port with ENABLE=0.
|CHK4
|Generated bits match golden.
|CHK5
|The Key Manager consumes an entropy word and it matches golden.
|CHK5_pool
|As in `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`.
|CHK-ALERTS-ZERO
|As in `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`.
|CHK-FIFO-CLOSED
|`INTR_STATUS.FIFO_UNDERFLOW` and `FIFO_OVERFLOW` read 0 before the first pop. Each `FIFO_STATUS` sample reads LEVEL=0 and WPTR at its reset, so no word went into the FIFO after the TRNG reset. The pops set `FIFO_UNDERFLOW`, which shows they reached the FIFO, and a W1C clears it. The PASS line gives the seed-port words, the seeds the DRBG packed and the seeds CSRNG accepted in the window. A window with fewer than 12 words or no packed seed fails, because an idle window cannot show a closed read path.
|CHK-FIFO-ENABLE-LOCKED
|Under `FIPS_LOCK` a write of ENABLE=1 reads back ENABLE=0. After 12 more seed-port words, LEVEL is 0 and WPTR is at its reset.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*What a broken design does here*: if ENABLE also gated the seed port, no seed would reach CSRNG. CHK3 would have no seed, and the bounded seed wait fails the leaf.

*Not claimed*: the drain step of the certified sequence (clear ENABLE, read `FIFO_RDATA` until LEVEL is 0, then lock) with words already in the FIFO. This leaf clears ENABLE before a word reaches the FIFO, so that drain has nothing to read. The data an empty-FIFO read returns: the RDL calls it undefined, so it is not graded.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test]]
===== 1.24 `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — directed persistent-failure trip.

*Objective*: A short window plus stuck forced noise trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD`,
and the alert bookkeeping the RDL defines is read at that trip. An opening
`INTR_TEST` sweep, before the trip, drives every interrupt source the RDL defines.

*Test Coverage*: Entropy-source alerts reach status and the aggregator.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` and confirm that the forced noise is routed. Require ALERT, PERSISTENT_FAILURE and PIC source 16 at 0 (CHK-NO-ALERT).
. From the clean pre-trip state, pulse each `INTR_TEST` source on its own (CHK-INTR-TEST).
. Enable only PERSISTENT_FAILURE, arm a short window, and hold the forced noise stuck until it trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD` (CHK-ALERT, CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE, CHK-PIC-16, CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO).
. Read the alert counters and the health-test latch at the trip (CHK-ANY-FAIL-WINDOWS, CHK-FAIL-ATTRIB, CHK-HT-STATUS, CHK-GEN-HEALTH).
. Leave AlertHang, then W1C the ALERT and PERSISTENT_FAILURE bits (CHK-W1C).
. Set `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE=0`, then W1C every latched health-test bit (CHK-HT-STATUS).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NO-ALERT
|ALERT, PERSISTENT_FAILURE, and PIC source 16 are 0 before the trip.
|CHK-INTR-TEST
|Each `INTR_TEST` source `ENTROPY_SOURCE.fields("INTR_TEST")` names sets only
its own `INTR_STATUS` bit, raises PIC source 16, and clears on W1C. Swept
before the trip: `INTR_STATUS` bits are re-driven from their live cause, so a
source whose real condition is latched cannot clear. The expected count is
that field list, not a literal.
|CHK-ALERT
|`MAIN_SM_STATUS.ALERT` asserts after the threshold.
|CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE
|`INTR_STATUS.PERSISTENT_FAILURE` asserts. Note it is the OR of the AlertHang
trip, a main-FSM error and a counter-integrity error; CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO is what
makes this one attributable.
|CHK-PIC-16
|`sep_internal_interrupts[15]` (PIC source 16) asserts. Only PERSISTENT_FAILURE
is enabled for the trip, so this adds the aggregate slot wiring on top of
CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE rather than an independent cause.
|CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO
|`MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR` stays 0 across the trip, so PERSISTENT_FAILURE is
attributable to the alert threshold.
|CHK-ANY-FAIL-WINDOWS
|`ALERT_SUMMARY_FAIL_COUNTS.ANY_FAIL_COUNT` sits inside `[ALERT_THRESHOLD,
ALERT_THRESHOLD + 8]` at the trip and stays at or below the summed per-lane fail
pulses. `ALERT_THRESHOLD` is 3: at 1 the alert comparator already implies the
lower bound, and no denomination is distinguishable at a count of one. The upper
bound against the per-lane pulse total is what rejects a counter denominated in
events rather than in failing *windows*.
|CHK-FAIL-ATTRIB
|`REPCNT_TOTAL_FAILS` is non-zero, attributing the trip to the repetition test
under stuck noise with `REPETITION_LIMIT` = 5, and each lane's 32-bit total
covers its 4-bit `ALERT_FAIL_COUNTS` counter. The two take the same fail pulse
but carry different widths and different clears, so the compare is a
DUT-to-DUT contract. The reverse does not hold and is not claimed:
`entropy_source.rdl` scopes `ALERT_FAIL_COUNTS` to the current alert sequence,
which an accepted passing window ends, while each total counts since the last
`CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE` rising edge.
|CHK-HT-STATUS
|`HEALTH_TEST_STATUS.HEALTH_STATUS` latches a failing test while the alert is
asserted. No reserved bit latches: `entropy_source.rdl` allocates
repetition [0], APT [3], Markov high [4] and Markov low [5], leaving [1:2] and
[6:7] reserved. After `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE=0` the latch still holds the trip
bits, and a write of one to every latched bit reads back 0.
|CHK-GEN-HEALTH
|All twelve `GENERATOR_n_HEALTH_STATUS` registers are read at the trip. At least
one latches a failing lane, and none latches a reserved bit.
|CHK-W1C
|After leaving AlertHang, W1C clears both bits and the PIC line.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: This leaf is the second vehicle for the ESRC lock-and-alert contract, with <<sep_esrc_fips_lock_test>>. Health-test *quality* is not claimed.

*Not claimed*: `es_cntr_err` -> `MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR`. This is the health-test
counter-redundancy countermeasure: `prim_count` holds each count twice, up and
down, and reports an error only when `cnt_q[0] + cnt_q[1]` stops equalling the
counter maximum. No stimulus can break that invariant — both copies take the same
enable and the same step — so the path is reachable by a physical fault and by
nothing else. Proving it therefore requires fault injection, which no vehicle
in `all` performs — see <<sep-vplan-scope>>.
CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO proves the bit is 0 on the alert path, which is what makes
PERSISTENT_FAILURE attributable to the threshold trip; it does not claim the
error path works.

'''''

[[sep-vplan-phase3-crypto-entropy]]
==== 1. Crypto and entropy, Phase 3

Phase 3 tests extend the open Phase 1 and Phase 2 tests of the crypto and
entropy area with drawn stimulus, error injection and checks that those tests
do not grade. Each entry names the open test that it extends and states what
that test does not grade. A contract that an open test can carry as a leg of
its own run is an extension leg of that open test and not a separate leaf (see
the entries for `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` and
`sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`). An extension leg adds only the
cells that the open test does not grade. The entries carry the contract, the scenario, the checks and the
logged proof.

===== Common rules

Every entry follows these rules. An entry states only what differs.

* *Seed rule.* Every random value is a pure function of the run seed, from the
seeded SHA-256 counter-mode generator of the open environment.
`--stage sim --seed N` replays the leaf. The generator is not a CSPRNG.
* *Drawn cells.* Regression seeds are random per leaf, and a leaf does not know
its run index. No entry takes a cell from a seed index, and no testlist entry
pins a cell: each leaf has one testlist entry. An entry runs a cell in every
leaf only when the cell is cheap and contract-critical: a legal minimum or
maximum, one representative of each leg, or the error-injection case itself.
Such a cell set is small and adds no boot, no cold reset and no transfer of real
size per cell, and its coverage bins fill in one `all --cov` regression. The
seed draws every other cell of a larger cross (`SepSeededRng`; a weighted draw
where the entry says so). The coverage of a drawn cell closes on a merge of
regressions and never in one regression (<<sep-fcov-rand>>). For N equally
likely cells and d draws per leaf, the expected closure is about
N * ln(N) / (d * reseed) regressions.
* *ESRC interrupt status.* The specification does not state whether an ESRC
`INTR_STATUS` bit latches while its `INTR_ENABLE` bit is clear (see
<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). A TRNG reset returns
`INTR_ENABLE` to 0. So an entry that grades an `INTR_STATUS` bit writes 1 to
the `INTR_ENABLE` bit of that source after every TRNG reset and before the
stimulus. `sep_esrc_csr_rw_walk_rand_test` writes an enable bit 0 under a
status event; it logs that status and does not grade it.
* *Test data only.* All drawn data is test data. Keys, key shares, IV seeds and
ABR seeds come from a fixed table of published test constants. The seed picks
the table row and never the bytes. No document quotes a per-seed value as
evidence.
* *Log shape.* The leaf logs its drawn plan first (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`). Each
check logs `CHK-<NAME> PASS|FAIL seed=<n> <values>`. The leaf ends with
`RESULT <test> seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>`. PASS needs positive evidence in
`results.xml`. A clean simulator exit is not evidence. A blocked check
logs `OBS-BLOCKED <check> reason=<item>` and is not counted in
`checks=<k>`. A control that shows no
cause logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf. A line that starts with `OBS-` is
logged and never graded.
* *Observation points.* Register readback, AXI response code and read data come
from the CPU-LSU master (in `no_cpu` mode the master spliced onto the CPU bus).
Every entry lists its probes and injects under "Probes and injects" and marks
each one `approved`, observation tap or added hook. An approved item is in the
closed set of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` or in `tb_top.sv`. The approved items that the
entries use are: the interrupt vector probe `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`
(interrupt source N on bit N-1); `crypto_edn_req_o[i]`, `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]`
and `crypto_edn_fips_o[i]` (client 0 AES, 1 KMAC, 2 OTBN RND, 3 OTBN URND);
`km_entropy_*`, `axis1_*`, `axis2_*` and `pool_edn_*` (the streams after the
source mux and the pool client); `esrc_decor_bytes_o`, `esrc_decor_valid_o`,
`esrc_whiten_push_o`, `esrc_compress_vld_o` and `esrc_compress_data_o`;
`esrc_noise_ext_i` (the noise inject); `trng_axi_isolated_probe_o` and
`trng_reset_active_probe_o`; the gated-reset probes of HMAC, KMAC, AES, OTBN
and ABR and the isolate probes of HMAC, KMAC and ABR; and the inject
`hmac_fifo_drain_stall_i`. An observation tap is a read-only probe or tap
outside that closed set: the test adds it to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe
list, and a check on it is graded. An added hook is an error inject or a drive
of a `sep` port outside that closed set: the test adds an error inject to the
inject exceptions of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and a top-port drive to the port table
of its HDL Top section, and a check on it is graded. Testbench hooks lists the
observation taps and the added hooks. A check that needs a PROPOSED item
(<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>) is blocked and is marked `Blocked: needs <item>`.
It is not counted as a reached check. The leaf runs its other checks.
* *Control for "never" checks.* Each "never" check names a window in which a
healthy design drives the signal, or a same-seed cell in which the cause is
present. The leaf logs that control in the same line or the next line.
* *Time bounds.* A bound is a multiple of the time of a clean run of the same
operation in the same seed, and the leaf logs both. No check uses an absolute
clock count as a contract.
* *One fault per injection.* The state is clean before each trial. The reset
kinds are the block reset bit of `SW_RESET_N` for AES, HMAC, KMAC, OTBN and
ABR, and the TRNG bit for ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Each reset injection changes one bit of
`SW_RESET_N` from the baseline value of the leaf: 0x7E with the engines outside
the draw held at 0 (see Run mode). The leaf logs the baseline. Bit 0 (Key Manager) stays 0 unless
the entry needs the Key Manager (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`).
* *Disable vector.* The vector stays at the harness value. SYS_DIS bit 0 is
never randomized, because it filters inbound traffic.
* *Data.* Message and plaintext words are unique per word and differ from the
register reset value, so a reorder is visible.
* *Run mode.* `no_cpu` leaves use target `lsu_stub_all_live` with
`+skip_fuse_sense`. Entropy leaves add `+esrc_noise_force`. Leaves that need
the Key Manager use the firmware image and the plusargs of the open Key Manager
sideload tests, with real fuse sense. Each leaf is in `crypto.toml` and in the
groups `cpu_stub` and `all`. An extension leg runs in the testlist entry of its
open test. Every leaf that runs an AES, KMAC or OTBN operation
also adds `+esrc_noise_force`, selects the internal source in
`SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL` (the reset value selects the external source,
which the open elaboration ties to zero), brings up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN, and
holds in reset the engines that are not in the draw. Stated exceptions:
`sep_trng_ext_sink_reset_rand_test` takes the words of its AES, KMAC and OTBN
operations from the external stream, so it adds no `+esrc_noise_force`, selects
the external source and leaves ESRC, CSRNG and EDN uninitialized; the extension
leg of `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test` keeps HMAC out of reset for
its bystander; `sep_aes_stream_status_rand_test` keeps HMAC out of reset and
idle for the control of its no-interrupt check. Tool "Verilator and VCS" means
both.
Tool "VCS" means a `tools = ["vcs"]` pin, because the leaf reads state after
reset.
* *Noise injection.* The leaf drives `esrc_noise_ext_i` (one bit per generator)
per lane with the stuck modes `stuck0` and `stuck1` or with an exact pattern
(next rule). It changes a lane bit only in the `clk_i` cycle after a strobe of
`esrc_decor_valid_o` and holds it to the next strobe. With BYPASS set for a
lane the decorrelator byte is then 0x00 or 0xFF, the held bit in all eight
bits: the feedback is zero, the chain shifts the noise bit unmixed into the
tap stages 21 to 28, and the byte period (`SAMPLE_CLK_DIV` + 1, 64 at the reset
value) is longer than the 29-stage chain
(`hw/ip/entropy_source/doc/architecture.adoc`, Decorrelation and BIW Mixing).
With BYPASS clear the feedback from the last stage mixes the noise, so a noise
bit does not give a stated byte. Every leg that drives a stuck mode or a pattern
therefore sets `DECORRELATOR_CTRL.BYPASS` on all 12 lanes, and keeps
`SAMPLE_CLK_DIV` and `ENTROPY_BYTE_MASK` at their reset values unless the entry
states another value. Control: in each window whose health-test outcome a check
uses, the leaf compares the 12 lane bytes of `esrc_decor_bytes_o` at every
strobe with the held bits (0xFF for 1, 0x00 for 0); a mismatch logs
`CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf. Expected values come from this rule and
never from a copy of the RTL.
* *Exact patterns.* A pattern repeats every 64 words (strobes of
`esrc_decor_valid_o`). Every window size of the area is a multiple of 64, so a
window of W words holds W/64 whole periods whatever its start, and each count
below is exact for every window. `balanced`: lane j carries at word n the
parity of m_j AND p(n mod 64), where m_0 to m_11 are 12 different 6-bit masks
of odd weight and p is a permutation of 0 to 63 drawn from the seed. Each lane
has 32 ones per period, so W/2 ones per window. No mask is the XOR of two
others, so each bit of a GF(2^8) multiply-add a * b + c of three different lane
bytes (0x00 or 0xFF) also has 32 ones per period, whichever lanes feed the four
BIW multiply-adds (the specification does not state the lane map). `bias70`
and `bias30`: 45 and 19 ones per period at positions drawn from the seed, so
45W/64 and 19W/64 ones per window. `square` L (L = 8, 16 or 32): runs of exactly
L zeros and L ones, with a phase per lane drawn from the seed; a window holds
W/2 ones, its longest run is L, and it holds W/L - 1 or W/L changes from word
to word (the window edge can drop one) and at most W/L pair alternations
(convention "Health-test limit trips"). `alternate`: the lane bit changes at
every word, with a start value per lane drawn from the seed; a window holds W/2
ones, its longest run is 1, and every pair of consecutive words differs. The
leaf logs the masks, the permutation, the one positions, the phases and the
start values in its plan.
* *Clean start.* Every ESRC start of the area (after a TRNG reset, and at every
rise of `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`) is a clean start unless the entry states a
failing start. While `MODULE_ENABLE` = 0 the leaf writes
`DECORRELATOR_CTRL.BYPASS` = 0xFFF, the window W (2048 unless the entry states
another) and the clean limits of W, drives `balanced` on all 12 lanes, and then
writes `MODULE_ENABLE` = 1. The clean limits of a window W are
`APT_PROPORTION_1BIT` = 3W/4, `APT_PROPORTION_LO` = W/4,
`PROB_01_THRESHOLD` = W + 1, `PROB_10_THRESHOLD` = 0 and `REPETITION_LIMIT`
= 200. No window of a `balanced`, `bias70`, `bias30`, `square` or `alternate`
lane, and no 32-bit lane of a `balanced` stream, reaches a clean limit: their
one counts lie between 19W/64 and 45W/64, a window holds at most W
alternations, every count meets `PROB_10_THRESHOLD` = 0, and no
run is longer than 45 words, or 90 words across one change of pattern. So the
startup window passes by construction. A stuck lane fails the clean limits: 0
or W ones per window, and a run above 200 words. An entry writes other
limits only after `BOOT_PHASE_DONE` reads 1 and states them. A leg that grades
a reset limit writes it after the clean start.
* *Field bits.* Every graded compare, poll and X check of a register value uses
the field bits of the register text only (the overlay or generated register
text, or the RDL). A bit that the register text lists as `Reserved`, with access
"-" and reset "-", has no stated read value: the leaf masks it out, logs it in
the line of the check (`rsvd=0x<r>`) or as `OBS-RSVD seed=<n> reg=<r> rsvd=0x<r>`,
and no check grades it. A written value is compared with the field bits of the
readback. A register that is one 32-bit field (DIGEST, `STATE`, DATA_OUT, IV,
`INSN_CNT`, the HMAC `ERR_CODE`) is compared in full. The CSRNG and EDN
`ERR_CODE` registers hold `Reserved` bits, so only their field bits are compared.
* *Unstated values.* A leg that would rest on a value the specification does not
state is not part of an entry. It is listed under
<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>.

===== Conventions where the text is silent

* Register codes that the generated register text lists by name only (AES
`MODE` ECB, CBC and CTR; AES `PRNG_RESEED_RATE` PER_64 and PER_8K; KMAC `mode`,
`kstrength` and `CMD`; HMAC `digest_size` and `key_length`; the KMAC
`ENTROPY_SEED` write count; the KMAC `STATE` share layout) come from the
vendored upstream register description
`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/<ip>/data/<ip>.hjson`, the ruling
source for them. The rate order PER_1 > PER_64 > PER_8K is the one in that
file. The `HT_WATERMARK_NUM` codes come from the `WATERMARK_TEST` enum of
`hw/ip/entropy_source/regs/entropy_source.rdl`.
* Window scaling: the reset health-test limits belong to the reset window size 2048.
A leaf that writes another window writes it in the clean start, with the clean
limits of that window (common rule "Clean start"), and logs them. A leaf that
sets its own limits states them in the entry.
* Alert receiver index: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` gives each block a range of
receiver indices (HMAC [0]; OTBN [2:1]; AES [4:3]; KMAC [6:5]; CSRNG [8:7]; EDN
[10:9]) and does not map an `ALERT_TEST` bit to one index of the range. The
leaves of this area grade the aggregate source 33 (vector bit 32) only. The check
that exactly one receiver of the range of the block pulses belongs to
`sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test` and reads the alert receiver probe, an
observation tap.
No leaf grades which index of the range pulses (see
<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).
* Malformed alert: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (OpenTitan Alert Termination)
states that a malformed encoding on the alert pair raises the integrity-fail of
the receiver and that the Crypto alert source is the OR of every receiver pulse
and integrity-fail. It does not list the malformed encodings. The leaf uses one:
`alert_p` and `alert_n` at the same value, which is not a differential code.
* Health-test limit trips: `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.REPETITION_LIMIT` is the maximum
allowed repetition count, `MARKOV_TEST_PROB_THRESHOLDS.PROB_01_THRESHOLD` is the
maximum allowed alternation count and `PROB_10_THRESHOLD` is the minimum allowed
alternation count at the window boundary (`entropy_source.adoc`). The Markov
test counts bit-pair transitions (`architecture.adoc`, Health Testing) with the
internal structure of the OpenTitan `entropy_src` Markov test: it splits the
words of a window into non-overlapping pairs of consecutive words and counts,
per lane, the pairs whose two bits differ. That count is the alternation count
of this area. A count of the changes from one word to the next is the other
reading, and every limit trip below trips under both readings. The repetition
and Markov low trials drive `square` L on all 12 lanes: the longest run is L,
and a window holds at most W/L pair alternations (the phase of a lane sets
where its changes fall against the pairs) and W/L - 1 or W/L changes from word
to word. The Markov high trial drives `alternate` on all 12 lanes: every pair
of consecutive words differs, so a window holds exactly W/2 pair alternations
whatever the phase and the window start, and W - 1 or W changes from word to
word. The failing limit lies at least three counts beyond these statistics:
`REPETITION_LIMIT` = L - 3, `PROB_01_THRESHOLD` = W/2 - 3 or
`PROB_10_THRESHOLD` = W/L + 3. The margin covers the window edge and both
readings of "allowed" (a fail above the limit or at it). The other limits keep
their clean values. The clean `PROB_10_THRESHOLD` of 0 is met by every count,
a count of 0 included, because the register text names it the minimum allowed
count. The leaf writes the
failing limit only after two whole windows of the pattern, so every window that
the limit meets holds only that pattern. The leaf counts words at
`esrc_decor_valid_o` and uses no absolute byte period.
* Shadow update error: for the AES `CTRL_SHADOWED` and `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`
registers the upstream `aes.hjson` states that two writes are required, that two
writes of different values trigger a recoverable alert, and that a read restarts
the phase tracking, so the next write is a first write. The injection is a
second write with a different value and no read between the two writes. For
KMAC the register text names the update error and does not state the cause, so
the KMAC shadowed trials log their outcome and grade nothing on it (see
<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).
* HMAC digest order: `digest_swap` = 1 converts each DIGEST word to big-endian
byte order. The default 0 is read as the opposite byte order.
* Multi-bit boolean: the RDL of CSRNG and EDN gives kMultiBitBool4True = 6 and
kMultiBitBool4False = 9, and the reset value 0x9.
* OTBN: the specification points to the OpenTitan OTBN documentation for the
core. Expected results come from independent Python operators, never from the
DUT.
* ESRC observe FIFO: `NOISE_OBS_OVERFLOW` and `BIW_OBS_OVERFLOW` say that a word
is discarded when the FIFO is full. The entries grade the plateau level, the
overflow bit and its write-one clear. Which word is discarded is blocked.
* Window offsets of HMAC, KMAC and OTBN come from the integration RDL, the
ruling source for them.

===== Testbench hooks

Every hook below is outside the closed set of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. A read-only
probe, tap or monitor is an observation tap: the test adds it to the
SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list, and a check on it is graded. A drive of a
`sep` port and a fault force are added hooks: the test adds a top-port drive to
the port table of the HDL Top section of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` and an error inject
to its inject exceptions, and a check on it is graded. Each force is released
after the trial.

* External stream driver (added hook, top-port drive). A driver on `ext_trng_axis_req_i[2:0]` and
a driver on `ext_trng_irq_i` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`). The open
integration wrapper ties the request array and `ext_trng_irq_i` to zero, so the
driver is a wrapper variant or a testbench force at the `sep` boundary. The
driver has a source reset: when a leaf asserts the TRNG reset it can withdraw
the beat it holds. This is a reset of the source, not a dropped `tvalid` of a
live beat, and the leaf logs it. It serves
`sep_trng_ext_stream_handshake_rand_test`,
`sep_trng_ext_sink_reset_rand_test` and the external Key Manager leg of
`sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test`.
* External stream response monitor (observation tap). A read-only monitor on
`ext_trng_axis_rsp_o[2:0]`. It serves the same tests as the external stream
driver.
* Alert receiver probe (observation tap). A read-only probe on the 11-bit
receiver pulse vector `crypto_alert_pulse` in `sep_crypto`. It serves
`sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test` of the mailbox, interrupt and PIC area,
which owns the receiver-range check. No leaf of this area uses it.
* Alert sender inject (added hook, error inject). A force of the `alert_p` and `alert_n` pair of
one crypto alert receiver to the same value for one trial. It serves the
malformed alert leg of `sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test`.
* Detune probe (observation tap). A read-only probe on the per-lane
ring-oscillator detune select. It serves `sep_esrc_autotune_detune_test`.
* OTBN status probe (observation tap). A read-only probe on the OTBN operational
state code (`status_q` of the OTBN core), sampled on every `clk_i`. It serves
`sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test`.
* Internal DRBG endpoint probe (observation tap). A read-only probe on the
internal DRBG stream endpoint of stream i before the source mux
(`drbg_int_axis_req[i]`, `drbg_int_axis_rsp[i]` in `sep_crypto`). It serves
`sep_trng_ext_stream_handshake_rand_test` and the external Key Manager leg of
`sep_drbg_sink_mix_rand_test`.
* ESRC SHA-256 status tap (observation tap). A read-only tap on the next value
of the ESRC `SHA256_STATUS` register fields, sampled on every `clk_i`. It
serves `sep_esrc_conditioning_matrix_rand_test`.
* ESRC FIFO integrity inject (added hook, error inject). A one-bit force of one stored parity
bit of the main FIFO entry at the read pointer, and a one-bit force of one bit
of one pointer copy of the main FIFO. It serves the integrity leg of
`sep_esrc_fifo_flow_rand_test`.
* ESRC noise-source stuck-at inject (added hook, error inject). A force of the
output of the noise ring oscillator of each lane to a constant 0 or 1, a stuck
noise source. The constant reaches the sampling flop, the synchronizer and the
pre-decorrelator samples of the noise observe FIFO (`NOISE_OBS_RDATA`). It
serves the lane, flush and lock legs of `sep_esrc_observe_fifo_rand_test`.

The ABR entry takes its register map from `integration/rdl/sep/abr_reg.rdl`. The two entries `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` and `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test` are extension legs of open tests: they add cells to the run of the open test and are not separate leaves. The external stream checks of the TRNG entries, the malformed alert check, the FIFO integrity checks and the noise observe word checks use the added driver and injects listed under Testbench hooks.

===== Entries
[[sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test_leg]]
===== 1.25 `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`, extension leg: crypto interrupt lines

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (Interrupts)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `reseed = 4`; Verilator and VCS (the testlist entry of the open test has no `tools` pin; VCS is 4-state and shows an X in the zero reset readback of step 2 that the 2-state Verilator hides). The leg runs in the testlist entry of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` (`system.toml`) and keeps its run mode: `INTR_TEST` sets a state bit, so the leg needs no entropy and no fuse set-up. That entry has `seed = 1` and no `reseed`; the leg needs it to take `reseed = 4`, which the build of the leg adds to the entry.

*Source Module*: `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master readback and write of `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE` and `INTR_TEST` of HMAC, KMAC, CSRNG and EDN, and of `SW_RESET_N` (approved); `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`, source N on bit N-1 (approved); the gated-reset probes of HMAC and KMAC and `trng_reset_active_probe_o` (approved); register model from the generated register description. No inject. No PROPOSED item. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: per block the `INTR_ENABLE` mask E, the `INTR_TEST` mask T and the clear mask C; the block order; the source order inside a block; the order of the three cells after the first cell; the flip order. Legal range: the driven bits of a block, which are its defined source bits except the `fifo_empty` bits of HMAC and KMAC. E, T and C hold 0 at the `fifo_empty` positions. Every block has more than one driven bit, and E and T each hold at least one 0 and one 1 over the driven bits. C holds at least one set bit when T has one. `reseed = 4` samples different masks and orders. The plan shape (all 10 sources, all four cells of each, the corner masks) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: Each interrupt line of HMAC (sources 18 and 20), KMAC (21 and 23), CSRNG (24 to 27) and EDN (28 and 29) is the `INTR_STATE` bit AND the `INTR_ENABLE` bit, and vector bit N-1 carries source N. `INTR_TEST` forces a state bit. The 10 state bits clear on a write of one. The `fifo_empty` lines (sources 19 and 22) and the OTBN line (source 30) belong to other entries (see the scope limit). Spec: `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/<ip>/regs/gen/adoc/<ip>.adoc` for hmac, kmac, csrng and edn (`INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`, `INTR_TEST`).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`, which walks `INTR_TEST` one source at a time with the enable set for the CSRNG, EDN and HMAC `err` sources and grades the write-one clear and the one-hot isolation of the vector, and `sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`, which raises `hmac_done`, `kmac_done`, `cs_cmd_req_done` and `edn_cmd_req_done` together with the enable set. This leg adds the source `kmac_err` to the table of the first test. For every source it adds the cell with the enable clear, the enable flip with the state set, the write-one clear with the enable clear and drawn mixed masks. This leg grades only the new cells. The open owners keep the rest. The cell (1, 1) and the write-one clear with the enable set of the sources that the open tests walk run as controls of the new cells, and so does the cell (0, 0) of the seven sources that `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` walks (the four CSRNG sources, the two EDN sources and `hmac_err`), whose baseline check grades the line low with the state and the enable of the source at 0. Cells: 10 sources in the four (state, enable) cells; an enable flip at state 1 and a write-one clear at enable 0 and 1 for each of the 10 bits; drawn mixed masks per block; the corner masks all ones, all zeros and a single common bit over the driven bits. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_w1c_clear`, `cp_enable_flip`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`, the run mode of the open test. Write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7E and log it as the baseline. The Key Manager bit 0 stays 0 (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`). Print `PLAN seed=<n>` with the drawn masks and orders.
. Define the block reset that every trial uses. Write the baseline with the bit of the block cleared (HMAC bit 3, KMAC bit 4; the TRNG bit 5 for CSRNG and EDN). Wait for the gated-reset probe of the block (`trng_reset_active_probe_o` for the TRNG) to show active. Write the baseline back. Wait for the first OKAY readback of `INTR_STATE` of the block. Each wait is bounded and a timeout fails the leaf. Then read `INTR_STATE` and `INTR_ENABLE` of the block. Both read 0 (RDL reset value). A non-zero read fails the leaf, because the state is not clean.
. From here to the end, sample `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` on every `clk_i` and log the clock of each change of a vector bit of the four blocks.
. Cell walk. For each of the 10 sources, in the drawn block order and the drawn source order inside the block, run the cell (1, 1) first and then the other three cells in the drawn order. Per cell: reset the block (step 2); write `INTR_ENABLE` with only the bit of the source set when the enable is 1; write `INTR_TEST` with only the bit of the source set when the state is 1, and write the enable before the test; read `INTR_STATE`; take the vector sample of the block (CHK-INTR-BITWALK).
. In the cell (1, 1) of a source, log t_rise: the clocks from the write response that completes the cell to the first clock with the vector bit high (0 when it is high at the response). The wait for the bit is bounded and a timeout fails CHK-INTR-BITWALK. Every expected-low sample of that source takes the window from the response of the last write of the trial to the end of the readback of the same trial plus a hold of 4 times t_rise. The bit stays low for the whole window. The leaf logs t_rise and the hold.
. Enable flip, for each source in a cell with the state 1. Write `INTR_ENABLE` with the bit of the source toggled. Take the vector sample (CHK-INTR-FLIP). Toggle the bit back and take the vector sample again. The two toggles give one rise and one fall.
. Write-one clear, for each source in a cell with the state 1. Read the state (it reads 1). Write 1 to the bit of the source in `INTR_STATE`. Read `INTR_STATE` and take the vector sample (CHK-INTR-BITWALK). The enable value at the clear is the enable value of the cell.
. Mask trial. For each block, run 2 drawn trials and the five corner trials in the drawn order. The corner trials use (E, T) = (all ones, all ones), (all ones, 0), (0, all ones), (0, 0) and one pair that shares exactly one set bit. All ones means every driven bit of the block. Per trial: reset the block; write E to `INTR_ENABLE`; write T to `INTR_TEST`; read `INTR_STATE`; take the vector sample of the block with a hold of 4 times the largest t_rise of the block (CHK-INTR-LINE). Each expected-high bit rises within a bounded wait, and a timeout fails CHK-INTR-LINE.
. In the same trial, write C to `INTR_STATE`. Read `INTR_STATE` and take the vector sample (CHK-INTR-CLEAR). C holds driven bits only.
. In the same trial, for each set bit that remains, run the enable flip of step 6 (CHK-INTR-FLIP).
. Write the baseline to `SW_RESET_N`. Print `RESULT sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>` when every check passed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-INTR-LINE
|After the `INTR_TEST` write, `INTR_STATE` of the block reads the test mask T and every vector bit of the block equals its state bit AND its enable bit (`interrupts.adoc`, source N on bit N-1; `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE` and `INTR_TEST` of each block). A wrong DUT shows an ignored enable (a line high with the enable 0), an aliased bit (another bit of the block moves), a line that never rises, or a state bit that does not set. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback and `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`. Control for the low lines: the cell (1, 1) of the same bit raises the line in the same seed, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-INTR-LINE PASS seed=<n> block=<b> enable=0x<e> test=0x<t> state=0x<s> vec=0x<v> expect=0x<s&e>`; `vec` is the vector bits of the block, shifted to bit 0.
|CHK-INTR-CLEAR
|A write-one clear of the drawn subset C leaves `INTR_STATE` = T AND NOT C, the vector follows (state AND enable), and no bit outside C changes (`INTR_STATE` is RW1C for the driven bits). A wrong DUT clears extra bits, leaves a bit of C set, or leaves a line high. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback and the vector probe. Control: the state read of CHK-INTR-LINE in the same trial shows the bits of C that are set before the clear. Evidence: `CHK-INTR-CLEAR PASS seed=<n> block=<b> clear=0x<c> state=0x<s2> vec=0x<v2>`.
|CHK-INTR-FLIP
|For each set bit, writing the toggled enable bit moves the line to state AND the new enable, and writing it back moves the line back, so the line follows the enable in both polarities. A wrong DUT shows a line that ignores a cleared enable or does not return. Anchor: the vector probe. Control: each flip holds one rising toggle, so the line rises after an enable write in the same trial, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-INTR-FLIP PASS seed=<n> block=<b> bit=<i> enable=<0 or 1> vec=<0 or 1>`; one line per toggle.
|CHK-INTR-BITWALK
|For each of the 10 sources and each (state, enable) cell, the vector bit equals state AND enable and no other bit of the block changes from its value after the block reset. For a bit in a cell with the state 1, the write-one clear drops the state and the line with the enable 0 and with the enable 1. A wrong DUT shows a line that ignores the enable, a bit that moves a neighbour, a clear that works with one enable value only, or a state that stays set. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback and the vector probe. Control: the cell (1, 1) of the same bit (line high), and the state read 1 just before the clear. For the sources that the open tests walk, the cell (1, 1) and the clear with the enable set are controls and are not claimed as new coverage, and the cell (0, 0) of the seven sources that `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` walks (CSRNG, EDN and `hmac_err`) is a control too. Evidence: `CHK-INTR-BITWALK PASS seed=<n> block=<b> bit=<i> cell=<s><e> vec=<v> others_unchanged=1 w1c=<n/a, 0 or 1>`; `w1c` is the enable value at the clear, and n/a for a cell with the state 0.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This leg grades only the cells that the open tests do not walk. The cell (1, 1) and the write-one clear with the enable set of the sources that the open tests walk, and the cell (0, 0) of the seven sources that `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` walks, are controls here, and the open owners keep their grade. The `hmac_fifo_empty` and `kmac_fifo_empty` lines belong to `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`, which makes the status read 1 from the FIFO and grades the line gate. The `otbn_done` line belongs to `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test`: the OTBN reset in a cell needs the entropy chain, which this run mode does not bring up, and the specification does not state whether the initial wipe after a reset raises `done`. The real done and error events of HMAC and KMAC are graded in `sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test` and `sep_kmac_stream_cfg_rand_test`. The ESRC interrupt bits are graded in `sep_esrc_csr_rw_walk_rand_test`.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test_leg]]
===== 1.26 `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`, extension leg: four-engine concurrency

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (Cross-engine)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`, `reseed = 4`; Verilator and VCS (the testlist entry of the open test has no `tools` pin). The leg runs in the testlist entry of `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test` (`crypto.toml`) and keeps its run mode. That entry has `reseed = 3`; the leg needs it to take `reseed = 4`, which the build of the leg adds to the entry. The internal source is selected in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, and ESRC, CSRNG and EDN are up as in the open test. The engines outside a group are held in reset. Stated exception: HMAC stays out of reset and idle in a group without an HMAC member, for the bystander.

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master with non-zero IDs (approved); `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]`, client 0 AES, 1 KMAC, 2 OTBN RND, 3 OTBN URND (approved; the leg counts the EDN words of the whole run, all four clients, from the first word of the run); the gated-reset probes of HMAC, KMAC, AES and OTBN (approved); independent models for the results: the software SHA-2 and HMAC model `sep_hmac_golden`, a Keccak model, an AES model and a Python operator for the OTBN program. No inject. No PROPOSED item. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the drawn pair of the third group; the start order; the start skew of each member after the first, measured from the start write response of the first member, 0 to half the shortest clean busy time of the group; the operation of each member; the message and block counts; the data. Legal ranges: HMAC SHA-256 or SHA-512 with 64 to 512 bytes; KMAC SHA3-256 or SHAKE-128 with 0 to 300 bytes in EDN entropy mode; AES ECB or CBC with a 128-bit or 256-bit key and 4 to 64 blocks; OTBN ADD, XOR or AND. Keys, IV seeds and operands come from the fixed table of test constants, and the seed picks the row. A count that would take the EDN word total of the run above the generate budget of the open test is halved against the running total (see the steps). `reseed = 4` samples different pairs, orders, skews and counts. The plan shape (three groups on every seed) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: HMAC, KMAC SHA-3, AES and OTBN are separate engines, each with its own host path and its own software reset, reached through one 64-bit slave port. An operation on one engine does not change the result of another. The specification implies this and states no direct rule. Spec: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (Cryptographic Subsystem); `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc` and `hw/sys/sep/doc/kmac.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (Isolation and Reset Sequencing).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`, which runs AES and KMAC at the same time and grades both results (`CHK-BOTH-COMPLETE`) and the overlap of their EDN beats (`CHK-OVERLAP`), and `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`, which holds one sibling result across a reset. This leg adds the HMAC and OTBN members, skewed starts, interleaved host reads and the HMAC bystander. It runs no group of the pair AES and KMAC alone. This leg grades only the new cells. The open owners keep the rest. The EDN word total of the whole run (the open test's own AES and KMAC operations, every clean run, every group run and the OTBN post-reset wipes) stays inside the generate budget of the open test. Cells: on every seed the group of all four engines, the group AES, KMAC and OTBN, and one drawn pair that is not AES and KMAC; each result against its independent golden; the overlap of every pair of a group; the finished HMAC digest read while the other engines run. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_bus_access_cg` (`cp_engine_pair`, `cp_busy_count`, `cp_bystander_read`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense` and `+esrc_noise_force` as the open test does. Select the internal source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, and bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Run the groups inside the run of the open test, after the operations of the open test, with the entropy stack that it brings up. The generate budget is half of the boot Generate that the open test leaves open. The open test does not write `EDN.BOOT_GEN_CMD`, so EDN issues the boot Generate with the reset word 0xFFF003 (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/edn/regs/gen/adoc/edn.adoc`, `BOOT_GEN_CMD`). Its GLEN of 0xFFF blocks of 128 bits, under the CSRNG command header that the register text names, is 16380 EDN words of 32 bits, so the budget is 8190 words. The other half covers the words that the EDN endpoint FIFOs of the other clients buffer. The `glen` of 32 that the open test names is the length of its `GENERATE_CMD` and of its legal Generate segments, not the length of the open boot Generate. The leg writes no EDN or CSRNG command register, so the boot Generate stays open for the whole run and the Generate segmentation check of the open test sees no completed command. Log the budget. Keep a running total W of the EDN words acked on `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` for all four clients from the first word of the run. W includes the words of the operations of the open test, of every clean run, of every group run and of the OTBN wipe after each OTBN reset. Log W at the end of this step.
. Draw the plan: the group of all four engines, the group AES, KMAC and OTBN, and one pair from HMAC with AES, KMAC or OTBN, and AES or KMAC with OTBN. Draw the operation, counts and data of each member. Print `PLAN seed=<n>` with the groups and the operations.
. For each group in the order of step 2, write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7E with the bits of the engines outside the group cleared (OTBN bit 1, AES bit 2, HMAC bit 3, KMAC bit 4). In a group without an HMAC member the HMAC bit stays 1. The Key Manager bit 0 stays 0. Wait for the gated-reset probe of each engine that is cleared to show active (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Then, for each of AES and OTBN that is a member of the group, wait until `AES.STATUS.IDLE` = 1 or `OTBN.STATUS.status` = IDLE (EDN is up; a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Steps 3 to 13 run once for each group.
. Reset each member of the group and, in a group without an HMAC member, the HMAC, one bit at a time. Clear the bit, wait for the gated-reset probe to show active, set the bit, then wait for the first OKAY readback of the `STATUS` of the engine. For AES, then wait until `AES.STATUS.IDLE` = 1. For OTBN, then wait until `OTBN.STATUS.status` = IDLE (EDN is up). Each wait is bounded and a timeout fails the leaf. No configuration write goes to AES or OTBN before this idle wait ends.
. Clean runs. Run each member alone, in the drawn order. Configure it, start it, poll its end bit (`HMAC.INTR_STATE.hmac_done` = 1, `AES.STATUS.IDLE`, `KMAC.STATUS.sha3_squeeze` = 1, `OTBN.STATUS.status` = IDLE) until it shows the end (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf), then read its result. A KMAC member reads `STATE` in the squeeze stage and then writes `CMD.done` (`kmac.hjson`, `CMD.done`: the engine goes back to idle). An AES member writes each block to `DATA_IN` when `STATUS.INPUT_READY` = 1 and reads each block from `DATA_OUT` when `STATUS.OUTPUT_VALID` = 1; its run ends when `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 after the last block is read. For KMAC, a `STATUS` read that shows busy is a read with `sha3_squeeze` = 0. For HMAC the end is `hmac_done` (`hmac.hjson`, `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`: HMAC/SHA-2 has completed), because the register text does not state when `STATUS.hmac_idle` reads 1, and a read that shows busy is an `INTR_STATE` read with `hmac_done` = 0. The member reset before each run clears `hmac_done`. Compare the result with its golden: a mismatch fails the leaf (CTRL-CONC-CLEAN). Log t_op, the clocks from the start write response to the last `STATUS` read that shows busy, and log the EDN words of the run from `crypto_edn_ack_o[i]` (the change of W over the run). Reset the member after the run, as in step 4.
. The group run repeats each member operation once. If W plus the sum of the clean-run EDN words of the group is above the generate budget, halve the largest drawn count of the group and repeat steps 4 and 5. The words of the discarded clean runs stay in W. If every count of the group is at its minimum and the sum is still above the budget, the leaf fails (CTRL-CONC-BUDGET).
. In a group without an HMAC member, run the bystander job: reset HMAC as in step 4, write `CFG` with `sha_en` = 1, `hmac_en` = 0 and `digest_size` = SHA2_256, write `CMD.hash_start`, write a 64-byte message to `MSG_FIFO`, write `CMD.hash_process`, poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf), read DIGEST, compare it with the software golden, and write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. Keep the digest as the bystander golden. HMAC stays out of reset and idle.
. Configure every member of the group: keys, IV or cipher mode, `CFG`, the OTBN program and the operands. An AES member gets its KEY words first, then a bounded wait until `AES.STATUS.IDLE` = 1 (a timeout fails the leaf), then its IV words. An HMAC member sets `CFG` with `sha_en` = 1, `hmac_en` = 1, the drawn `digest_size` and a `key_length` that is legal for it (not Key_None; Key_1024 only with SHA2_512), and writes the `KEY` words of the table row. A KMAC member has `entropy_mode` = EDN, `entropy_ready` = 1 and `sideload` = 0. The OTBN result word holds the complement of its golden. Read back the configuration of each member.
. Start the members as independent tasks on the CPU-LSU master with non-zero IDs, in the drawn start order. Each member after the first starts when its drawn skew has elapsed from the start write response of the first member. Each task starts its engine (an HMAC or KMAC task also writes its message), polls the end bit of its engine as in step 5, and reads its result after the end. A KMAC task then writes `CMD.done`. An AES task writes and reads its blocks one at a time as in step 5. The polls and result reads of the tasks interleave on the one master. Each poll is bounded by 8 times the t_op of the member plus its skew, and a timeout fails the leaf.
. In a group without an HMAC member, run a further task from the first start to the last idle read: it reads the HMAC DIGEST words in a loop with a read interval that the leaf chooses and logs. Every read completes OKAY.
