. For each pair of the group, take the busy window of each member from its start write response to its last `STATUS` read that shows busy. Compute the overlap of the pair and log it (CTRL-CONC-OVERLAP). An overlap of 0 logs `CTRL-MISSING`.
. Read W. The words of the group are the change of W over steps 3 to 12. A W above the generate budget fails the leaf (CTRL-CONC-BUDGET).
. Compare each result with its golden (CHK-CONC-RESULT). Compare each bystander read with the bystander golden (CHK-CONC-HOLD).
. After the last group, read W and log the total line of the run. A total above the generate budget fails the leaf (CTRL-CONC-BUDGET).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CONC-RESULT
|Each engine result under overlap equals its independent golden: the software SHA-2 or HMAC digest, the Keccak model digest, the AES model ciphertext, and the Python operator result in OTBN DMEM. Every host access of the group completes OKAY. The engines have separate host paths and resets (`crypto.adoc`; `reset_controller.adoc`), so a wrong DUT shows cross-talk between engines, a lost host access or a wrong result only under overlap. Anchor: the `DIGEST`, `STATE`, `DATA_OUT` and DMEM readback through the CPU-LSU master. Control: the clean run of the same member in the same seed returns its golden (CTRL-CONC-CLEAN), and the busy windows of every pair of the group overlap (CTRL-CONC-OVERLAP, `overlap_clk` > 0), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-CONC-RESULT PASS seed=<n> group=<g> engine=<e> golden_ok=1 skew=<s> busy_clk=<b>`, one line per member; `CTRL-CONC-OVERLAP seed=<n> pair=<e1,e2> overlap_clk=<k>`, one line per pair; `CTRL-CONC-CLEAN seed=<n> engine=<e> golden_ok=1 t_op=<t> edn_words=<w>`, one line per member; `CTRL-CONC-BUDGET seed=<n> group=<g> words=<w> run_total=<t> budget=<b>`, one line per group, with `words` the change of W over the group; and one total line `CTRL-CONC-BUDGET seed=<n> total=<t> budget=<b>` at the end of the run. A total above the budget fails the leaf.
|CHK-CONC-HOLD
|In a group without an HMAC member, every HMAC DIGEST word read while the other engines run equals the digest of the HMAC job of step 7, and no read returns another value. A wrong DUT shows a bystander digest that a peer operation changes. Anchor: the `DIGEST` readback. Control: the logged count of reads that completed while at least one peer is busy (from the busy windows of step 11) is at least 1, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`; the bystander golden is non-zero, because the leaf draws the bystander message so that it is. Evidence: `CHK-CONC-HOLD PASS seed=<n> bystander=hmac reads=<r> stable=1`; `r` counts the reads that completed while a peer is busy.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This leg claims only the HMAC and OTBN members, the skewed starts, the interleaved host reads and the HMAC bystander. It grades the AES and KMAC results only in groups that also have an HMAC or an OTBN member. The open owners keep AES and KMAC alone (`CHK-BOTH-COMPLETE`, `CHK-OVERLAP`) and the sibling result across a reset. The leg claims no EDN beat overlap, grant order or routing. The specification implies separate engines with their own host paths and software resets and states no direct rule, so the contract is the implied one.

'''''

[[sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test]]
===== 1.27 `sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (HMAC)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `reseed = 4`; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master readback and write of the HMAC registers (`INTR_STATE`, `CFG`, `CMD`, `STATUS`, `DIGEST`, `ERR_CODE`, `KEY`, `MSG_FIFO`) (approved); the gated-reset probe of HMAC (approved); `sep_hmac_golden`, a software SHA-2 and HMAC model with the key word order of `hmac.hjson` (the `KEY` multireg). No inject. No PROPOSED item. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the order of the ten classes, the digest size of the plain SHA-2 class, the invalid `digest_size` value (two or three of the bits 2:0 set), the invalid `key_length` value (two to five of the bits 4:0 set), the message length of each hash that runs (0 to 64 bytes), the key row of the fixed table, the key length of each recovery hash (legal for its digest size), and the message bytes. The leaf redraws the length and bytes of a recovery message until the golden digest of the recovery hash differs from D_prev. `reseed = 4` samples four orders and draws. The plan shape (all ten classes on every seed) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: In HMAC mode (`hmac_en` = 1) a start with `key_length` Key_None (an invalid or all-zero value maps to Key_None), or with Key_1024 on SHA2_256, is blocked and signalled: `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` sets, no `hmac_done` is raised and DIGEST is unchanged. A start with `digest_size` SHA2_None (an invalid or all-zero value maps to SHA2_None) is blocked and signalled in the same way, with `hmac_en` = 0 or 1. A plain SHA-2 start (`hmac_en` = 0) ignores `key_length`. Key_1024 on SHA2_384 and on SHA2_512 is legal. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/hmac/regs/gen/adoc/hmac.adoc` (`INTR_STATE`, `CFG`, `STATUS`, `ERR_CODE`); upstream `hmac.hjson` (`CFG.digest_size`, `CFG.key_length`) for the codes and the blocked starts.

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`, which drives the legal cells and reads a zero error status (`CHK-ERR`), and `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`, which forces `hmac_err` with `INTR_TEST`. This test raises `hmac_err` from a real configuration error and grades the blocked cells and the recovery. The legal cells run only as controls (CTRL-HMAC-NOBLOCK), and the open owner keeps them. Cells: `hmac_en` = 1 with Key_None on SHA2_256, SHA2_384 and SHA2_512; `hmac_en` = 1 with Key_1024 on SHA2_256; `hmac_en` = 1 with an invalid multi-bit `key_length` on SHA2_256; `hmac_en` = 0 with `digest_size` all-zero; `hmac_en` = 0 with an invalid multi-bit `digest_size`; the write-one clear of `hmac_err` and a legal hash after each blocked start. These seven blocked cells and the recovery are graded. The three legal cells (`hmac_en` = 0 with Key_None on a drawn digest size; `hmac_en` = 1 with Key_1024 on SHA2_384 and on SHA2_512) run as controls. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_invalid_encoding_cg` (`x_hmac_start_class`, `cp_hmac_key_src`) and `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_hw_raise` for `hmac_err`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`. Print `PLAN seed=<n>` with the class order and the draws. Reset HMAC: write `SW_RESET_N` with bit 3 cleared from the baseline 0x7E, wait for the gated-reset probe of HMAC to show active, write the baseline back, and wait for the first OKAY readback of `STATUS` (each wait bounded; a timeout fails the leaf). Every `CFG` write of this test sets `sha_en` = 1.
. Reference hash. Write `CFG` (SHA2_256, `hmac_en` = 0), write `CMD.hash_start`, write a drawn message to `MSG_FIFO`, write `CMD.hash_process`, and poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Log t_clean, the clocks from the `hash_start` write response to the first read that shows `hmac_done`. Read DIGEST and compare it with the golden. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. For each class in the drawn order, read `STATUS` and log `hmac_idle` as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE` (not graded), and read `INTR_STATE` (`hmac_err` and `hmac_done` read 0). Then run steps 4 to 8 for a blocked class or step 9 for a legal class.
. Blocked class (the three Key_None classes, Key_1024 on SHA2_256 and the invalid key length, all with `hmac_en` = 1; `digest_size` all-zero and the invalid `digest_size`, both with `hmac_en` = 0). Before the `CFG` write of the class, read DIGEST under the `CFG` of the last completed hash (the words of its digest size) and keep it as D_prev. D_prev equals the golden digest of that hash, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails; the leaf draws the message of that hash so that its golden is non-zero.
. Write `CFG` with the digest size, key length and `hmac_en` of the class. When `hmac_en` = 1 write the 32 `KEY` words of a table row (the engine is idle, so the write is legal).
. Error injection. Point: `CMD.hash_start`. Kind: a start with the blocked `digest_size`, `key_length` and `hmac_en` of the class. Expected response: `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` = 1, no `hmac_done` and DIGEST unchanged. Write `CMD.hash_start`. Hold for 3 times t_clean. Log both values.
. At the end of the hold, read `INTR_STATE`, `STATUS` and `ERR_CODE`. Then write `CFG` back to the `CFG` of the last completed hash (`sha_en` = 1, so the digest is not cleared) and read the DIGEST words of its digest size (CHK-HMAC-BLOCK). The register text gives the DIGEST layout per digest size and does not state the readback after a change of `digest_size`, so DIGEST is compared only under the `CFG` under which D_prev was read. Log the `ERR_CODE` value (`OBS-HMAC-ERRCODE`); the leaf grades only that it is non-zero. Log `STATUS.hmac_idle` as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE` (not graded).
. Recovery. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` and read `INTR_STATE` (`hmac_err` reads 0). Write `CFG` for a legal HMAC hash (a drawn digest size and a key length that is legal for it), write the `KEY` words, write `CMD.hash_start`, write a drawn message whose golden digest differs from D_prev, write `CMD.hash_process`, and poll until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait). Read DIGEST and compare it with the golden and with D_prev (CHK-HMAC-RECOVER). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. Go to step 3 for the next class.
. Legal class (`hmac_en` = 0 with Key_None on a drawn digest size; `hmac_en` = 1 with Key_1024 on SHA2_384 and on SHA2_512). Write `CFG`, write the `KEY` words when `hmac_en` = 1, write `CMD.hash_start`, write a drawn message, write `CMD.hash_process`, and poll until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING`). Read `INTR_STATE`, DIGEST and compare it with the golden (CTRL-HMAC-NOBLOCK). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. Go to step 3 for the next class.
. Print `RESULT sep_hmac_illegal_cfg_start_test seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>` when every check passed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HMAC-BLOCK
|For a blocked class, at the end of the hold `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` reads 1, `hmac_done` reads 0, DIGEST read under the restored `CFG` of the last completed hash equals D_prev (the golden of that hash), and `ERR_CODE` is non-zero (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CFG`: "starting is blocked and an error is signalled to SW"; `hmac.hjson`, `CFG.digest_size` and `CFG.key_length`). `STATUS.hmac_idle` is logged and not graded, because the register text does not state its value after a blocked start (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). A wrong DUT starts the hash (`hmac_done` rises or DIGEST changes) or blocks it with no error. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE`, `STATUS`, `DIGEST` and `ERR_CODE` readback. Control for "never starts": D_prev, a golden digest, is non-zero by the draw; the legal classes of the same seed raise no error, set `hmac_done` and return their golden digest (CTRL-HMAC-NOBLOCK); and the recovery hash of the same seed changes DIGEST. Without these the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-BLOCK PASS seed=<n> class=<c> digest=0b<s> keylen=0b<k> hmac_en=<e> err=1 done=0 digest_unchanged=1 d_prev=0x<p> err_code_nonzero=1 window_clk=<w> t_clean=<c>`; `p` is DIGEST_0 of D_prev. `OBS-HMAC-ERRCODE seed=<n> err_code=0x<c>` and `OBS-HMAC-IDLE seed=<n> class=<c> phase=<before or blocked> idle=<i>` are logged and not graded. `CTRL-HMAC-NOBLOCK seed=<n> digest=<s> keylen=<k> hmac_en=<e> err=0 done=1 digest==golden`, one line per legal class.
|CHK-HMAC-RECOVER
|After the write-one clear of `hmac_err` the bit reads 0, and a legal HMAC hash returns its golden digest, which differs from D_prev (`hmac.adoc` register text of `INTR_STATE`: RW1C). A wrong DUT keeps the error bit set or leaves the engine unable to start. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback and the DIGEST readback against the golden. Control: the digest differs from D_prev, so a started hash does change DIGEST. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-RECOVER PASS seed=<n> err_cleared=1 digest==golden digest_changed=1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This test claims the blocked start cells and the recovery. The legal start cells run only as controls, and the open owner keeps them. It grades only that `ERR_CODE` is non-zero, because the specification does not list the values. `STATUS.hmac_idle` is logged and not graded: the register text gives only its reset value 1 and the rule that context writes are legal while it is 1. It does not drive a blocked `hash_continue`. The open owner (`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`) keeps the legal cells and the zero error status. The `hmac_err` line to the interrupt aggregator, driven with `INTR_TEST`, belongs to `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`.

'''''

[[sep_hmac_fifo_stream_rand_test]]
===== 1.28 `sep_hmac_fifo_stream_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (HMAC)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `reseed = 4`; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_hmac_fifo_stream_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master readback, write and write-response timing (`STATUS`, `INTR_STATE`, `CFG`, `CMD`, `KEY`, `DIGEST`, the `MSG_FIFO` window at offset 0x1000) (approved); the inject `hmac_fifo_drain_stall_i`, which holds the read side of the message FIFO idle (approved); the gated-reset probe of HMAC (approved); `sep_hmac_golden`, a software SHA-2 and HMAC model. No PROPOSED item. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the cell order, the write mode of each cell, the gap of the gapped mode (0 to 200 clocks), the message words (unique per word and different from the register reset value), `hmac_en` of each cell, the key row of the fixed table, the key length (legal for the digest size), the number of writes between two `STATUS` reads (1 to 8; a stalled leg reads `STATUS` only as the steps say), the order of the three stalled legs, the digest size and plain or HMAC choice of each stalled leg and each `fifo_empty` leg, and the digest size, the key length, the message and the wipe value w of the wipe leg (w has four different bytes and differs from every digest word of the hash before the wipe). `reseed = 4` samples different orders, modes and gaps. The plan shape (25 cells, three stalled legs, two `fifo_empty` legs and one wipe leg on every seed) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: `STATUS.fifo_depth`, `fifo_full` and `fifo_empty` report the message FIFO occupancy. A write to a full FIFO back-pressures the bus and the data is not lost. `INTR_STATE.fifo_empty` is raised only if the FIFO has been full before, software can still write and HMAC is not in the second round. A write of w to `WIPE_SECRET` overwrites the digest and the secret key with w. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/hmac/regs/gen/adoc/hmac.adoc` (`INTR_STATE`, `STATUS`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/data/hmac.hjson` (`WIPE_SECRET`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (Single-Beat Access Only).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`, which uses messages of 1 to 16 words. This test grades the FIFO status, long messages, back-pressure and the `fifo_empty` interrupt. The open owner keeps the legal digest cells. Cells: every (digest size, length class) cell, SHA-256 with 0, 1, 3, 4, 55, 56, 63, 64, 65, 200 and 1000 bytes and SHA-384 and SHA-512 with 111, 112, 127, 128, 129, 200 and 1000 bytes, plain SHA-2 or HMAC drawn per cell; three write modes (a burst of back-to-back single-beat posted writes, single writes with drawn gaps, single-byte writes), each with a full FIFO reached with the drain stall and drained after the release; the `fifo_empty` interrupt after a full FIFO and with a FIFO that never reaches full; the `WIPE_SECRET` overwrite of the digest and of the key after a hash. Functional coverage: `sep_hmac_cfg_op_cg` (`x_len_class`, `x_fifo_state_mode`, `cp_backpressure`) and `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`cp_hmac_fifo_empty_qual`, `x_line_gate`, `cp_hw_raise` for `hmac_fifo_empty`). The wipe leg fills no Phase 2 bin.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`. Print `PLAN seed=<n>` with the cell order and the draws. Define "reset HMAC": write `SW_RESET_N` with bit 3 cleared from the baseline 0x7E, wait for the gated-reset probe of HMAC to show active, write the baseline back, and wait for the first OKAY readback of `STATUS` (each wait bounded; a timeout fails the leaf). Every `CFG` write sets `sha_en` = 1. In an HMAC hash, write the 32 `KEY` words of a table row after `CFG` and before `CMD.hash_start`.
. Reset HMAC. Read `STATUS`. `fifo_empty` and `hmac_idle` read 1 (RDL reset values). Count the read as a `STATUS` sample (CHK-HMAC-STATUS).
. Cell leg, for each of the 25 cells in the drawn order. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` for the cell, with plain SHA-2 or HMAC drawn. Write `CMD.hash_start`. The drain stall is not applied.
. Write the message of the cell to the `MSG_FIFO` window in the drawn write mode: a burst of back-to-back single-beat posted writes (`AxLEN` = 0, no gap; no AXI burst goes to the crypto region), single writes with the drawn gap, or single-byte writes. Read `STATUS` after the drawn number of writes (CHK-HMAC-STATUS). Log `hmac_idle` of each read as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE` (not graded: the register text does not state when the bit reads 0).
. Write `CMD.hash_process`. Poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-MSG). Read `STATUS` (CHK-HMAC-STATUS) and log `hmac_idle` as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE`. Read DIGEST and compare it with the software golden of the whole message (CHK-HMAC-MSG). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Stalled leg, for each of the three write modes in the drawn order. First find the write count N that fills the FIFO: Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` with a drawn digest size and plain SHA-2 or HMAC. Apply the inject `hmac_fifo_drain_stall_i`. Write `CMD.hash_start`. Write unique writes of the width of the leg mode (32-bit for the burst and gap modes, one byte for the byte mode), one at a time, and read `STATUS` after each write, until a read shows `fifo_full` = 1 (CHK-HMAC-STATUS, phase stalled). Log the depth of that read as D and the count of writes as N. If the 1000-byte message ends before `fifo_full` reads 1, log `CTRL-MISSING`. Release the stall and reset HMAC. The hash is not processed.
. Run the leg with the same `CFG`. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG`. Apply the inject `hmac_fifo_drain_stall_i`. Write `CMD.hash_start`.
. Write the first write of a 1000-byte message in the leg mode. Log t_clean_wr, the clocks from the issue of the beat to its write response. The FIFO is not full, so this is a clean write.
. Write the next writes of the message in the leg mode until exactly N writes are posted, with no `STATUS` read between them. Read `STATUS` once (CHK-HMAC-STATUS, phase stalled): `fifo_full` reads 1. If it reads 0, log `CTRL-MISSING`.
. With the stall held and `fifo_full` read 1, issue the next beat of the message. Hold for 16 times t_clean_wr and log both values. Do not read `STATUS` during the hold: the host path carries one transaction at a time, so a read behind the held write waits for it. The beat gets no write response during the hold (CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE).
. Release the stall. Wait for the write response of the held beat (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE). The response is OKAY.
. Write the rest of the message in the leg mode. Read `STATUS` after the drawn number of writes (phase drain, from the release on).
. Read `STATUS` until `fifo_depth` reads 0 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-STATUS). Each read is a sample.
. Write `CMD.hash_process`. Poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-MSG). Read `STATUS` (CHK-HMAC-STATUS) and log `hmac_idle` as `OBS-HMAC-IDLE`. Read DIGEST and compare it with the software golden of the 1000 bytes (CHK-HMAC-MSG). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Full `fifo_empty` leg. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` for a drawn digest size and plain SHA-2 or HMAC. Apply the inject `hmac_fifo_drain_stall_i`. Write `CMD.hash_start`. Write unique 32-bit words, one at a time, and read `STATUS` after each, until a read shows `fifo_full` = 1. Log the depth of that read as D.
. Release the stall. Read `STATUS` until `fifo_depth` reads 0. Log t_drain, the clocks from the release to the first read that shows `fifo_depth` = 0. Hold for 4 times t_drain. Read `INTR_STATE` (CHK-HMAC-FIFOEMPTY, leg full). Do not write `CMD.hash_process` before this read.
. Not-full `fifo_empty` leg. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` for a drawn digest size and plain SHA-2 or HMAC. Do not apply the stall. Write `CMD.hash_start`. Write 2 unique words, one at a time, each after the write response of the one before, and read `STATUS` after each. The depth D that the full leg logs is above 2, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`.
. Read `STATUS` until `fifo_depth` reads 0 (a bounded wait of 4 times t_drain). Hold for 4 times t_drain. Read `INTR_STATE` (CHK-HMAC-FIFOEMPTY, leg notfull). Do not write `CMD.hash_process` before this read.
. Wipe leg. Reset HMAC. Write `CFG` with the drawn digest size and HMAC, write the `KEY` words of a table row with the drawn key length, write `CMD.hash_start`, write the drawn message, write `CMD.hash_process` and poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait). Read DIGEST (CHK-HMAC-MSG). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. Write w to `WIPE_SECRET`. Read the DIGEST words of the digest size (CHK-HMAC-WIPE, digest half).
. Write `CFG` with the same digest size, HMAC and key length, and write no `KEY` word (the one exception to the key rule of step 1). Write `CMD.hash_start`, write a second drawn message, write `CMD.hash_process` and poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait). Read DIGEST (CHK-HMAC-WIPE, key half). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Reset HMAC. Print `RESULT sep_hmac_fifo_stream_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>` when every check passed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HMAC-STATUS
|On every `STATUS` sample `fifo_empty` is 1 if and only if `fifo_depth` is 0, `fifo_full` implies `fifo_empty` is 0, and no sample with `fifo_full` = 0 reports a `fifo_depth` above that of a sample with `fifo_full` = 1 in the same seed (`hmac.adoc` register text of `STATUS`). The depth at full is logged and is not an expected value. A wrong DUT shows a depth that disagrees with the empty or full bit. Anchor: the `STATUS` readback. Control: the stalled leg reads `fifo_full` = 1, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-STATUS PASS seed=<n> phase=<stalled, drain, run or idle> depth=<d> full=<f> empty=<e> idle=<i> OBS-depth_at_full=<D>`.
|CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE
|With the drain stall held and `fifo_full` read 1, a write beat into the message window gets no write response over a hold of 16 times the clean write latency. After the stall is released, the beat completes with OKAY, and CHK-HMAC-MSG shows that no word is lost (`hmac.adoc` register text of `STATUS`: "Data written to the FIFO whilst it is full will cause back-pressure on the interconnect"). A wrong DUT answers the beat at once and drops the word, or never answers. Anchor: the write response timing at the CPU-LSU master, the `STATUS` readback and the stall inject. Control: a beat written with the FIFO not full completes with a response (t_clean_wr), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-BACKPRESSURE PASS seed=<n> mode=<burst, gap or byte> full=1 no_resp_clk=<w> clean_clk=<c> resp_after_release=OKAY`.
|CHK-HMAC-MSG
|The digest equals the software golden of the whole message, so no word is lost or reordered. The golden takes the message bytes in write order, each write in lane order (`endian_swap` = 0). A wrong DUT loses a word under back-pressure or reorders words. Anchor: the DIGEST readback. Control: the cells of the same seed cover every length class, so a dropped final word changes the digest. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-MSG PASS seed=<n> len=<L> mode=<burst, gap or byte> sha=<s> hmac_en=<e> digest==golden`, one line per hash.
|CHK-HMAC-FIFOEMPTY
|`INTR_STATE.fifo_empty` reads 1 in the full leg and 0 in the not-full leg, both before `hash_process` (`hmac.adoc` register text of `INTR_STATE`: the interrupt needs a FIFO that has been full). A wrong DUT raises it with no full FIFO before it, or never raises it. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback. Control for "0 when not full": the full leg of the same seed sets it, and the not-full leg writes fewer words than the logged depth at full. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-FIFOEMPTY PASS seed=<n> leg=<full or notfull> full_before=<w> state=<s> t_drain=<d>`.
|CHK-HMAC-WIPE
|After a write of w to `WIPE_SECRET`, every DIGEST word of the digest size reads w, and a following HMAC hash with no `KEY` write returns the HMAC golden of the second message under a key whose words are all w (`hmac.hjson`, `WIPE_SECRET`: the written value overwrites the secret key and the digest; for SHA-384 and SHA-512 the 32-bit value is duplicated into each 64-bit word). The golden maps w to a DIGEST word and to a key word with the byte order that CHK-HMAC-MSG uses at `digest_swap` = 0 and `key_swap` = 0, and the four different bytes of w make a wrong byte order visible. A wrong DUT that ignores the write keeps the digest of the hash before it and hashes with the table key. Anchor: the DIGEST readback against w and against the software golden. Control: the DIGEST read before the wipe equals the golden of the first message (CHK-HMAC-MSG), and w differs from each of its words. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-WIPE PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> klen=<k> w=0x<w> words=<m> digest_all_w=1 key_half_digest==golden_key_w=1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The depth at full is a logged value and is not an expected value, because the specification does not state it. `STATUS.hmac_idle` is logged (`OBS-HMAC-IDLE seed=<n> phase=<p> idle=<i>`) and not graded during or after a hash: the register text gives only its reset value 1 and the rule that context writes are legal while it is 1. The second round of HMAC mode, and a `fifo_empty` with `hash_process` or `hash_stop` already written, have no stimulus here, so the unmet conditions of the interrupt rule are not claimed. The open owner (`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`) keeps the digest cells of 1 to 16 words. The HMAC key and message order are claimed only through the digest golden. The `WIPE_SECRET` overwrite of the intermediate hash and of the message schedule has no read path other than the digest registers and is not claimed apart from the digest.

'''''

[[sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test]]
===== 1.29 `sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (HMAC)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `reseed = 4`; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: CPU-LSU master readback and write of the HMAC registers (`CFG`, `CMD`, `STATUS`, `DIGEST`, `MSG_LENGTH_LOWER`, `MSG_LENGTH_UPPER`, `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`, `MSG_FIFO`), with write strobes of 4, 2 and 1 bytes (approved); `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`, source 18 on bit 17 (approved); the gated-reset probe of HMAC (approved); `sep_hmac_golden`, a software SHA-2 model. No inject. No PROPOSED item. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the digest size order, the message length (4 to 200 bytes in the swap cells; in the context trips the whole blocks of each part and a final part of 0 to one block) and the message bytes (distinct per message), the write widths of each message (32, 16 and 8 bit; at least one multi-byte write), the part count and the block count of each part (at least one block in each part before the last), the interleave choice and the unrelated message, and which `hmac_done` events are graded. Constraint filters on the software golden: the message before each `sha_en` clear gives a digest with a non-zero word, and the message of each `digest_swap` pair gives a digest with a word that differs from its byte swap. `reseed = 4` samples different lengths, splits and widths. The plan shape (12 swap cells, 15 context trips, one `sha_en` clear per digest size on every seed) is the same on every seed.

*Objective*: With `endian_swap` = 1 each multi-byte value written to `MSG_FIFO` joins the message in big-endian byte order, and a one-byte write is not affected. With `digest_swap` = 1 each DIGEST word is in big-endian byte order, and the register order does not change. `hash_stop` sets `hmac_done` after the current block. DIGEST and MSG_LENGTH are written while the engine is idle. `hash_continue` hashes on from the restored digest and length. Clearing `sha_en` clears the digest. A completed hash sets the `hmac_done` state bit, and with `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` set vector bit 17 (source 18) rises. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/hmac/regs/gen/adoc/hmac.adoc` (`CFG`, `CMD`, `STATUS`, `DIGEST`, `MSG_LENGTH_LOWER`, `MSG_LENGTH_UPPER`, `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map).

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`, which pins one byte-order convention and never uses `hash_stop`, `hash_continue` or a context restore. The done-line check grades a real completion event; the enable gate of the line belongs to the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`. This test grades the swap, context and `sha_en` cells and the rise of the done line. The open owners keep the rest. Cells: the four (`endian_swap`, `digest_swap`) cells for each digest size, with a mix of 32-bit, 16-bit and 8-bit writes; the same message at `digest_swap` 0 and 1; plain SHA-2 context round trips of 2 to 4 parts for each digest size, split at block boundaries, with an optional unrelated hash between parts; the corners of a split after the last byte and a split at the first block boundary; the single-pass run of each trip as its control; the `sha_en` clear after a finished hash; the vector rise at the completion events with `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` set. Functional coverage: `sep_hmac_cfg_op_cg` (`x_swap_cell`, `x_write_width`, `cp_context_round_trip`, `cp_ctx_write_legality`, `cp_sha_en_clear`) and `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_hw_raise` for `hmac_done`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`. Print `PLAN seed=<n>` with the draws. Reset HMAC: write `SW_RESET_N` with bit 3 cleared from the baseline 0x7E, wait for the gated-reset probe of HMAC to show active, write the baseline back, and wait for the first OKAY readback of `STATUS` (each wait bounded; a timeout fails the leaf). Every `CFG` write sets `hmac_en` = 0 and `sha_en` = 1, except in step 17.
. Swap cells. For each digest size, draw a message with distinct bytes and a write plan with a mix of 32-bit, 16-bit and 8-bit writes, with at least one multi-byte write. Across the 12 cells of the seed, every width appears with each `endian_swap` value.
. For each digest size and each `endian_swap` value, run the two `digest_swap` values in the drawn order. Write `CFG`, write `CMD.hash_start`, write the message by the plan to `MSG_FIFO`, write `CMD.hash_process`, and poll `INTR_STATE` until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-SWAP). Read the DIGEST words of the digest size (8, 12 or 16 words) and write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Build the golden of each cell. The message bytes follow the plan: a multi-byte write adds its bytes in little-endian order when `endian_swap` = 0 and in big-endian order when `endian_swap` = 1, and a one-byte write adds its byte as written. Hash the bytes with the software SHA-2 model. DIGEST word n is the big-endian read of digest bytes 4n to 4n+3 when `digest_swap` = 0 (DIGEST_0 holds the first 4 bytes of the digest), and the 32-bit byte swap of that word when `digest_swap` = 1, which puts the bytes of each register value in big-endian order in memory. Compare (CHK-HMAC-SWAP).
. For each digest size and `endian_swap` value, compare every DIGEST word at `digest_swap` = 1 with the 32-bit byte swap of the word at `digest_swap` = 0 (CHK-HMAC-DSWAP-REL). For each digest size, compare the four register sets of the four cells. Any two equal sets log `CTRL-MISSING`.
. Context trips, plain SHA-2 only, with `endian_swap` = 0 and `digest_swap` = 0. Every golden digest of a trip has DIGEST word n as the big-endian read of digest bytes 4n to 4n+3. Run one trip for each digest size and each part count 2 to 4, and the corner trips: for each digest size one trip split after the last byte (an empty final part, with a drawn part count of 2 to 4), and one trip of 2 parts whose first part is one block. A block is 64 bytes for SHA-256 and 128 bytes for SHA-384 and SHA-512. Every part before the last is one or more whole blocks.
. Per trip, run the whole message in one pass first: write `CFG`, `CMD.hash_start`, the message, `CMD.hash_process`, poll until `hmac_done` reads 1. Log t_single, the clocks from the `hash_start` write response to the first read that shows `hmac_done`. Read DIGEST, compare it with the software golden of the whole message, and keep it as the single-pass digest. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Every trip has 2 or more parts. The single-pass digest of step 7 is the control of CHK-HMAC-CTX; a single-pass digest alone is graded by `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test` and not here.
. For a trip of 2 or more parts, write `CFG`, write `CMD.hash_start`, and write part 1 to `MSG_FIFO` except its last write. Read `INTR_STATE` (`hmac_done` reads 0). Write `CMD.hash_stop`, then write the last write of part 1, so that the last block of the part is not complete before the stop. Poll until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait of 3 times t_single; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-STOP). Poll `STATUS` until `hmac_idle` reads 1 (a bounded wait; CHK-HMAC-STOP). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Read DIGEST_0 to DIGEST_15 for every digest size, and `MSG_LENGTH_LOWER` and `MSG_LENGTH_UPPER`. Keep them as the saved context. `hmac.hjson` (`CMD.hash_stop`) names DIGEST_0 to DIGEST_15 and the message length as the context to save, and the SHA-384 state has 16 words although its digest has 12.
. If the trip interleaves, run an unrelated plain SHA-2 hash of a drawn message with the same `CFG`: `CMD.hash_start`, the message, `CMD.hash_process`, poll until `hmac_done` reads 1, write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`. Read DIGEST. It equals the software golden of the unrelated message, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. The leaf logs whether it differs from the saved digest as `OBS-HMAC-CTX-CHANGED` (not graded).
. Poll `STATUS` until `hmac_idle` reads 1. Write the 16 saved DIGEST words and the saved `MSG_LENGTH_LOWER` and `MSG_LENGTH_UPPER`. The engine is idle, so the writes are legal.
. Write `CMD.hash_continue`. For a part that is not the last, write the part to `MSG_FIFO` with its last write after the `CMD.hash_stop` write, wait for `hmac_done` and `hmac_idle` as in step 9, and repeat steps 10 to 12. For the last part, write the part to `MSG_FIFO`, write `CMD.hash_process`, and poll until `hmac_done` reads 1 (a bounded wait). An empty last part writes no data.
. Read DIGEST. Compare it with the single-pass digest and with the software golden of the whole message (CHK-HMAC-CTX). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Done-line events. The leaf grades the first `hmac_done` event of the seed and each later event of steps 3, 7, 9, 11 and 13 with a drawn yes or no. At a graded event, write 1 to `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` and read vector bit 17 as 0 before the command that ends the hash (`hash_process` or `hash_stop`). After the event, read `INTR_STATE` (`hmac_done` reads 1) and wait for vector bit 17 to read 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-DONE-LINE).
. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`; this clear replaces the clear of the step that ran the event. Read `INTR_STATE` (`hmac_done` reads 0) and log vector bit 17 as `OBS-HMAC-DONE-FALL`. Write 0 to `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done`.
. `sha_en` clear, once per digest size. After a finished hash, read DIGEST (it equals the software golden of that hash, and the leaf draws the message of that hash so that the golden has a non-zero word of the digest size). Write `CFG` with `sha_en` = 0 and the other fields unchanged. Read DIGEST until every word of the digest size reads 0 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-HMAC-SHAOFF). Write `CFG` with `sha_en` = 1.
. Print `RESULT sep_hmac_swap_context_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=<k>` when every check passed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HMAC-SWAP
|For each swap cell and digest size the digest equals the software golden built with the byte order that the swap bits select (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CFG`: `endian_swap`, `digest_swap`). A wrong DUT uses the wrong byte order for a multi-byte write, swaps a one-byte write, or does not swap the digest words. Anchor: the DIGEST readback. Control: the four cells of a digest size give four different register sets (the leaf logs the comparison), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-SWAP PASS seed=<n> endian=<e> dswap=<d> sha=<s> len=<L> w32=<a> w16=<b> w8=<c> digest==golden`.
|CHK-HMAC-DSWAP-REL
|For the same message every DIGEST word at `digest_swap` = 1 equals the 32-bit byte swap of the word at `digest_swap` = 0, and the word order is the same (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CFG`: "does not affect the order of the digest output registers"). A wrong DUT reorders the words or swaps part of them. Anchor: the DIGEST readback of both runs. Control: the golden digest has a word that differs from its 32-bit byte swap, because the leaf draws the message so that it has, so the relation is visible. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-DSWAP-REL PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> words=<n> rel=bswap32 mismatches=0`.
|CHK-HMAC-CTX
|A message that is split, stopped, saved, restored and continued returns the digest of the single-pass run, which also equals the software golden of the whole message (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CMD`: `hash_stop` and `hash_continue`; `hmac.hjson`, `CMD.hash_stop`: DIGEST_0 to DIGEST_15 and the message length are the context, and the trip saves and restores all 16 words). An unrelated hash between the parts does not change the result. A wrong DUT loses the saved digest or length, or continues from the wrong state. Anchor: the DIGEST readback. Control: after the unrelated hash DIGEST equals the golden of the unrelated message, so the restore, and not a leftover state, returns the right state; whether that digest differs from the saved digest is logged as `OBS-HMAC-CTX-CHANGED`. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-CTX PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> parts=<k> split=<b1,b2,b3> interleave=<0 or 1> len=<L> digest==single_pass`.
|CHK-HMAC-STOP
|After `hash_stop` the engine sets `hmac_done` and returns to idle (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CMD`, `STATUS`). A wrong DUT never sets `hmac_done`, or stays busy. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` and `STATUS` readback. Control: `hmac_done` reads 0 just before the `hash_stop` write. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-STOP PASS seed=<n> done_after_stop=1 idle=1`.
|CHK-HMAC-SHAOFF
|Writing `sha_en` = 0 after a finished hash makes every DIGEST register of the digest size read 0 (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CFG`: "When the SHA-2 engine is disabled the digest is cleared"). A wrong DUT keeps the digest. Anchor: the DIGEST readback. Control: the DIGEST read before the write equals the golden of the finished hash, which has a non-zero word of the digest size by the draw. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-SHAOFF PASS seed=<n> nonzero_before=1 digest_after=0`.
|CHK-HMAC-DONE-LINE
|At a graded completion event with `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` set, a finished hash sets the `hmac_done` state bit and vector bit 17 (source 18) rises (`interrupts.adoc`, source 18 on bit 17). A wrong DUT sets the state bit and never raises the line, or raises it before the hash ends. Anchor: the `INTR_STATE` readback and `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`. Control: vector bit 17 reads 0 before the command that ends the hash, so the rise comes from the event. Evidence: `CHK-HMAC-DONE-LINE PASS seed=<n> event=<k> enable=1 state=1 vec17_before=0 vec17=1`; `OBS-HMAC-DONE-FALL seed=<n> event=<k> vec17_after_w1c=<v>` is logged and not graded.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This test does not write DIGEST or MSG_LENGTH while the engine is not idle, and does not stop an HMAC-mode hash, because the specification states no rule for either. The test pins the word value of `digest_swap` = 0 as the big-endian read of the digest bytes and of `digest_swap` = 1 as its 32-bit byte swap (`hmac.adoc` register text of `CFG`), and grades no other meaning of the value. It does not grade the MSG_LENGTH value, only its save and restore. The open owner (`sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`) keeps the byte-order convention of its legal cells. The line check grades the rise at a completion event with the enable set. The enable clear, the enable flip and the write-one clear of the line belong to the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`, which grades them for every source of its table.

'''''
[[sep_crypto_delivered_key_select_rand_test]]
===== 1.30 `sep_crypto_delivered_key_select_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (Key delivery)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`), `+esrc_noise_force`, the firmware image and plusargs of the open Key Manager sideload tests, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_delivered_key_select_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_km_mailbox_seq.py` (Key Manager key load and key transfer); `seq_lib/sep_hmac_seq.py` and `env/sep_hmac_golden.py` (HMAC driver and golden); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` and `env/sep_aes_golden.py` (AES driver and golden); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. Probe: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved; vector bit 32 is the Crypto alert). Inject: none. PROPOSED items: none.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the table rows of the delivered HMAC key K_h and the delivered AES key K_a (K_h differs from K_a), the table row of the software decoy key K_d of HMAC (K_d differs from K_h), the table row of the software AES key K_s (K_s differs from K_a in every 32-bit word), the message, the AES key size and mode of the software-key leg, and the order of the HMAC cells. The seed picks table rows and never key bytes. `reseed = 4` samples other rows, messages, AES sizes and modes and cell orders. The 14 keyed HMAC cells, the Key_None leg, the AES legs and the two-engine leg run on every seed.

*Objective*: HMAC uses the delivered key whenever `KEY_CTRL.KEY_VALID` is set and has no select bit. The delivered 256-bit key sits at the most-significant end of the 1024-bit key field, and only `CFG.key_length` = 256 matches it. A supported `key_length` that disagrees with the delivered key gives a repeatable digest and no error; the engine takes key material from the top of the 1024-bit field, so `key_length` 128 takes the top 128 bits of the delivered key. An unset `key_length` (Key_None) with `hmac_en` = 1 raises a configuration error at start. AES ignores the delivered key while `CTRL_SHADOWED.sideload` = 0 and reports no error, and uses it while `sideload` = 1. Two engines that hold two different delivered keys each use their own key. Spec: `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (Key Manager Key Delivery); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/hmac/data/hmac.hjson` (`key_length` and `digest_size` codes).

*Test Coverage*: The 14 legal keyed HMAC cells (digest size x `key_length`; `key_length` 1024 is illegal on SHA2_256) run, and 13 are graded: 2 matched cells (`key_length` 256 on SHA2_384 and SHA2_512) and 11 mismatched cells. The matched SHA2_256 cell is a control. The Key_None start with `hmac_en` = 1 and a valid delivered key. AES with `sideload` = 0 and a software key while a delivered key is valid, with `sideload` = 1 as the control. Two delivered keys transferred back to back, then both engines run. This entry extends `sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test` (the matched length on SHA2_256 against a software decoy key) and `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test` (`sideload` = 1). Only the 13 keyed cells, the Key_None start, the `sideload` = 0 leg and the two-engine leg are graded, and each open test keeps the cells that it owns: the SHA2_256 matched cell and the `sideload` = 1 run appear here as controls only. Functional coverage: `sep_hmac_cfg_op_cg`, `sep_crypto_invalid_encoding_cg`, `sep_aes_cfg_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal entropy source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, then bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN.
. Release the Key Manager, TRNG and AES reset bits of `SW_RESET_N`. Hold the OTBN, KMAC and ABR bits at 0 and the HMAC bit at 0 until step 4. Log the baseline value.
. Draw the plan from the seed (K_h, K_a, K_d, K_s, message, AES size and mode, HMAC cell order) and log it (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`).
. Boot the Key Manager and load K_h and K_a into two key handles, with the firmware image and the command sequence of the open sideload tests. Then release the HMAC reset bit.
. Transfer K_h to HMAC and then K_a to AES, back to back, with no engine access between the two transfers.
. Write K_d to the 32 HMAC `KEY` registers (non-zero, distinct words). Clear `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` and `hmac_done`. Write `CFG` with `hmac_en` = 1, `sha_en` = 1, SHA2_256, `key_length` = 256 and the byte-order bits of the open HMAC sideload test. Write `CMD.hash_start`, write the message to the message FIFO window, write `CMD.hash_process` and wait for `hmac_done` in a bounded wait (a timeout fails the check). Read DIGEST. This sequence is the HMAC run of the later steps. Keep this digest as H_two.
. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 of AES in a bounded wait. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `sideload` = 1, AES_256, ECB, automatic mode. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 again. Write the four DATA_IN words of one block and wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` in a bounded wait. Read the four DATA_OUT words. Keep them as A_two.
. For each of SHA2_384, SHA2_512 and SHA2_256 (the control), run the HMAC run with `key_length` = 256 and read DIGEST.
. For each of the 11 mismatched cells (SHA2_256 with `key_length` 128, 384 and 512; SHA2_384 and SHA2_512 each with `key_length` 128, 384, 512 and 1024), in the drawn order, do the HMAC run twice. Error injection: point `HMAC.CFG.key_length`; kind a legal length for another key size; expected response a repeatable digest and no `hmac_err`. After each run read `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` and DIGEST. For `key_length` 128 compare DIGEST with the HMAC golden of the top 128 bits of K_h. For the other lengths log the comparison of DIGEST with the matched digest of the same digest size from step 8 and with the decoy golden.
. Wait for `STATUS.hmac_idle` = 1 in a bounded wait. Write K_d, clear `INTR_STATE.hmac_err`, and write `CFG` with `hmac_en` = 1, `sha_en` = 1 (it stays 1, so the digest is not cleared), a drawn digest size, `key_length` = Key_None and the byte-order bits of the HMAC run. Then read DIGEST as d_prev, after the `CFG` write and before the start, so that d_prev and the DIGEST read after the start use the same `digest_size` (the register text does not state the readback after a change of `digest_size`). Write `CMD.hash_start`. Error injection: point `HMAC.CFG.key_length`; kind Key_None; expected response a configuration error and no digest. Poll `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` in a bounded wait, then read DIGEST. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_err`.
. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 of AES. Sample vector bit 32 on every clock from this step to the end of step 12. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `sideload` = 0 and the drawn key size and mode (AES_ENC, automatic mode). The software key write of the next step lands only after this write, because a `sideload` = 1 value in the register drops software key writes.
. Write all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1 of K_s and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. For CBC and CTR write the IV from the table and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the four DATA_IN words of one block, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words. Read `STATUS` (bits `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` and `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT`).
. Control: write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `sideload` = 1, AES_256 and ECB, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write the same block and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. Control: sample vector bit 32 on every clock from before the `ALERT_TEST` write is issued, write 1 to one drawn `ALERT_TEST` bit of AES, and keep sampling to the end of a bounded window after the write response. The specification does not order the pulse against the write response, so a high clock anywhere from the issue of the write to the end of the window counts (`ctrl_alert_cycles`). No high clock logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf.
. Compare every value with its golden and log the checks, then log `RESULT sep_crypto_delivered_key_select_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=5`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-MATCH
|With `key_length` = 256 on SHA2_384 and on SHA2_512 the DIGEST equals the HMAC golden of the delivered key K_h, although K_d fills the software `KEY` registers (`hmac.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: the delivered key replaces the software key whenever `KEY_CTRL.KEY_VALID` is set, and `key_length` 256 matches it). A DUT that uses the software key gives the decoy digest and fails. Anchor: DIGEST readback against `env/sep_hmac_golden.py`. Control: the SHA2_256 cell with `key_length` 256 equals the same golden, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-MATCH PASS seed=<n> sha=<384\|512> keylen=256 digest==golden_kh` for each of the two sizes, and `CTRL-KEYSEL-256 seed=<n> sha=256 keylen=256 digest==golden_kh`.
|CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-MISMATCH
|For each of the 11 mismatched cells the two runs give equal digests and `hmac_err` stays 0. For the three cells with `key_length` 128 (SHA2_256, SHA2_384 and SHA2_512) the digest equals the HMAC golden of the top 128 bits of K_h (`hmac.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: a supported length that disagrees gives a wrong but repeatable digest and no error, and the engine takes key material from the top of the 1024-bit field, where the delivered key is most-significant-bit justified; `crypto.adoc`, Key Manager Key Delivery table). The longer lengths mix the padding below the delivered key into the key, and the specification does not state the padding value, so their digest has no stated value: the leaf logs its comparison with the matched digest and with the decoy golden as `OBS-KEYSEL-MISMATCH` and grades no value. A DUT that uses the software key, or that ignores `CFG.key_length` and uses the full delivered key, fails the golden of a 128 cell; a DUT that raises an error sets `hmac_err`; a DUT that gives two different digests fails the repeat. Control: CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-NONE of the same seed observes `hmac_err` = 1, so the bit can be seen to rise; a leaf in which that control does not rise logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Anchor: DIGEST and `INTR_STATE` readback, and the golden of the top 128 bits of K_h; the `hmac_done` of each run is the positive evidence that the run completed. Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-MISMATCH PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> keylen=<k> err=0 run1==run2=1 digest==golden_kh128=<1 or na>` for each of the 11 cells, `na` for the longer lengths, and `OBS-KEYSEL-MISMATCH seed=<n> sha=<s> keylen=<k> eq_matched=<b> eq_decoy=<b>` for the longer lengths.
|CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-NONE
|With `hmac_en` = 1, Key_None and a valid delivered key, `CMD.hash_start` sets `INTR_STATE.hmac_err` and leaves DIGEST at d_prev (`hmac.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: an unset length raises a configuration error when hashing starts; `hmac.hjson` `key_length`: the start is blocked). A DUT that hashes with the delivered key shows no error and a new digest. The `CFG` write keeps `sha_en` = 1 and HMAC is idle before it, so the digest is not cleared and the start is blocked only by Key_None. Anchor: `INTR_STATE` and DIGEST readback; d_prev is the DIGEST read after the `CFG` write and just before the start, under the same `CFG` as the read after the start. Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-HMAC-NONE PASS seed=<n> sha=<s> err=1 digest_unchanged=1`.
|CHK-KEYSEL-AES-SW
|With `sideload` = 0 and a valid delivered key the ciphertext equals the golden of K_s. No pulse occurs on vector bit 32 during the leg, and `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` and `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` read 0 (`aes.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: the delivered key has no effect and no error is reported). K_s differs from K_a in every 32-bit word, so a DUT that takes any part of K_a gives a different ciphertext. For AES_256 the golden of K_s also differs from the golden of K_a (both come from fixed table rows, and the leaf checks the two goldens before the run); for a shorter key the part of K_a that the engine takes is not stated, so that comparison is logged as `na`. A DUT that uses the delivered key, or that signals an alert, fails. Anchor: DATA_OUT, `STATUS` and the vector probe. Controls: the `ALERT_TEST` write of the same seed pulses bit 32 (`ctrl_alert_cycles` above 0), and the `sideload` = 1 run equals the golden of K_a; either control missing logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-AES-SW PASS seed=<n> keylen=<k> mode=<m> ct==golden_ks=1 ct!=golden_ka=<1\|na> alert_cycles=0 status_err=0 ctrl_alert_cycles=<c> ctrl_sideload_ct==golden_ka=1`.
|CHK-KEYSEL-TWO
|After K_h is transferred to HMAC and K_a to AES back to back, the HMAC digest equals the golden of K_h and the AES ciphertext (`sideload` = 1, AES_256) equals the golden of K_a (`crypto.adoc`, Key Manager Key Delivery). A DUT that sends one key to both engines, or that lets the second transfer overwrite the first, gives one wrong result. Anchor: DIGEST and DATA_OUT readback against the goldens (H_two and A_two). Evidence: `CHK-KEYSEL-TWO PASS seed=<n> hmac==golden_kh=1 aes==golden_ka=1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade the value of a mismatched-length digest above 128 bits, the part of a delivered key that a 128-bit or 192-bit AES key takes, the behaviour when the delivered-key valid qualifier drops during an operation, or the delivered-key outcome of KMAC, OTBN and ABR. `sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test` owns the matched length on SHA2_256 and `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test` owns `sideload` = 1; this entry reads those two cells as controls and the open tests keep them.

'''''

[[sep_aes_encoding_inject_rand_test]]
===== 1.31 `sep_aes_encoding_inject_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (AES)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_aes_encoding_inject_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` and `env/sep_aes_golden.py` (AES driver and golden); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. Probes: none beyond CPU-LSU readback of `STATUS` and `DATA_OUT` (approved). Inject: none beyond the invalid register writes of the procedure. PROPOSED items: none.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the key table row and the plaintext, the multi-hot value of each field (KEY_LEN from 3'b011, 3'b101, 3'b110 and 3'b111; OPERATION 2'b11; MODE a value with two to five bits set, never 6'b11_1111 because that is the code of AES_NONE itself) and the order of the six cells. `reseed = 4` samples other keys, plaintexts, multi-hot values and cell orders. The six cells {KEY_LEN, OPERATION, MODE} x {all-zero, multi-hot} run on every seed.

*Objective*: An invalid one-hot value written to `CTRL_SHADOWED` maps to a safe value: KEY_LEN (several bits, or 3'b000) to AES_256, OPERATION (several bits, or 2'b00) to AES_ENC, MODE (several bits, or 6'b00_0000) to AES_NONE. A `TRIGGER.START` does not start the unit when MODE is AES_NONE. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_SHADOWED`, `TRIGGER`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson` (the codes of `KEY_LEN`, `OPERATION` and `MODE`).

*Test Coverage*: Invalid values of KEY_LEN, OPERATION and MODE, each in an all-zero cell and a multi-hot cell, and each against a legal neighbour cell in the same trial. The suppressed start with MODE = AES_NONE in manual mode. This entry extends `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`, which writes legal encodings only: only the invalid-encoding cells are graded, and the open test keeps every legal cell. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_invalid_encoding_cg`, `sep_aes_op_status_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal entropy source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, then bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN.
. Release the AES reset bit of `SW_RESET_N`. Hold the HMAC, KMAC, OTBN, ABR and Key Manager bits at 0. Log the baseline value.
. Draw the plan from the seed (key K as two shares from the table, plaintext P, cell order, multi-hot values) and log it (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`).
. Pulse the AES reset bit (0 then 1) and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait.
. Calibration: write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC and `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 1. Write all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1 of K and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the four DATA_IN words of P and write `TRIGGER.START`. Poll `STATUS` back to back until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait. Log the clocks from the START write response to that sample as t_blk. Read the four DATA_OUT words. Compare the block with the golden; a mismatch logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf. The golden gives C = E_K(P) for the OPERATION cells.
. For each of the six cells in the drawn order, start a trial: pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Steps 7 to 14 give the actions of a trial for its cell type (KEY_LEN, OPERATION or MODE). Every key load in these steps follows the order: write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice, write the key words, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR`, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. KEY_LEN cell. Error injection: point `CTRL_SHADOWED.KEY_LEN`; kind 3'b000 or one drawn multi-hot value; expected response the AES-256 behaviour. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with that KEY_LEN, ECB, AES_ENC and automatic mode (both writes answer OKAY). Read `CTRL_SHADOWED` and log the field (`OBS-AES-ENC-RB`). Write the eight key words of K, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. KEY_LEN cell. Write the four DATA_IN words of P, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. KEY_LEN control. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with KEY_LEN = AES_128 and the other fields as before. Write the key words of K as the driver writes them for a 128-bit key, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write P, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. OPERATION cell. Error injection: point `CTRL_SHADOWED.OPERATION`; kind 2'b00 or 2'b11; expected response the AES_ENC behaviour. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with that OPERATION, ECB, AES_256 and automatic mode. Read `CTRL_SHADOWED` and log the field. Write the eight key words of K, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the four DATA_IN words of C, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. OPERATION control. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with OPERATION = AES_DEC, then write the eight key words of K (a decryption needs the key written after the control register, so that the decryption key is generated), wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write C, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. MODE cell. Error injection: point `CTRL_SHADOWED.MODE`; kind 6'b00_0000 or one drawn multi-hot value; expected response AES_NONE, which does not start. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with that MODE, AES_256, AES_ENC and `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 1. Read `CTRL_SHADOWED` and log the field. Write the eight key words of K, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Read the four DATA_OUT words as the base words.
. MODE cell. Write the four DATA_IN words of P and write `TRIGGER.START`. Poll `STATUS` back to back for a window of 20 x t_blk clocks (a test parameter). Then read the four DATA_OUT words.
. MODE control. Do a key load in the order of step 6 with MODE = ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC, `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 1 and the eight key words of K (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`: a Control Register write starts a new message, and software gives a new key afterwards). Then write the four DATA_IN words of P and write `TRIGGER.START`. Poll `STATUS` back to back until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait (a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). Log the clocks as `ctrl_valid_clk`, then read the four DATA_OUT words.
. Compare every value with its golden, log the checks and log `RESULT sep_aes_encoding_inject_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=3`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-AES-INV-KEYLEN
|After an invalid KEY_LEN (3'b000 or several bits) the ciphertext of P equals the AES-256 golden of K and differs from the AES-128 golden (`aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_SHADOWED.KEY_LEN`: invalid values map to AES_256). The two goldens differ: the leaf computes both before the run and draws the plaintext again if they are equal, so the inequality follows from the equality with the AES-256 golden. A DUT that maps the value to AES_128 or AES_192 gives the wrong ciphertext. Anchor: DATA_OUT against `env/sep_aes_golden.py`. Control: KEY_LEN = AES_128 in the same trial gives the AES-128 ciphertext, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-INV-KEYLEN PASS seed=<n> written=0b<v> ct==golden256=1 ct!=golden128=1 ctrl_ct==golden128=1`.
|CHK-AES-INV-OP
|After an invalid OPERATION (2'b00 or 2'b11) the result for the input C is the encryption of C, not its decryption, which is P (`aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_SHADOWED.OPERATION`: invalid values map to AES_ENC). The encryption of C differs from P: the leaf computes both goldens before the run and draws the plaintext again if they are equal. A DUT that maps the value to AES_DEC returns P. Anchor: DATA_OUT against the encrypt and decrypt goldens. Control: OPERATION = AES_DEC in the same trial returns P, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-INV-OP PASS seed=<n> written=0b<v> result==enc_golden=1 result!=dec_golden=1 ctrl_result==dec_golden=1`.
|CHK-AES-INV-MODE
|With an invalid MODE in manual mode a `TRIGGER.START` leaves `OUTPUT_VALID` at 0 at every sample of a window of 20 clean block times, and DATA_OUT keeps its base words (`aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_SHADOWED.MODE`: invalid values map to AES_NONE; `TRIGGER.START` is cleared if MODE = AES_NONE). A DUT that maps the value to a real mode starts, sets `OUTPUT_VALID` and changes DATA_OUT. Anchor: `STATUS` and DATA_OUT readback. Control: MODE = ECB in the same trial, with a new key load, the same input and a new START, sets `OUTPUT_VALID` in a bounded wait, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails; this shows that the input and the START path work. The readback of the invalid MODE is logged and not graded. Evidence: `CHK-AES-INV-MODE PASS seed=<n> written=0b<v> output_valid=0 data_out_unchanged=1 window_clk=<w> ctrl_valid_clk=<t>` and `OBS-AES-ENC-RB seed=<n> field=<f> written=0x<w> read=0x<r>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade the readback value of an invalid encoding (the register text does not say whether it shows the written or the mapped value), the invalid PRNG_RESEED_RATE values (owned by `sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test`), the AES_192 and GCM encodings, or any legal cell. `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` owns the legal cells and keeps them.

'''''

[[sep_aes_stream_status_rand_test]]
===== 1.32 `sep_aes_stream_status_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (AES)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, HMAC out of reset and idle, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_aes_stream_status_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` and `env/sep_aes_golden.py` (AES driver and streaming golden); `seq_lib/sep_hmac_seq.py` (the HMAC control operation); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. Probe: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved; all 43 vector bits). Inject: none. PROPOSED items: none.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the order of the three mode streams (ECB, CBC and CTR, all three on every seed), and for each stream the key size, the operation (encrypt or decrypt for ECB and CBC; encrypt for CTR), the block count N (34 to 100), the input gap and the output gap of every block (0 to 40 clocks each), the indexes of the stall pairs and of the control pairs (1 to 2 of each per stream; each index i is drawn from 1 to N - 2, and the pairs {i, i + 1} do not overlap, so no block belongs to two pairs and block 0 belongs to none), the stall delay G of each pair (2 to 20 times t_blk), and the wait of the manual leg (2 to 8 times t_blk). The key, IV and CTR start counter come from the table; the CTR start counter keeps its low 32 bits at or below 2^32 minus N, so the counter does not wrap. `reseed = 4` samples other mode orders, sizes, counts, gaps, indexes and delays. The legs (the three mode streams, stall pairs with control pairs, automatic START, manual overwrite with its control, IDLE samples, no-interrupt check) run on every seed.

*Objective*: In automatic mode (`MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0) the unit starts when new input arrives and stalls in the last cycle while the previous output is unread (`STATUS.STALL` = 1, `IDLE` = 0). A `TRIGGER.START` write in automatic mode does not start the unit. In manual mode the unit starts only on `TRIGGER.START` and overwrites unread output, which sets `STATUS.OUTPUT_LOST` until the Control Register is rewritten. `IDLE` is 0 while a block runs. AES has no interrupt. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_SHADOWED`, `TRIGGER`, `STATUS`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` (OCAH Modifications: AES exports `idle_o` and no interrupt); `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (PIC source map).

*Test Coverage*: An ECB, a CBC and a CTR stream on every seed, each of 34 to 100 blocks with drawn input and output gaps. Stall pairs and control pairs inside each stream. A START write in automatic mode with no input. A manual-mode overwrite of unread output, and the same sequence with the output read between the blocks. `IDLE` samples over block runs. The interrupt vector over the whole leaf, with an HMAC operation as the control. This entry extends `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`, which runs 2 blocks per cell in automatic mode and reads `STATUS` for alert bits only: only the cells above are graded, and the open test keeps its cells. Functional coverage: `sep_aes_op_status_cg`, `sep_crypto_intr_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal entropy source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, then bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN.
. Release the AES and HMAC reset bits of `SW_RESET_N`. Hold the KMAC, OTBN, ABR and Key Manager bits at 0. Log the baseline value.
. Draw the plan from the seed and log it (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`).
. Read the HMAC `STATUS` and require `hmac_idle` = 1. Write HMAC `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` = 1.
. Write 0 to `INTR_ENABLE` of ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Read all 43 vector bits of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` and log them as S0. Sample these bits on every clock from this step until the sampling stops in step 24. Vector bits 36 and 37 (source 37, entropy pool low, and source 38, entropy pool fill stall) are level sources with no enable that follow the pool occupancy and the EDN fill, which still change after the entropy bring-up, so the leaf logs their changes (`OBS-POOL-VEC`) and excludes them from the compare.
. Steps 6 to 13 run once for each of the three mode streams, in the drawn order, with the draws of that stream. Pulse the AES reset bit (0 then 1) and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait.
. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0 and the mode of the stream, key size and operation. Write all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1 of the drawn key and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. For CBC and CTR write the IV and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. Block 0: write the four DATA_IN words with no input gap. Poll `STATUS` back to back until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait, and keep every sample. Read the four DATA_OUT words at once. Log the clocks from the last DATA_IN write response to the sample with `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 as t_blk.
. For every other block that is not part of a stall pair or a control pair, in order: wait the drawn input gap, write the four DATA_IN words, poll `STATUS` back to back until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait (keep every sample), wait the drawn output gap and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. Stall pair at each drawn index j. After block j sets `OUTPUT_VALID`, do not read it. Write the four DATA_IN words of block j + 1, wait G clocks and read `STATUS`. Keep the `STALL` and `IDLE` bits of that sample.
. Stall pair. Read the four DATA_OUT words of block j. Poll `STATUS` until `STALL` = 0 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait. Read the four DATA_OUT words of block j + 1.
. Control pair at each drawn index c. After block c sets `OUTPUT_VALID`, read its four DATA_OUT words at once. Write the four DATA_IN words of block c + 1, wait the same G clocks and read `STATUS`. Keep the `STALL` bit. Poll until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words of block c + 1.
. Compare the N outputs of the stream with the streaming golden (CBC chains and CTR advances by one per block over all N blocks).
. Pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0 and ECB. Write the eight key words and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Read `STATUS`. Require `IDLE` = 1 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0 as the base sample (another value logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf).
. Write `TRIGGER.START` = 1 with no DATA_IN word written. Poll `STATUS` back to back for a window of 20 x t_blk clocks (a test parameter) and keep every sample.
. Control: write the four DATA_IN words of one block. Poll `STATUS` until `IDLE` = 0 in a bounded wait, then until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait (a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). Read the four DATA_OUT words.
. Control: poll `STATUS` until `IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait (a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf).
. Manual leg. Pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 1 and ECB. Write the eight key words and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. Manual leg. Write the four DATA_IN words of block 1 and write `TRIGGER.START`. Poll `STATUS` back to back until `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait, and keep every sample.
. Manual leg. Without reading DATA_OUT, write the four DATA_IN words of block 2 and write `TRIGGER.START`. Wait the drawn multiple of t_blk, then poll `STATUS` until `IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait.
. Manual leg. Read `STATUS` and keep `OUTPUT_LOST`. Read the four DATA_OUT words and keep them.
. Manual leg. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with the same value as before. Read `STATUS` and keep `OUTPUT_LOST`.
. Control trial: repeat steps 18 and 19. Read all four DATA_OUT words of block 1. Write the four DATA_IN words of block 2 and write `TRIGGER.START`, wait the same time and poll `STATUS` until `IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait. Read `STATUS` and keep `OUTPUT_LOST`. Read the four DATA_OUT words and keep them.
. Stop the vector sampling. Control: write HMAC `CFG` with `hmac_en` = 0, `sha_en` = 1 and SHA2_256, write `CMD.hash_start`, write the message for one hash to the message FIFO window and write `CMD.hash_process`. Wait for vector bit 17 to rise in a bounded wait (a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.hmac_done`.
. Compare every value with its golden, log the checks and log `RESULT sep_aes_stream_status_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=6`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-AES-STREAM
|Every block of the stream equals the streaming golden: CBC chains and CTR advances across all N blocks, with the drawn gaps (`aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_SHADOWED.MANUAL_OPERATION`: in automatic mode the unit starts on new input). A DUT that drops, repeats or reorders a block, or that loses the chain across a stall, gives a mismatch. Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against the streaming golden of `env/sep_aes_golden.py`. Evidence: `CHK-AES-STREAM PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> keylen=<k> op=<o> blocks=<N> mismatches=0 max_in_gap=<a> max_out_gap=<b>`.
|CHK-AES-STALL
|At each stall pair, G clocks after the next input is written while the previous output is unread, `STATUS.STALL` is 1 and `IDLE` is 0. After the previous output is read, `STALL` falls and the next block equals its golden (`aes.adoc` register text, `STATUS.STALL` and `STATUS.IDLE`: the unit is not idle while it is stalled). A DUT that overwrites the unread output without a stall, or that reports `IDLE` while stalled, fails. Anchor: `STATUS` and DATA_OUT readback. Control: at each control pair the previous output is read first, and the sample at the same G reads `STALL` = 0; a stall that shows in both pairs would be a stuck bit. Evidence: `CHK-AES-STALL PASS seed=<n> block=<j> gap_clk=<G> stall=1 idle=0 then_stall=0 ct==golden ctrl_stall=0`.
|CHK-AES-START-AUTO
|In automatic mode a `TRIGGER.START` write with no input leaves `IDLE` = 1 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0 at every sample of a window of 20 clean block times (`aes.adoc` register text, `TRIGGER.START`: the trigger is cleared if `MANUAL_OPERATION` = 0). A DUT that starts on the trigger shows `IDLE` = 0 or `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1. The base sample before the write shows `IDLE` = 1 and `OUTPUT_VALID` = 0, so the window starts from a unit that is able to show a change. Anchor: `STATUS` readback. Control: four DATA_IN words in the same seed start a block (`IDLE` falls) and the block completes with the golden ciphertext, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-START-AUTO PASS seed=<n> idle_samples=<k> idle=1 output_valid=0 ctrl_data_in_idle=0`.
|CHK-AES-LOST
|In manual mode a second block started without a read of the first output sets `OUTPUT_LOST`, DATA_OUT holds the result of block 2, and a rewrite of `CTRL_SHADOWED` clears `OUTPUT_LOST` (`aes.adoc` register text, `MANUAL_OPERATION` and `STATUS.OUTPUT_LOST`). A DUT that keeps the first output, or that never sets or never clears the bit, fails. Anchor: `STATUS` and DATA_OUT readback. Control: with all four output words read between the blocks, `OUTPUT_LOST` stays 0 and DATA_OUT holds the result of block 2. Evidence: `CHK-AES-LOST PASS seed=<n> output_lost=1 data_out==block2 rewrite_clears=1 ctrl_reads_between=4 ctrl_output_lost=0`.
|CHK-AES-IDLE
|After the last DATA_IN write of a stream block, and after the `TRIGGER.START` of manual block 1, `IDLE` reads 0 at some sample (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check) and at every later sample until the sample that shows `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 (`aes.adoc` register text, `STATUS.IDLE`: the flag is 0 while a block runs). A DUT that reports idle during a run fails; a DUT that never drops `IDLE` times out. Samples before the first `IDLE` = 0 and the sample that shows `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 are not graded. The reset value of `IDLE` is not graded. Anchor: `STATUS` readback. Control: after the last output is read and the unit is quiet, `IDLE` reads 1 in a bounded wait, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-IDLE PASS seed=<n> idle_busy_samples=<k> idle_busy=0 ctrl_idle_after_quiet=1`.
|CHK-AES-NOIRQ
|The 41 vector bits of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` other than bits 36 and 37 keep the value S0 on every clock from before the first AES write to the end of the manual leg (`hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: AES exports no interrupt; `interrupts.adoc`: the vector has no AES source, and an AES alert or bridge fault would reach the CPU on source 33, bit 32, or source 43, bit 42). The leg enables the interrupt of no block except HMAC and starts no HMAC operation before the sampling stops, so no compared bit has a stated reason to change. Bits 36 and 37 are the entropy pool low and fill-stall sources (`interrupts.adoc`, sources 37 and 38; `hw/sys/sep/doc/entropy_pool.adoc`): they have no enable and follow the pool occupancy and the EDN fill, so a healthy design can change them, and the leaf logs them and does not grade them. A DUT that raises an interrupt or an alert on a busy, stall or lost-output event changes a bit. Anchor: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` sampled on every clock. Control: with HMAC `INTR_ENABLE.hmac_done` set, one HMAC operation at the end raises vector bit 17 (source 18), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails; HMAC is out of reset and idle for the whole leaf. Evidence: `CHK-AES-NOIRQ PASS seed=<n> vector_bits=41 vector_changes=0 ctrl_hmac_done_seen=1` and `OBS-POOL-VEC seed=<n> bit36_changes=<a> bit37_changes=<b>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade the reset value of `STATUS.IDLE`, the fall of `OUTPUT_VALID` on a partial read of DATA_OUT (no fall condition is stated), a DATA_OUT read before `OUTPUT_VALID`, a CTR decrypt cell, or the CFB, OFB and GCM modes. `STATUS.INPUT_READY` is not graded (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` owns the 2-block cells of the open test and keeps them.

'''''

[[sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test]]
===== 1.33 `sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (AES)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test`
