*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` and `env/sep_aes_golden.py` (AES driver and golden); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. Probe: `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` (approved; the AES client of EDN). Inject: none beyond the invalid register writes of the procedure. PROPOSED items: none.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the multi-hot invalid value (one of 3'b011, 3'b101, 3'b110 and 3'b111), the order of the five rate runs (PER_1, PER_64, PER_8K, the all-zero invalid value 3'b000 and the drawn multi-hot value), the block count N (129 to 300, one value per seed), the order of the two clear trials, the rate of the trigger leg (one of the three legal rates), the key table row and the plaintexts. `reseed = 4` samples other multi-hot values, counts, orders, rates and data. The three legal rate runs, the all-zero run, one multi-hot run, the trigger leg and both clear trials run on every seed. The multi-hot value is a seed draw and has no bin of its own: the coverage class is multi-hot.

*Objective*: `CTRL_SHADOWED.PRNG_RESEED_RATE` (one-hot; PER_1 at reset, PER_64, PER_8K) sets how often the masking PRNG reseeds from EDN, and an invalid value maps to PER_1. A reseed request comes from the rate, from `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` = 1, or from a new key while `KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` is at its reset value 1. A `TRIGGER` write with `PRNG_RESEED` = 0 keeps the current states of the PRNGs. `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and `TRIGGER.KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` clear the named registers with pseudo-random data; the specification states no value after a clear and no status bit that a clear sets or clears. After `KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` the engine takes a new key, IV and plaintext and encrypts them. Each block is the AES result of its key and plaintext at every rate (`hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc`: an AES cipher with the stated key sizes and modes). Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_SHADOWED`, `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, `TRIGGER`, `STATUS`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson` (the rate codes and their order; `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED`).

*Test Coverage*: The rates PER_1, PER_64 and PER_8K, the all-zero invalid value and one drawn multi-hot invalid value, each over N blocks (N of 129 or more, so PER_64 reseeds at least twice). The reseed trigger on an idle AES: a `TRIGGER` write with `PRNG_RESEED` = 0 and a write-and-acknowledge control. Both clear triggers while idle, each followed by a reload and an encryption. This entry extends `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`, which writes `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` and grades the request (`CHK-EDN-PEND-AES`) and the acknowledge (`CHK-EDN-SIBLING`), and `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`, which writes the same trigger and grades the acknowledge (`CHK-NO-STARVE`). Here the write-and-acknowledge is a control. Only the `PRNG_RESEED` = 0 window, the rate order, the invalid-value mapping and the encryption after the key clear and a reload are graded; the values after both clear triggers are logged, and the open tests keep the request and the acknowledge cells. `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` keeps the grade of the AES beats against the stream. Functional coverage: `sep_aes_cfg_cg`, `sep_crypto_invalid_encoding_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal entropy source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, then bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN.
. Release the AES reset bit of `SW_RESET_N`. Hold the HMAC, KMAC, OTBN, ABR and Key Manager bits at 0. Log the baseline value.
. Draw the plan from the seed and log it (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`). `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` stays at its reset value for the whole leaf.
. For each of the five rate runs in the drawn order, pulse the AES reset bit (0 then 1) and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait.
. Rate run. For an invalid value the write is an error injection: point `CTRL_SHADOWED.PRNG_RESEED_RATE`; kind 3'b000 or several bits set; expected response the PER_1 behaviour. Write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice with the run's rate value, ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC and automatic mode.
. Rate run. Start the counter of rises on `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]`, then write all eight words of KEY_SHARE0 and KEY_SHARE1 of the key. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 again. These actions are the same in all five runs.
. Rate run. For each of the N blocks write the four DATA_IN words, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait, read the four DATA_OUT words and compare them with the golden.
. Rate run. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait, stop the counter and log its value. In the first run also log the clocks from the last DATA_IN write response of block 0 to `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 as t_blk.
. Compare the five counts: count(PER_1) > count(PER_64) > count(PER_8K), and each of the two invalid values gives a count above count(PER_64).
. Trigger leg. Pulse the AES reset bit, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice (ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC, automatic, the drawn legal rate), write the eight key words and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 again.
. Trigger leg. Write `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` = 1 and log the clocks from the write response to the first rise of `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` as t_r (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check). Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. Trigger leg. Write `TRIGGER` with every field 0, so that `PRNG_RESEED` = 0, and count the rises of `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` over a window of 4 x t_r clocks (a test parameter).
. Trigger leg, control. Write `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` = 1 again and count the rises of `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` over a window of 4 x t_r clocks.
. Clear trial for `DATA_OUT_CLEAR` (in the drawn order). Pulse the AES reset bit, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice (ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC, automatic, PER_1), write the eight key words and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. Clear trial for `DATA_OUT_CLEAR`. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 (the same seed clear as in the rate runs). Write one block P, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait, read the four DATA_OUT words twice and keep them as O0. Wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1.
. Clear trial for `DATA_OUT_CLEAR`. Write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` = 1, wait 4 x t_blk clocks (a test parameter), wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. Clear trial for `KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` (in the drawn order). Pulse the AES reset bit, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `CTRL_SHADOWED` twice (ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC, automatic, PER_1), write the eight key words, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write the four IV words from the table and read the four IV words.
. Clear trial for `KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR`. Write `TRIGGER.KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` = 1, wait 4 x t_blk clocks (a test parameter), wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four IV words.
. Clear trial for `KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR`. Write the eight key words again and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the four IV words again, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the four DATA_IN words of P, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words.
. Compare every value with its golden, log the checks, log `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT reason=DATA_OUT value after clear not stated` and log `RESULT sep_aes_reseed_trigger_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=5`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-AES-RESEED-RATE
|Over N blocks (one N per seed) the number of rises of `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` satisfies count(PER_1) > count(PER_64) > count(PER_8K) (`aes.hjson`, `PRNG_RESEED_RATE`: PER_1 reseeds once per block, PER_64 about once per 64 blocks, PER_8K about once per 8192 blocks). Every run has the same prefix, which includes the reseed that the first key write causes, so only the rate differs. A DUT that ignores the rate, or that swaps two rates, breaks the order. The number of acknowledges per reseed is not stated and is not graded; only the order is. Anchor: the EDN acknowledge probe `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]`. Evidence: `CHK-AES-RESEED-RATE PASS seed=<n> n=<N> c_per1=<a> c_per64=<b> c_per8k=<c> order=a>b>c`.
|CHK-AES-RESEED-INVALID
|For each of the two invalid rate values (3'b000 and the drawn multi-hot value) the count exceeds count(PER_64) (`aes.adoc` register text, `PRNG_RESEED_RATE`: invalid values map to PER_1). A DUT that maps an invalid value to PER_64 or PER_8K gives a count at or below count(PER_64). Anchor: `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]`. Control: the PER_1 and PER_64 runs of the same seed. Evidence: `CHK-AES-RESEED-INVALID PASS seed=<n> value=0b<v> c=<c> c_per64=<b>` for each of the two values.
|CHK-AES-RESEED-TRIG
|On an idle AES with EDN enabled, after a `TRIGGER` write with `PRNG_RESEED` = 0, no rise of `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]` occurs in a window of 4 x t_r clocks (`aes.hjson`, `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED`: 0 keeps the current states). A DUT that reseeds on any `TRIGGER` write shows a rise. The window starts after `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, so a reseed that is still running is not counted. Anchor: `crypto_edn_ack_o[0]`. Control: a `TRIGGER.PRNG_RESEED` write in the same seed is acknowledged within a window of the same length, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails; this shows that the window is long enough to see an acknowledge and that the probe sees a rise. Evidence: `CHK-AES-RESEED-TRIG PASS seed=<n> window_clk=<w> acks_after_zero_write=0 ctrl_acks_after_write=<k> t_r_clk=<t>`.
|CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT
|Blocked: the specification does not state a deterministic value of DATA_OUT after `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR` (`aes.adoc` register text: the registers are cleared with pseudo-random data), or a status bit that the clear sets or clears (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked-legs,Blocked legs of tested features>>). The leaf logs `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT reason=DATA_OUT value after clear not stated` and `OBS-AES-CLEAR-OUT seed=<n> before=0x<b> after=0x<a> ctrl_reads_equal=<e>`, does not log `CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT PASS`, and does not count the check. Each rate run of the same seed writes `DATA_OUT_CLEAR` before its first block, so CHK-AES-RATE-CT grades that the engine encrypts after the clear.
|CHK-AES-CLEAR-KEY
|After `TRIGGER.KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR` and a reload of key, IV and plaintext, the encryption equals the golden (`aes.adoc` register text, `TRIGGER.KEY_IV_DATA_IN_CLEAR`; `aes.hjson`, `KEY_SHARE0`, `KEY_SHARE1` and `IV`: the registers take new values while the engine is idle). A DUT that stays busy or broken after the clear, or that ignores the reloaded key, fails the golden. The IV words after the clear are logged as `OBS-AES-CLEAR-IV` and not graded: the specification states pseudo-random data and no value (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked-legs,Blocked legs of tested features>>). Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against `env/sep_aes_golden.py`. Control: the IV read before the clear equals the written IV, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AES-CLEAR-KEY PASS seed=<n> reload_ct==golden=1 ctrl_iv_read_equal=1`, and `OBS-AES-CLEAR-IV seed=<n> iv_before=0x<b> iv_after=0x<a>`.
|CHK-AES-RATE-CT
|Every block of every rate run equals the golden ciphertext. The contract is the AES cipher definition (`hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc`: an AES cipher with the stated key sizes and modes), which the golden `env/sep_aes_golden.py` implements; the cipher result is a function of the key and the plaintext only, so the reseed rate has no term in it. A DUT whose reseed corrupts the data path shows a mismatch. Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against `env/sep_aes_golden.py`. Evidence: `CHK-AES-RATE-CT PASS seed=<n> blocks=<N> mismatches=0` with the total over the five runs.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes, except the blocked CHK-AES-CLEAR-OUT, which logs `OBS-` lines, is not counted, and does not fail the leaf. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade the number of acknowledges per reseed (not stated), the values of DATA_OUT and IV after a clear trigger (not stated), `FORCE_MASKS`, or a write of `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` (so `KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` stays at its reset value). `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test` and `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test` own the request and the acknowledge of a reseed write and keep them. `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` owns the grade of the AES beats against the stream.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test]]
===== 1.34 `sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (AES, KMAC and alerts)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, `tools = ["vcs"]` (a read after reset must show no X), 4 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (CPU-LSU master; in `no_cpu` mode it is spliced onto the CPU bus); `seq_lib/sep_aes_seq.py` and `env/sep_aes_golden.py` (AES driver and golden); `seq_lib/sep_kmac_seq.py` and `env/sep_kmac_golden.py` (KMAC driver and SHA3 golden); `env/sep_seeded_rng.py`. Probe: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved; vector bit 32 is the Crypto alert). Added hook: the alert sender inject, an error inject that forces the `alert_p` and `alert_n` pair of one crypto alert receiver to the same value for one trial (CHK-ALERT-MALFORMED); the test adds it to the inject exceptions of `docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the legal values A and B of each shadowed control register (A differs from B), the legal values of the AES AUX pair (they differ in `KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` only, never in `FORCE_MASKS`) and of the KMAC threshold pair (two different 10-bit values), the gap between the two writes of a pair (0 to 64 clocks), the message of the KMAC digest, the order of the six blocks of the malformed alert leg, the receiver of each block (one of the two of its range, or the one of HMAC), the wire value of the malformed encoding (0 or 1) and the `ALERT_TEST` bit of each block. `reseed = 4` samples other values, gaps and orders. The four shadowed targets, the AUX lock, the AES reset leg and the six malformed alert trials run on every seed.

*Objective*: An update error in the shadowed Control Register of AES (`CTRL_SHADOWED`) sets `STATUS.ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` and a recoverable alert, and the unit restarts when the register is rewritten (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`). The KMAC `CFG_SHADOWED` register has a status bit of the same name, and the specification does not state what causes its update error, so the KMAC trials log their outcome. `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` (RW0C, reset 1), once cleared, locks `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, and a write of 1 does not set it again. An AES reset clears the error (the AUX reset value is graded in `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test`). A malformed encoding on the alert pair of any crypto block raises the Crypto alert source (PIC source 33, vector bit 32), the source that an `ALERT_TEST` write of the block raises. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/aes/regs/gen/adoc/aes.adoc` (`CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN`, `STATUS`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/aes/data/aes.hjson` (`CTRL_SHADOWED`, `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/kmac/regs/gen/adoc/kmac.adoc` (`STATUS`, `CFG_SHADOWED`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` (OCAH Modifications: the AES core uses `rst_ni` for both reset domains); `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (OpenTitan Alert Termination); `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (source 33); the `ALERT_TEST` registers of HMAC, OTBN, AES, KMAC, CSRNG and EDN in the register descriptions.

*Test Coverage*: The four targets AES `CTRL_SHADOWED`, AES `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`, KMAC `CFG_SHADOWED` and KMAC `ENTROPY_REFRESH_THRESHOLD_SHADOWED`, each with a mismatched pair and an equal pair. The AES results are graded. The KMAC results (the update error, the alert, the readback and the digest after the rewrite) are logged. Recovery of the AES control register by a rewrite and one operation. The AUX lock. An AES reset with the AUX register at a value other than its reset value and the update error set. The malformed alert leg on six blocks (HMAC, OTBN, AES, KMAC, CSRNG, EDN) with an `ALERT_TEST` write as a timing reference that the leaf does not grade. This entry extends `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`, which clears `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN`, pulses the AES reset bit and requires `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` = 1 after it: only the status bit, the X-free readback after the reset, the new AUX pair after the reset and the lock of `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` are graded here, and the open test keeps its cell. `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` grades the AUX register value after an AES reset. `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` (`check_status_clean`) grades a clean AES status after equal-pair writes of `CTRL_SHADOWED`, so the equal-pair cell here grades only the pulse on vector bit 32 and the readback. The Crypto alert for each `ALERT_TEST` bit and the receiver range of each alert belong to `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`. This entry sets the update error and drives the malformed alert encoding. Functional coverage: `sep_aes_fault_cg`, `sep_crypto_reg_lock_cg`, `sep_crypto_alert_cg`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal entropy source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`, then bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN.
. Release the AES and KMAC reset bits of `SW_RESET_N`. Hold the HMAC, OTBN, ABR and Key Manager bits at 0. Log the baseline value.
. Draw the plan from the seed and log it (`PLAN seed=<n> ...`).
. Steps 4 to 11 run once for AES and then once for KMAC. For KMAC every value of steps 5 to 11 is logged as `OBS-SHADOW-KMAC` and no check grades it, and a timeout of a KMAC wait logs `OBS-SHADOW-KMAC` and does not fail the leaf. Start the injection trial of the control register: pulse the reset bit of the block (0 then 1) and wait for the block to be idle (`STATUS.IDLE` = 1 for AES, `STATUS.sha3_idle` = 1 for KMAC) in a bounded wait.
. Error injection. Write A to the control register (`CTRL_SHADOWED` of AES, `CFG_SHADOWED` of KMAC), wait the drawn gap and write B, with no read of the register between the two writes. Point: the shadowed register. Kind: two different legal values. Expected response: update-error status and a recoverable alert. Both writes answer OKAY. Every AES word is legal, and A and B differ in one drawn legal field (KEY_LEN, MODE or OPERATION). Every KMAC word keeps SHA3, L256, `kmac_en` = 0, `sideload` = 0, `entropy_mode` = EDN and `entropy_ready` = 1, and A and B differ in one drawn endianness bit.
. Vector bit 32 is sampled on every clock from before the issue of the first write of the pair. Poll the `STATUS` of the block until `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` = 1 in a bounded wait (a timeout fails CHK-SHADOW-ERR). Log as c_err the clocks from the issue of the second write to the first high clock of vector bit 32, counted from 1, so c_err is 1 or more whether the pulse comes before or after the write response (the specification does not order them; a bounded wait, a timeout fails the check). Count the clocks with the bit high as `alert_cycles`. For KMAC take the window base c_err from the AES trial of the same seed.
. Read `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` of the block.
. Recovery. Write the legal word R twice (AES: ECB, AES_256, AES_ENC, automatic; KMAC: the word with both endianness bits 0). Run one operation: for AES write the eight key words, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write `TRIGGER.DATA_OUT_CLEAR`, wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1, write one block, wait for `OUTPUT_VALID` = 1 in a bounded wait and read the four DATA_OUT words; for KMAC run one SHA3-256 hash of the drawn message with the command sequence of `seq_lib/sep_kmac_seq.py`, wait for `kmac_done` in a bounded wait and read the digest from STATE.
. Equal-pair trial of the control register: pulse the reset bit of the block, wait for the block to be idle, write A, wait the drawn gap and write A again. Count the high clocks of vector bit 32 from the issue of the first write to 4 x c_err clocks after the issue of the second write (a test parameter), read the update-error status and log it as `OBS-SHADOW-STATUS`, and read the register back.
. AUX or threshold target of the same block: pulse the reset bit, wait for idle, write the first value of the pair, wait the drawn gap and write the second value (a mismatched pair). Read the update-error bit of `STATUS` and count the clocks with vector bit 32 high over a window of 4 x c_err clocks. Log them as `OBS-SHADOW-AUX`.
. AUX or threshold target of the same block: pulse the reset bit, wait for idle, write the first value twice (an equal pair), read the register back and log the same values as `OBS-SHADOW-AUX`.
. AUX lock leg. Pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1. Write the AUX value A_aux (`KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` = 0, which differs from the reset value) twice and read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`.
. AUX lock leg. Write 0 to `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` and read it. Write the AUX value B_aux (`KEY_TOUCH_FORCES_RESEED` = 1) twice and read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`. Write 1 to `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` and read it.
. AES reset leg. With `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` = 0 and the AUX register at A_aux, write A and B to `CTRL_SHADOWED` of AES (a mismatched pair) and wait for `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` = 1 in a bounded wait. Read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`.
. AES reset leg. Pulse the AES reset bit and wait for `STATUS.IDLE` = 1 in a bounded wait. Read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` and `STATUS`, and check that every field bit of the read data is 0 or 1 (common rule "Field bits").
. AES reset leg. Write the AUX pair B_aux twice and read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED`. Then write the AUX pair A_aux twice and read `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` again. A_aux and B_aux differ, so at least one of the two pairs changes the register, whatever value the reset leaves in it.
. Malformed alert leg. Release the HMAC and OTBN reset bits of `SW_RESET_N`.
. Malformed alert leg. Steps 18 to 20 run once for each of the six blocks in the drawn order. Sample vector bit 32 on every clock from before the `ALERT_TEST` write is issued. Write 1 to the drawn `ALERT_TEST` bit of the block and log c, the clocks from the issue of the write to the first high clock of bit 32, counted from 1 (the specification does not order the pulse against the write response), as the timing reference of the trial, as `OBS-ALERT-REF seed=<n> block=<b> clk=<c>` (a bounded wait; a timeout logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf). The rise for each `ALERT_TEST` bit is graded by `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`. Wait for vector bit 32 to fall.
. Malformed alert leg. Sample vector bit 32 (it reads 0). Error injection (the alert sender inject). Force `alert_p` and `alert_n` of the drawn receiver of the block to the same drawn value. Point: the alert pair of one receiver. Kind: both wires at one value, which is not a differential code. Expected response: a rise of vector bit 32. Hold the force until the bit rises or 4 x c clocks pass. Log the clocks to the rise as `raise_clk`.
. Malformed alert leg. Release the force, wait 4 x c clocks and sample vector bit 32.
. Compare every value with its golden, log the checks and log `RESULT sep_crypto_shadow_update_err_rand_test seed=<n> PASS checks=6`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SHADOW-ERR
|For AES `CTRL_SHADOWED`, a second write that differs from the first sets `STATUS.ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR`, pulses vector bit 32 and leaves `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` at 0 (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`; `aes.adoc` register text, `STATUS`; `crypto.adoc`, OpenTitan Alert Termination; `interrupts.adoc`, source 33). A DUT that takes B or ignores the mismatch shows no status and no pulse, and a DUT that raises the fatal path shows `ALERT_FATAL_FAULT` = 1. Anchor: `STATUS` readback and `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o[32]`. Control: CHK-SHADOW-CTRL of the same target, where the equal pair gives no pulse. The AUX and threshold targets and the KMAC `CFG_SHADOWED` are logged and not graded. Evidence: `CHK-SHADOW-ERR PASS seed=<n> reg=aes_ctrl a=0x<a> b=0x<b> status_err=1 alert_cycles=<c> fatal=0 c_err=<e>` and `OBS-SHADOW-AUX seed=<n> reg=<aes_aux\|kmac_cfg\|kmac_thr> pair=<mismatched\|equal> status_err=<s> alert_cycles=<c>`.
|CHK-SHADOW-CTRL
|For AES `CTRL_SHADOWED`, an equal pair (A, A) raises no pulse on vector bit 32 over a window of 4 x c_err clocks and reads back A (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`: two writes of the same value commit; a recoverable alert follows two different values only). The update-error status of the equal pair is read and logged, because `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test` grades a clean status after equal-pair writes. A DUT that flags every write, or that fails to commit an equal pair, fails. The window runs from the issue of the first write to 4 x c_err clocks after the issue of the second write, and c_err is the delay from the issue of the second write of the mismatched pair to its first high clock, so the window covers a pulse before or after the write response. Anchor: the vector probe and register readback. Control: the mismatched pair of the same target pulses in the same seed (`c_err` above 0), or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-SHADOW-CTRL PASS seed=<n> reg=aes_ctrl a=0x<a> alert_cycles=0 readback=0x<a> window_clk=<w> ctrl_c_err=<e>` and `OBS-SHADOW-STATUS seed=<n> reg=<r> status_err=<s>`.
|CHK-SHADOW-RESTART
|After the legal word R is written twice, the next AES block equals its golden (`aes.hjson`, `CTRL_SHADOWED`: a write of the Control Register starts a new message; `aes.adoc` register text, `STATUS`). A DUT that stays in the error state or corrupts the result fails. Anchor: DATA_OUT readback against `env/sep_aes_golden.py`; the golden compare is the positive evidence. The KMAC digest after the rewrite is compared with `env/sep_kmac_golden.py` and logged as `OBS-SHADOW-KMAC`. Evidence: `CHK-SHADOW-RESTART PASS seed=<n> reg=aes_ctrl op==golden`.
|CHK-AUX-REGWEN
|After 0 is written to `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` it reads 0, a new AUX pair leaves `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` at the value that it held before the pair, and a write of 1 leaves `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` at 0 (`aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN`: RW0C; a cleared bit blocks writes). A DUT that lets the AUX write land, or that sets the bit again, fails. Anchor: register readback. Control: the same AUX write before the lock lands, so the refused write is one that would change the register, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AUX-REGWEN PASS seed=<n> regwen_after0=0 aux_before=0x<x> aux_after=0x<x> regwen_after1=0 ctrl_pre_lock_lands=1`.
|CHK-AUX-RESET
|After an AES reset `ALERT_RECOV_CTRL_UPDATE_ERR` reads 0, the field bits of the read data have no X, and two new AUX pairs of different values (B_aux, then A_aux) each land, so each readback equals the value of its pair (`hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc`, OCAH Modifications: the AES core uses `rst_ni` for both reset domains; `aes.adoc` register text, `CTRL_AUX_SHADOWED` and `STATUS`). The AUX register value after the reset is read and logged; `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` grades it. A DUT that keeps the error across the reset, that returns an unknown value, or that does not unlock the AUX register fails. Anchor: register readback on VCS. Control: before the reset the AUX register differs from its reset value and the status bit reads 1, or the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails. Evidence: `CHK-AUX-RESET PASS seed=<n> ctrl_aux_pre_reset=0x<v> ctrl_err_pre_reset=1 status_err=0 x_free=1 aux_after_b=0x<b> aux_after_a=0x<a> write_pairs_land=1` and `OBS-AUX-RESET seed=<n> aux=0x<reset>`. A refused pair leaves one readback at the other value and fails the check.
|CHK-ALERT-MALFORMED
|For each of the six blocks, a malformed encoding on the alert pair of one receiver (both wires at one value) raises vector bit 32, and the bit reads 0 before the injection and after the release (`crypto.adoc`, OpenTitan Alert Termination: a malformed encoding raises the integrity-fail of the receiver, and the Crypto alert source is the OR of every receiver pulse and integrity-fail). A DUT whose integrity-fail does not reach the Crypto alert source shows no rise. Anchor: `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o[32]`. Control: the rise during the injection is the positive evidence, and it is the control of the post-release low read. The bound of the hold and the post-release window are multiples of the `ALERT_TEST` delay of the same block (`OBS-ALERT-REF`). Evidence: `CHK-ALERT-MALFORMED PASS seed=<n> block=<hmac\|otbn\|aes\|kmac\|csrng\|edn> index=<i> pre=0 raised=1 post=0 raise_clk=<r> ref_clk=<c>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This entry does not grade a status bit or an alert for the AUX and threshold targets (the register text does not state them; they are logged only), the update error of the KMAC `CFG_SHADOWED` (its cause is not stated; logged only), which receiver or which index of a receiver range pulses on an `ALERT_TEST` write or an update error (owned by `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`), the alert ping (it is disabled), a block status bit after a malformed encoding, or the fatal-fault path. `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` owns the `CTRL_AUX_REGWEN` reset value after an AES reset and keeps it. `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` owns the AUX register reset value.
[[sep_kmac_unsupported_cfg_inject_test]]
===== 1.35 `sep_kmac_unsupported_cfg_inject_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (KMAC)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_kmac_unsupported_cfg_inject_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip with the CPU-LSU master readback of the KMAC `INTR_STATE`, `STATUS`, `ERR_CODE` and `STATE` registers (approved); `sep_kmac_golden` for the SHA3-256 digest. No observation tap. No inject beyond the unsupported configuration writes of the scenario.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the order of the 8 supported cells, the order of the 6 unsupported cells with `en_unsupported_modestrength` = 0, the order of the 6 unsupported cells with it set, and one message of 0 to 100 bytes for the recovery hash of each blocked cell. `reseed = 4` samples different orders and messages. The 20 cells are the same on every seed.

*Objective*: SHAKE and cSHAKE accept only the strengths 128 and 256, and another strength gives an error when hashing starts. With `en_unsupported_modestrength` = 0 KMAC does not propagate `CMD.start` to the SHA3 engine. With the bit set the engine moves forward. `CMD.err_processed` returns the state machine to its reset state. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/kmac/regs/gen/adoc/kmac.adoc` (`CFG_SHADOWED`, `CMD`, `INTR_STATE`, `STATUS`, `ERR_CODE`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/data/kmac.hjson` for the mode, strength and command codes.

*Test Coverage*: The 20 cells mode {SHAKE, cSHAKE} x strength {128, 224, 256, 384, 512} x `en_unsupported_modestrength` {0, 1}, in EDN entropy mode with `kmac_en` = 0. The 12 cells with strength 224, 384 or 512 are unsupported. The 8 supported cells are the control. Each blocked start is followed by `CMD.err_processed`, a write-one clear of `kmac_err` and a legal SHA3-256 hash. Extends `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`, which drives legal cells only. This test grades only the new cells: the unsupported strengths with the enable bit clear and set, and the recovery. The legal cells of the supported group are controls and are not graded; the owner of the open test keeps the legal digests. Functional coverage: `sep_crypto_invalid_encoding_cg` (`x_kmac_mode_strength`, `cp_kmac_err_recover`), `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_hw_raise` kmac_err).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`. Bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Set `SW_RESET_N` to the baseline of the common rules with the bits of OTBN, AES, HMAC and ABR clear. Log the baseline.
. Draw the plan with `SepSeededRng`: the three cell orders and the messages. Log `PLAN seed=<n>` with the orders.
. Supported group, each of the 8 cells in drawn order. Reset KMAC (bit 4 of `SW_RESET_N` low, then high). Read `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_CODE` and expect 0 (reset values). For a cSHAKE cell write the PREFIX words of the open helper (empty function name and customization string). Write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with the mode, the strength and `en_unsupported_modestrength` of the cell, `kmac_en` = 0, `sideload` = 0, `entropy_mode` = EDN and `entropy_ready` = 1. Write `CMD.start`. Wait for `STATUS.sha3_absorb` = 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check). Read `INTR_STATE.kmac_err`. Log the clock count from `CMD.start` to absorb as the start time of the cell.
. Take t_clean as the longest start time of step 3 and log it. Step 5 uses t_clean as the length of its hold window, because a healthy KMAC reaches absorb within it.
. Blocked group, each of the 6 unsupported cells with `en_unsupported_modestrength` = 0 in drawn order. This is the error injection. Point: `CFG_SHADOWED`. Kind: strength 224, 384 or 512 on SHAKE or cSHAKE. Expected response: `kmac_err` = 1 and no propagation of the start. Reset KMAC. Read `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_CODE` and expect 0. For a cSHAKE cell write the PREFIX words. Write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with the cell, `kmac_en` = 0, `sideload` = 0, EDN mode and `entropy_ready` = 1. Write `CMD.start`. Wait for `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` = 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check). Read `STATUS`, `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_CODE`. Hold for a window of t_clean. Read `STATUS` again and log the window length.
. Recovery for the same cell. Write `CMD.err_processed`. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.kmac_err`. Read `INTR_STATE` and `STATUS`. Write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with SHA3, strength 256, `kmac_en` = 0, `sideload` = 0, EDN mode and `entropy_ready` = 1. Write `CMD.start`. Wait for `STATUS.sha3_absorb` = 1. Write the drawn message to the `MSG_FIFO` window (empty for a message of 0 bytes). Write `CMD.process`. Wait for `STATUS.sha3_squeeze` = 1 and `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` = 1 (a bounded wait). Read the two `STATE` shares at offsets 0x400 and 0x500 and XOR them word by word. Write `CMD.done`. Read `INTR_STATE.kmac_err`.
. Enabled group, each of the 6 unsupported cells with `en_unsupported_modestrength` = 1 in drawn order. Reset KMAC. Read `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_CODE` and expect 0. For a cSHAKE cell write the PREFIX words. Write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with the cell, `kmac_en` = 0, `sideload` = 0, EDN mode and `entropy_ready` = 1. Write `CMD.start`. Wait for `STATUS.sha3_absorb` = 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check). Read `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` and log it. Reset KMAC. The leaf does not process a message in this group.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KMAC-UNSUP
|With `en_unsupported_modestrength` = 0, `CMD.start` on SHAKE or cSHAKE with strength 224, 384 or 512 gives `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` = 1, `STATUS.sha3_idle` = 1, `STATUS.sha3_absorb` = 0, `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` = 0 and a non-zero `ERR_CODE` (`kmac.adoc`, `CFG_SHADOWED.kstrength`: another strength gives an error when hashing starts; `CFG_SHADOWED.en_unsupported_modestrength`: with the bit clear KMAC does not propagate the start; `ERR_CODE`: reset 0, holds the cause after `kmac_err`). A wrong DUT that propagates the start shows `sha3_absorb` = 1 or no error. A DUT that raises the error and still absorbs fails the first read or the read after the hold window. The check reads `kmac_err` and `ERR_CODE` as 0 before the start, so the later values come from the start. Anchor: `INTR_STATE`, `STATUS` and `ERR_CODE` readback after a bounded wait for `kmac_err`, then a second `STATUS` read after the hold window of t_clean, in which a healthy KMAC reaches absorb on a supported cell. Control: in the same seed each supported cell (128 or 256 on SHAKE or cSHAKE, with either value of the enable bit) raises no `kmac_err` and reaches `STATUS.sha3_absorb` = 1 after `CMD.start`, so the trial flow reaches absorb when the strength is legal. The leaf logs `CTRL-KMAC-UNSUP seed=<n> mode=<m> kstrength=<128 or 256> en_unsup=<e> err=0 absorb=1 start_clk=<c>` for each supported cell, and a missing or failing control logs `CTRL-MISSING` and fails the leaf. The supported cells are not graded here: `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test` keeps the legal cells, and `sep_kmac_stream_cfg_rand_test` grades the absorb stage after `start`. The `ERR_CODE` value is logged and only "non-zero" is graded. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-UNSUP PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> kstrength=<s> en_unsup=0 err=1 absorb=0 idle=1 done=0 err_code_nonzero=1 hold_clk=<w>` and `OBS-KMAC-ERRCODE seed=<n> err_code=0x<c>`.
|CHK-KMAC-UNSUP-EN
|With `en_unsupported_modestrength` = 1 the unsupported cell reaches `STATUS.sha3_absorb` = 1 after `CMD.start` (`kmac.adoc`, `CFG_SHADOWED.en_unsupported_modestrength`: KMAC moves forward with an unsupported mode and strength). `kmac_err` is logged and not graded, because the text does not say whether the error is also raised. A wrong DUT that ignores the bit stays idle, as it does with the bit clear. Anchor: `STATUS` readback after a bounded wait. Control: the same (mode, strength) cell with the bit clear in the same seed does not absorb (CHK-KMAC-UNSUP). Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-UNSUP-EN PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> kstrength=<s> en_unsup=1 absorb=1 OBS-err=<e> ctrl_absorb=0`.
|CHK-KMAC-ERRPROC
|After `CMD.err_processed` and a write-one clear of `INTR_STATE.kmac_err`, `kmac_err` reads 0 and `STATUS.sha3_idle` reads 1 (`kmac.adoc`, `CMD.err_processed`: the state machine returns to its reset state). A legal SHA3-256 hash of the drawn message then returns the golden digest, and `kmac_err` still reads 0 after the hash. A wrong DUT that stays in the error state does not absorb or does not finish (the bounded wait times out), returns a wrong digest, or keeps `kmac_err` set. Anchor: `INTR_STATE` and `STATUS` readback, and the XOR of the two `STATE` shares against the `sep_kmac_golden` SHA3-256 digest of the message. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-ERRPROC PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> kstrength=<s> err_cleared=1 idle=1 len=<L> next_digest==golden err_after=0`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This test grades the unsupported strengths on SHAKE and cSHAKE and the recovery by `CMD.err_processed`. The following are not claimed. Mode 2'b01 is blocked and never driven. SHA3 with strength 128 is not driven, because the text states no rule for it. The `ERR_CODE` value is logged and only "non-zero" is graded. The digest of an unsupported cell and the `kmac_err` state after an enabled start are not graded. KMAC mode (`kmac_en` = 1) is not driven. The digests of the legal cells belong to `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`, and the `kmac_err` interrupt line belongs to the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`.

'''''

[[sep_kmac_stream_cfg_rand_test]]
===== 1.36 `sep_kmac_stream_cfg_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (KMAC)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_kmac_stream_cfg_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip with the CPU-LSU master readback of the KMAC `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`, `STATUS`, `CFG_REGWEN`, `CFG_SHADOWED` and `STATE` registers (approved); `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved, vector bit 20); `sep_kmac_golden` for the SHA3 and SHAKE digests. No observation tap. No inject.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the order of the 18 cells; the message bytes (the bytes of each multi-byte write differ from each other, so that a byte reorder changes the digest); the write width (32, 16 or 8 bit) of each message write. Cell i in drawn order takes the (`msg_endianness`, `state_endianness`) cell number i mod 4, so all four cells run on every seed. The plan places a one-byte write in at least one cell with `msg_endianness` = 1. `reseed = 4` samples different orders, bytes and widths. The 18 cells are the same on every seed.

*Objective*: `CMD.start` moves `STATUS` to `sha3_absorb`, and `CMD.run` in the squeeze stage gives the next block of a SHAKE output. With no command written `STATUS` stays idle. With `msg_endianness` = 1 each multi-byte `MSG_FIFO` write is added to the message in big-endian byte order, and a one-byte write is not changed. With `state_endianness` = 1 each `STATE` word is converted to big-endian byte order, and the order of the words does not change. With `INTR_ENABLE.kmac_done` set, a completed hash sets `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` and vector bit 20 (source 21) rises. `CFG_REGWEN` reads 0 while the KMAC is in operation, and `CFG_SHADOWED` does not change then. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/kmac/regs/gen/adoc/kmac.adoc` (`CFG_SHADOWED`, `CMD`, `STATUS`, `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/kmac.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/data/kmac.hjson` for the codes, the Keccak rate of each strength, the `STATE` share layout and `CFG_REGWEN`.

*Test Coverage*: The 18 cells of the six legal (mode, strength) combinations (SHA3 224, 256, 384, 512; SHAKE 128, 256) x message length {rate minus 1, rate, rate plus 1}, with `kmac_en` = 0 in EDN entropy mode. All four (`msg_endianness`, `state_endianness`) cells on every seed, with drawn 32-bit, 16-bit and 8-bit writes. The `run` command after the first squeeze of every SHAKE cell, for both strengths. The idle window before every start. The `CFG_REGWEN` value in the idle window and in the absorb stage, with a locked double write of `CFG_SHADOWED` in the absorb stage of every cell. The `kmac_done` line rise at the event with the enable set. Extends `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`, which runs one endianness setting and the first squeeze only, grades the keyed digests, and whose helper polls squeeze after `process` and idle after `done` with failing timeouts. This entry grades only the new cells: the `start` stage, the idle state with no command, the configuration lock, both endianness bits, the length corners and `run`. It uses the squeeze after `process` and the idle after `done` as controls only; the owner of the open test keeps their grading. The done-line check extends `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` and `sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`, which raise `kmac_done` with `INTR_TEST` and the enable set and grade the line and the write-one clear. This entry grades the rise at the real completion event with the enable set; the enable gate of the line belongs to the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`, and the owners of the open tests keep the `INTR_TEST` walk. Functional coverage: `sep_kmac_cfg_cmd_cg` (`x_cmd_stage`, `x_endian`, `x_len_corner`, `cp_run_block`), `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_hw_raise` kmac_done). The configuration lock fills no Phase 2 bin.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`. Bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Set `SW_RESET_N` to the baseline of the common rules with the bits of OTBN, AES, HMAC and ABR clear. Log the baseline.
. Draw the plan with `SepSeededRng`: the cell order, the endianness cell of each cell, the messages and the write widths. Log `PLAN seed=<n>`.
. Reset KMAC (bit 4 of `SW_RESET_N` low, then high). Run one warm-up hash that no check grades. Write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with SHA3, strength 256, `kmac_en` = 0, both endianness bits 0, `sideload` = 0, `entropy_mode` = EDN and `entropy_ready` = 1. Write `CMD.start` and log the clock count to `sha3_absorb` as t_start. Write one message byte, write `CMD.process`, wait for `sha3_squeeze` = 1, write `CMD.done`, wait for `sha3_idle` = 1 (each a bounded wait), write 1 to `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` and read it as 0.
. For each cell in drawn order, write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with its mode and strength, `kmac_en` = 0, its `msg_endianness` and `state_endianness`, `sideload` = 0, EDN mode and `entropy_ready` = 1. Read `CFG_SHADOWED` back as the cell value C (control of CHK-KMAC-REGWEN). Steps 5 to 15 run once per cell.
. Idle window. Write no command. For a window of a multiple of t_start (fixed by the leaf and logged) read `STATUS` and `INTR_STATE` repeatedly. Read `CFG_REGWEN` once in the window (CHK-KMAC-REGWEN).
. Write 1 to `INTR_ENABLE.kmac_done`.
. Write `CMD.start`. Wait for `STATUS` to show `sha3_absorb` = 1 and `sha3_idle` = 0, with a wait as long as the idle window (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check).
. Read `CFG_REGWEN`. Write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with C and its `state_endianness` bit inverted, then read `CFG_SHADOWED` (CHK-KMAC-REGWEN).
. Write the message of the cell (length rate minus 1, rate or rate plus 1 bytes) to the `MSG_FIFO` window in the drawn widths, naturally aligned.
. Write `CMD.process`. Wait for `STATUS` to show `sha3_squeeze` = 1 (a bounded wait).
. Sample vector bit 20 of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` at every clock from the `CMD.process` write of step 10 and require it to read 0 there. Wait for `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` = 1 (a bounded wait). Wait for bit 20 = 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-KMAC-DONE-LINE).
. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.kmac_done`. Read `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` as 0 and log vector bit 20 as `OBS-KMAC-DONE-FALL`.
. Read the two `STATE` shares at offsets 0x400 and 0x500 and XOR them word by word. The compared length is the digest size of a SHA3 cell, or the Keccak rate in bytes of a SHAKE cell.
. For a SHAKE cell, write `CMD.run`. Poll `STATUS` and `STATE` until `sha3_squeeze` = 1 and the rate block equals the second block of the golden output (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check).
. Write `CMD.done`. Wait for `STATUS` to show `sha3_idle` = 1 and `sha3_squeeze` = 0 (a bounded wait). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` and read it as 0, as the warm-up of step 3 does, so the idle window of the next cell starts from a cleared bit.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KMAC-SEQ
|After `CMD.start` `STATUS` shows `sha3_absorb` = 1 and `sha3_idle` = 0, and after `CMD.run` in the squeeze stage `STATUS` shows `sha3_squeeze` = 1 (`kmac.adoc`, `STATUS`, `CMD`). A wrong DUT stays idle after `start`, shows idle and absorb together, or leaves the squeeze stage after `run`. Anchor: `STATUS` readback, once per cell for `start` and once per SHAKE cell for `run`. Controls in the same cell: `sha3_squeeze` = 1 after `process` and `sha3_idle` = 1 after `done`, which show that the polls reach the other stages. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-SEQ PASS seed=<n> cmd=<start or run> idle=<i> absorb=<a> squeeze=<s> ctrl_process_squeeze=1 ctrl_done_idle=1`.
|CHK-KMAC-DIGEST
|The two `STATE` shares XORed equal the Keccak golden of the message. The message is the byte sequence that the writes add: a multi-byte write adds its bytes in big-endian order when `msg_endianness` = 1, and a one-byte write is not changed. Each `STATE` word is byte-swapped when `state_endianness` = 1, and the word order is not changed (`kmac.adoc`, `CFG_SHADOWED.msg_endianness` and `state_endianness`). The compared length is the digest size of a SHA3 cell, or the Keccak rate of a SHAKE cell. A wrong DUT with an endianness bit that does nothing, or that swaps the wrong bytes, returns a different digest. The unique bytes in each write make a reorder visible. Anchor: `STATE` readback against the `sep_kmac_golden` digest. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-DIGEST PASS seed=<n> mode=<m> strength=<s> kmac_en=0 msg_end=<x> state_end=<y> len=<L> widths=<w> digest==golden`.
|CHK-KMAC-RUN
|For a SHAKE cell, `CMD.run` in the squeeze stage gives the next block of the extended output, equal to the second rate block of the golden (`kmac.hjson`, `CMD.run`: it triggers the 24 rounds in the squeeze stage). A wrong DUT whose `run` does nothing still shows the first block and times out. Anchor: `STATE` readback against the `sep_kmac_golden` output of twice the rate in bytes, with the same byte swap as the first block. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-RUN PASS seed=<n> shake=<128 or 256> state_end=<y> block2==golden=1`.
|CHK-KMAC-IDLE
|With no command written, `STATUS` shows `sha3_idle` = 1, `sha3_absorb` = 0 and `sha3_squeeze` = 0, and `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` reads 0, at every read of the window before each start (`kmac.adoc`, `STATUS`: `sha3_idle` resets to 1). A wrong DUT that leaves idle or raises `kmac_done` without a command shows it in the window. The window is a multiple of t_start, fixed by the leaf and logged. Anchor: `STATUS` and `INTR_STATE` readback. Control: the `CMD.start` that follows changes `STATUS` to absorb within the same bounded window, so the window is long enough for a healthy KMAC to react. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-IDLE PASS seed=<n> window_clk=<w> idle=1 absorb=0 squeeze=0 done=0 ctrl_absorb_clk=<c>`.
|CHK-KMAC-DONE-LINE
|At the completion event with `INTR_ENABLE.kmac_done` set, `INTR_STATE.kmac_done` reads 1 and vector bit 20 (source 21) rises (`interrupts.adoc`, `kmac.adoc`, `INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`). A wrong DUT that sets the state bit and never raises the line, or that raises it before the hash ends, shows it at one of these samples. Anchor: `INTR_STATE` readback and the vector probe. Control: vector bit 20 reads 0 at the `CMD.process` write, so the rise comes from the event. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-DONE-LINE PASS seed=<n> enable=1 state=1 vec20_before=0 vec20=1`; `OBS-KMAC-DONE-FALL seed=<n> vec20_after_w1c=<v>` is logged and not graded.
|CHK-KMAC-REGWEN
|In the idle window with no command `CFG_REGWEN` reads 1 (RDL reset value 1). In the absorb stage after `CMD.start` it reads 0, and a double write of `CFG_SHADOWED` with the `state_endianness` bit inverted leaves `CFG_SHADOWED` at the cell value C (`kmac.hjson`, `CFG_REGWEN`: the contents of `CFG_SHADOWED` cannot change while the KMAC is in operation). A wrong DUT that keeps the register writable reads back the inverted bit, and CHK-KMAC-DIGEST of the same cell then fails on the `STATE` byte order. Anchor: the `CFG_REGWEN` and `CFG_SHADOWED` readback. Control: the write of step 4 in the idle state reads back as C, so the write path is live. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-REGWEN PASS seed=<n> cell=<i> idle_regwen=1 absorb_regwen=0 cfg=0x<C> cfg_after_locked_write=0x<C>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This test grades the stages, the endianness bits, the length corners, `run`, the idle state, the configuration lock in the absorb stage and the `kmac_done` line for SHA3 and SHAKE with `kmac_en` = 0. The value of `CFG_REGWEN` in the squeeze stage and the clock at which it returns to 1 are not stated and are not graded. The following are not claimed. `msg_mask` and `entropy_fast_process` act only in KMAC mode, and the keyed digest belongs to `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`. The cSHAKE cells are not driven, because their golden needs an encoded prefix that this test does not write. Whether `kmac_done` rises again after `run` or `done` is not stated: the leaf grades no value of the bit from the clear of step 12 to the clear of step 15, and the idle window of the next cell follows the clear of step 15. The `INTR_TEST` walk of the `kmac_done` line with the enable set belongs to `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` and `sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`. The enable clear, the enable flip and the write-one clear of the line belong to the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`. The `fifo_empty` lines belong to `sep_pic_pending_map_walk_rand_test`, and the `kmac_err` line belongs to the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` and to `sep_kmac_unsupported_cfg_inject_test`. The `STATUS` fields `fifo_depth`, `fifo_empty` and `fifo_full` and the two alert bits are not graded, and the effect of `msg_mask` and `entropy_fast_process` has no stated observation (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).

'''''

[[sep_kmac_entropy_mode_rand_test]]
===== 1.37 `sep_kmac_entropy_mode_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (KMAC)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_kmac_entropy_mode_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip with the CPU-LSU master readback of the KMAC registers (approved); `crypto_edn_req_o[1]` and `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` (approved; client 1 is KMAC); `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved, vector bit 22 for the `kmac_err` line, used for the clock count of the timer); `sep_kmac_golden` for the SHA3-256 digest. No observation tap. No inject: the stalled EDN is the omitted entropy bring-up after a TRNG reset.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the order of the legs after the six-seed leg, the late-write value of the mode lock, the wait timer T (2 to 4), the non-zero prescaler P (2 to 15), the hash count N of the counter leg (2 to 10), the reseed threshold R (2 to 4), the run length M of the reseed legs (R + 2 to 12) and the key and message rows from the fixed table of test constants. `reseed = 4` samples different orders, values and rows. The legs are the same on every seed.

*Objective*: `entropy_mode` is idle at reset, then edn or sw. After `entropy_ready` is written the PRNG configuration is fixed until a KMAC reset, unless an EDN request timeout occurs in EDN mode, and later writes change only the readback. In EDN mode `ENTROPY_PERIOD.wait_timer` bounds the wait for EDN, expiry moves the entropy module to an error state, and 0 waits for ever. `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` counts hashes, and `CMD.hash_cnt_clr` and `CMD.entropy_req` clear it. In EDN mode the module reseeds the PRNG from EDN after the hash counter reaches `ENTROPY_REFRESH_THRESHOLD_SHADOWED` and clears the counter, and a threshold of 0 disables the reseed by the counter. In sw mode KMAC can start processing commands only after six `ENTROPY_SEED` writes. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/kmac/regs/gen/adoc/kmac.adoc` (`CFG_SHADOWED`, `CMD`, `ENTROPY_PERIOD`, `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT`, `ENTROPY_REFRESH_THRESHOLD_SHADOWED`, `ENTROPY_SEED`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/kmac/data/kmac.hjson` (`CFG_SHADOWED.entropy_mode`: "Only after that, the module can start processing commands"; `ENTROPY_SEED`; `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` and `ENTROPY_REFRESH_THRESHOLD_SHADOWED`: "If the threshold is 0, the refresh by the counter does not work") for the seed count, the command gate and the automatic reseed.

*Test Coverage*: Software mode with six, five and zero seed words. The mode lock with sw locked first and with edn locked first, each on every seed, on a KMAC with no EDN timeout since its reset. EDN mode with the entropy chain uninitialized: `wait_timer` T, 2T, 8 and 0, each with prescaler 0 and with a drawn non-zero prescaler, on every seed. N keyed KMAC hashes (N from 2 to 10) with the threshold above N, then `CMD.hash_cnt_clr`, then `CMD.entropy_req` while idle. The automatic reseed in EDN mode with the chain up: on every seed a drawn threshold R and M keyed hashes with M from R + 2 to 12, then the threshold 0 with the same M. Error injection: a stalled EDN and a missing seed word. Extends `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`, which uses one setting in EDN mode, and `sep_hmac_kmac_cpu_crypto_smoke_test`, which runs software entropy mode with six seed words and grades `kmac_done`, `ERR_CODE` = 0 and a non-zero digest. This entry grades only the new cells. The six-seed leg adds only the exact golden digest. The five-seed and zero-seed cells, the mode lock, the timer, the hash counter and the automatic reseed are new. The owners of the open tests keep the keyed digests and the smoke checks. Functional coverage: `sep_kmac_entropy_cg` (`x_mode_lock`, `cp_wait_timer`, `cp_sw_seed_count`, `cp_hash_cnt`, `cp_entropy_req_ack`, `cp_auto_reseed`), `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_hw_raise` kmac_err).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`. Bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Set `SW_RESET_N` to the baseline of the common rules with the bits of OTBN, AES, HMAC and ABR clear. Log the baseline. "Chain up" means the state after this bring-up. "Chain uninitialized" means the state after a TRNG reset (bit 5 of `SW_RESET_N` low, then high) with no bring-up after it. Every leg that needs the chain up runs this bring-up first when the chain is uninitialized.
. Draw the plan with `SepSeededRng`. Log `PLAN seed=<n>` with the leg order, the late-write values, T, P, N, R, M and the table rows.
. Six-seed leg. Reset KMAC (bit 4 of `SW_RESET_N` low, then high). Write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with `entropy_mode` = sw, `entropy_ready` = 0, SHA3, strength 256, `kmac_en` = 0 and `sideload` = 0. Write it twice again with `entropy_ready` = 1. Write six `ENTROPY_SEED` words from the table.
. Write `CMD.start`. Wait for `STATUS.sha3_absorb` = 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check). Log the clock count from the start to absorb as t_ctrl.
. Write the message row. Write `CMD.process`. Wait for `STATUS.sha3_squeeze` = 1. Read the two `STATE` shares at offsets 0x400 and 0x500 and XOR them word by word. Write `CMD.done`.
. The legs in steps 7 to 20 run in the drawn order. Each leg starts with a KMAC reset and sets its own chain state.
. Seed-count leg, for 5 and for 0 seed words in drawn order. This is the error injection. Point: the seed writes. Kind: starvation (fewer than six words). Expected response: no start. Reset KMAC. Write `CFG_SHADOWED` as in step 3, first with `entropy_ready` = 0, then with `entropy_ready` = 1. Write the seed words. Write `CMD.start`. For a window of a multiple of t_ctrl (fixed by the leaf and logged) read `STATUS` repeatedly. Read `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` and log it.
. Mode lock leg, for sw first and for edn first in drawn order. Run the bring-up if the chain is uninitialized. Reset KMAC. Read `CFG_SHADOWED.entropy_mode` and expect idle (0). Write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with the first mode, `entropy_ready` = 0, SHA3, strength 256, `kmac_en` = 0 and `sideload` = 0. Write it twice again with `entropy_ready` = 1.
. Write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with the late-write value and `entropy_ready` = 1. The late-write value is edn or idle when sw is locked, and sw when edn is locked. Read `CFG_SHADOWED`. Write no seed word. Write `CMD.start`. With sw locked, read `STATUS` repeatedly for a window of a multiple of t_ctrl. With edn locked, wait for `STATUS.sha3_absorb` = 1 (a bounded wait), write the message row, write `CMD.process`, wait for `sha3_squeeze` = 1, read and XOR the two `STATE` shares, and write `CMD.done`.
. Timer leg, for prescaler 0 and for the drawn non-zero prescaler P in drawn order. This is the error injection. Point: the entropy chain state. Kind: starvation (EDN does not answer). Expected response: `kmac_err` after the timer. Make the chain uninitialized. For the wait timers T, 2T and 8 in this order, reset KMAC, write `INTR_ENABLE.kmac_err` = 1, read `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_CODE` and expect 0, and write `ENTROPY_PERIOD` with the wait timer and the prescaler.
. For each of the three runs of step 10, write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with `entropy_mode` = EDN, `entropy_ready` = 0, SHA3, strength 256, `kmac_en` = 0 and `sideload` = 0. Write it twice again with `entropy_ready` = 1. Count clocks from the rise of `crypto_edn_req_o[1]` to the rise of vector bit 22. Wait for the rise (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check). Log the count as the error time. Count the acknowledges on `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` in that interval. Read `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` and `ERR_CODE`.
. Wait timer 0 of the timer leg, with the same prescaler and chain state. Reset KMAC. Write `INTR_ENABLE.kmac_err` = 1. Write `ENTROPY_PERIOD` with `wait_timer` = 0. Write `CFG_SHADOWED` as in step 11. Hold a window of a multiple of the error time at wait timer 8 (fixed by the leaf and logged). Watch vector bit 22 and `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` throughout. Read `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` and `ERR_CODE` at the end. Reset KMAC.
. Counter leg. Run the bring-up if the chain is uninitialized. Reset KMAC. Write `ENTROPY_REFRESH_THRESHOLD_SHADOWED` twice with N + 5. Write `CFG_SHADOWED` twice with `entropy_mode` = EDN, `entropy_ready` = 0 and the keyed program of the open `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test` (cSHAKE, strength 256, `kmac_en` = 1, `sideload` = 0), then twice again with `entropy_ready` = 1. Log the clock count from the rise of `crypto_edn_req_o[1]` to the next rise of `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` as t_reseed. The leaf uses t_reseed only to set the length of a window and does not assume the number of acknowledges of the initial seeding.
. Run N keyed hashes with the key row, the message row and the PREFIX and `KEY_LEN` writes of that program. The leaf does not grade the digests. After each hash write `CMD.done`, wait for `sha3_idle` = 1 and read `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT`. Expect the number of hashes done.
. Write `CMD.hash_cnt_clr`. Read `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` and expect 0. Run one more keyed hash and expect the counter to read 1. Wait for `sha3_idle` = 1.
. Count the acknowledges on `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` for a window of a multiple of t_reseed (fixed by the leaf and logged) with no write. Expect none.
. Write `CMD.entropy_req` while idle. Count the acknowledges on `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` for a window of the same length. Read `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` and expect 0.
. Reseed leg with the threshold R. Run the bring-up if the chain is uninitialized. Reset KMAC. Write `ENTROPY_REFRESH_THRESHOLD_SHADOWED` twice with R. Write `CFG_SHADOWED` as in step 13. Run one keyed hash as in step 14, write `CMD.done` and wait for `sha3_idle` = 1 (a bounded wait). The module starts a command only after the initial seeding, so this hash ends after the seeding. The leaf does not count acknowledges for it. Write `CMD.hash_cnt_clr` and read the counter as 0.
. Run M keyed hashes as in step 14. Read `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` after the last hash. Then hold a settle window of a multiple of t_reseed (fixed by the leaf and logged) with no command, and after it wait until `crypto_edn_req_o[1]` is low (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf), so no reseed request of this run is pending. Count the acknowledges on `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` from the first `CMD.start` of this run (the second hash after the seeding) to the end of that wait. When a counter reseed fires and when EDN acknowledges it are not stated, so a reseed at the last hash (M a multiple of R) is acknowledged inside this run and not in the threshold-0 run.
. Reseed leg with the threshold 0, on the same KMAC. Write `ENTROPY_REFRESH_THRESHOLD_SHADOWED` twice with 0. Write `CMD.hash_cnt_clr` and read the counter as 0. Run M keyed hashes. Count the acknowledges from the first `CMD.start` to the last `CMD.done`. Read `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` after the last hash. Reset KMAC.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KMAC-SWSEED
|In sw mode, after `entropy_ready` and six `ENTROPY_SEED` writes, `CMD.start` reaches `STATUS.sha3_absorb` = 1 and the SHA3-256 digest equals the golden (`kmac.hjson`, `CFG_SHADOWED.entropy_mode`: after six seed writes the module can process commands; `ENTROPY_SEED`). A wrong DUT that does not take six words as the seed stays idle and times out, and one that corrupts the hash returns a wrong digest. Anchor: `STATUS` and `STATE` readback against the `sep_kmac_golden` digest. This run gives t_ctrl, the clean start time that the negative legs use. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-SWSEED PASS seed=<n> seeds=6 absorb=1 t_ctrl_clk=<c> digest==golden`.
|CHK-KMAC-SWSEED-NEG
|With five seed words, and with none, `STATUS.sha3_absorb` stays 0 at every read of the window (`kmac.hjson`, `CFG_SHADOWED.entropy_mode`: the module can process commands only after the six writes). A wrong DUT that starts with fewer words shows `sha3_absorb` = 1. `kmac_err` is logged and not graded, because the text does not state it. Anchor: `STATUS` readback over a window of a multiple of t_ctrl. Control: the six-seed run of the same seed absorbs within t_ctrl (CHK-KMAC-SWSEED). Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-SWSEED-NEG PASS seed=<n> seeds=<5 or 0> window_clk=<w> absorb=0 ctrl_absorb_clk=<c>` and `OBS-KMAC-SWSEED-ERR seed=<n> seeds=<5 or 0> kmac_err=<e>`.
|CHK-KMAC-MODE-LOCK
|`CFG_SHADOWED.entropy_mode` reads idle after the KMAC reset. After `entropy_ready` is written, a later write of another value changes the readback to the written value and does not change the behaviour (`kmac.adoc`, `CFG_SHADOWED.entropy_mode`: the PRNG configuration cannot be changed, and later writes change only the value that the register returns). With sw locked and no seed word, `sha3_absorb` stays 0 for the window, whether the late write is edn or idle. With edn locked and the chain up, the hash runs and returns the golden digest without any seed word, although the late write is sw. A wrong DUT that takes the late write shows an absorb with sw locked, or no absorb with edn locked. The KMAC has no EDN timeout since its reset, which is the stated escape of the lock. Anchor: `CFG_SHADOWED` and `STATUS` readback, and the digest against the golden. Control: t_ctrl of CHK-KMAC-SWSEED sets the window, and the chain is up so that a DUT in edn mode would absorb. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-MODE-LOCK PASS seed=<n> locked=<sw or edn> reset_mode=0 late_write=<m> readback=<v> behaviour=locked`.
|CHK-KMAC-TIMER
|With the chain uninitialized and `entropy_mode` = EDN, `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` rises and `ERR_CODE` is non-zero for each wait timer, and the clock count from the rise of `crypto_edn_req_o[1]` to the rise of the `kmac_err` line is larger for 2T than for T at the same prescaler, and for each wait timer it is larger with the prescaler P than with prescaler 0 (`kmac.adoc`, `ENTROPY_PERIOD.wait_timer`: the entropy module waits up to that many timer pulses after the request, then moves to an error state; `ENTROPY_PERIOD.prescaler`: a timer pulse rises when the clock count hits the prescaler value, so a larger prescaler gives a longer pulse period). A wrong DUT that ignores `wait_timer` shows equal counts for T and 2T, a wrong DUT that ignores the prescaler shows equal counts for P and 0, and a DUT with no timer shows no error. Only the order of the counts is graded, because the exact pulse period is not stated. T is 2 or more, so the phase of the first timer pulse, which the text does not state, cannot make the count with the prescaler P equal the count with prescaler 0. The test reads `kmac_err` and `ERR_CODE` as 0 before the request. No acknowledge occurs in the interval, so the error comes from the starvation. Anchor: `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_CODE` readback, `crypto_edn_req_o[1]`, `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` and vector bit 22. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-TIMER PASS seed=<n> t=<T> p=<P> p0_err_clk=<a0,b0,c0> p_err_clk=<a,b,c> err_code_nonzero=1 acks=0`, logged after both prescaler passes, each list with the counts for T, 2T and 8.
|CHK-KMAC-TIMER0
|With `wait_timer` = 0 no `kmac_err` is set over a window of a multiple of the error time at wait timer 8, and `ERR_CODE` stays 0 (`kmac.adoc`, `ENTROPY_PERIOD.wait_timer`: 0 waits for the EDN response for ever). A wrong DUT that times out with 0 shows `kmac_err` in the window. Anchor: `INTR_STATE` and `ERR_CODE` readback at the end, and vector bit 22 and `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` throughout. Control: wait timer 8 in the same seed, with the same prescaler and chain state, errors within its measured time, and `crypto_edn_req_o[1]` rose, so a request is pending. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-TIMER0 PASS seed=<n> p=<P> window_clk=<w> err=0 req=1 acks=0 ctrl_err_clk=<a>`.
|CHK-KMAC-HASHCNT
|After each of N keyed hashes `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` reads the number of hashes done, so it reads N at the end, with the threshold above N (`kmac.hjson`, `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT`). `CMD.hash_cnt_clr` makes it 0. After one more hash it reads 1, and `CMD.entropy_req` while idle makes it 0 and adds at least one acknowledge on `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` (`CMD.entropy_req`: manual reseed in EDN mode that also clears the counter). A wrong DUT that does not count, does not clear, or does not request a reseed shows the wrong counter or no acknowledge. Anchor: `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` readback and `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]`. Control: a window of the same length without the `entropy_req` write adds no acknowledge, so the acknowledge comes from the write. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-HASHCNT PASS seed=<n> n=<N> cnt=<N> after_clr=0 after_req=0 acks=<k> ctrl_acks=0 window_clk=<w>`.
|CHK-KMAC-AUTORESEED
|With the threshold R (2 to 4) and M keyed hashes with M of R + 2 or more in EDN mode, at least one acknowledge occurs on `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` between the first start of the run and the end of its settle window (the request line low after it), and `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` reads below M after the last hash (`kmac.hjson`, `ENTROPY_REFRESH_THRESHOLD_SHADOWED`: when the counter reaches the threshold the entropy module requests a new seed from EDN and clears the counter). A wrong DUT that counts past the threshold without a reseed request shows no acknowledge and a counter of M. The counted run starts after an uncounted first hash, which can start only after the initial seeding (`kmac.hjson`, `CFG_SHADOWED.entropy_mode`: the module starts processing commands only after the seeding), and after a counter clear. No acknowledge of the initial seeding falls into the counted run, so an acknowledge there comes from a reseed on the counter. The number of reseeds is not graded. Anchor: `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` and `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` readback. Control: CHK-KMAC-AUTORESEED-OFF of the same seed. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-AUTORESEED PASS seed=<n> thr=<R> n=<M> acks=<k> cnt_after=<c> cnt_below_n=1`.
|CHK-KMAC-AUTORESEED-OFF
|With the threshold 0 and M keyed hashes no acknowledge occurs on `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` in the run, and `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` reads M (`kmac.hjson`, `ENTROPY_REFRESH_THRESHOLD_SHADOWED`: with 0 the refresh by the counter does not work and only `CMD.hash_cnt_clr` resets the counter). A wrong DUT that reseeds with the threshold 0 shows an acknowledge or a counter below M. The run starts after the settle window of the R run, with `crypto_edn_req_o[1]` low, so no acknowledge of the R run falls into it. Anchor: `crypto_edn_ack_o[1]` and `ENTROPY_REFRESH_HASH_CNT` readback. Control: the run with R from 2 to 4 of the same seed acknowledges. If it does not, the leaf logs `CTRL-MISSING` and the leaf fails. Evidence: `CHK-KMAC-AUTORESEED-OFF PASS seed=<n> thr=0 n=<M> acks=0 cnt_after=<M> ctrl_acks=<k>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: This test grades the entropy mode contract of KMAC listed in the Objective. The following are not claimed. A mode write after an EDN timeout, which is the stated escape of the lock. The number of reseeds per run and the hash at which the first reseed occurs, because the text states a reseed after each threshold of messages and no count at the boundary. The keyed digests of the counter and reseed legs, which belong to `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`. The `kmac_err` state after a start with fewer than six seed words, which is logged only. The exact period of a timer pulse, which the register text leaves open. The line gate of `kmac_err` belongs to the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`.

'''''

[[sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test]]
===== 1.38 `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Crypto and entropy (OTBN)

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`, 4 seeds; testlist `crypto.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_otbn_cmd_status_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip with the CPU-LSU master readback and write of the OTBN registers and memories (approved); `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` (approved, vector bit 29); the TRNG reset bit of `SW_RESET_N` (approved, holds the RND read); the compute program and the independent Python operator golden of the open `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test` helper. The OTBN status probe, a read-only probe on the operational state code (`status_q` of the OTBN core) sampled on every `clk_i`, is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. It serves CHK-OTBN-CMD-BUSY and the reset arc of CHK-OTBN-INSN. No inject.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP. `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the command sequence (3 to 5 commands from EXECUTE, SEC_WIPE_DMEM and SEC_WIPE_IMEM, with each of the three commands at least once), the gap before each command (0 to 200 clocks), the program operation (ADD, XOR or AND) and its operands, the pair a and b (0 <= a < b <= 40 extra independent instructions), the order of the two enable runs of the done-line leg, the number k of busy `CTRL` writes (1 to 4), the unrecognised `CMD` value (an 8-bit `CMD.cmd` code other than 0xd8, 0xc3 and 0x1e, with bits 31:8 at 0) and the number j of busy `CMD` writes (1 to 4). `reseed = 4` samples different sequences, gaps, operands, pairs, orders and counts. The legs are the same on every seed.

*Objective*: `CMD` accepts EXECUTE (0xd8), SEC_WIPE_DMEM (0xc3) and SEC_WIPE_IMEM (0x1e). Each command moves `STATUS` from IDLE (0x00) to its busy code (0x01, 0x02, 0x03), back to IDLE, and raises `INTR_STATE.done`. `ERR_BITS` stays 0. `INSN_CNT` is the number of executed instructions. The done line follows `INTR_ENABLE`. A `CTRL` write while OTBN is not idle is ignored, and the same write while OTBN is idle is stored. A `CMD` write while OTBN is not idle is ignored, and an unrecognised `CMD` value is ignored. An `RND` read stalls OTBN until a number arrives from the EDN (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/otbn/data/csr.yml`, `rnd`), and native requests get no acknowledgement during the TRNG adapter clear (`hw/sys/sep/doc/trng.adoc`, Reset and Recovery Boundaries); this holds OTBN busy for the busy-write leg. Spec: `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/overlay/regs/otbn/regs/gen/adoc/otbn.adoc` (`INTR_STATE`, `INTR_ENABLE`, `INTR_TEST`, `CTRL`, `CMD`, `STATUS`, `ERR_BITS`, `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE`, `INSN_CNT`); `vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/otbn/data/otbn.hjson` (`CMD`: writes are ignored if OTBN is not idle, unrecognized commands are ignored); `hw/sys/sep/doc/otbn.adoc` (OCAH Modifications); `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc` (`SW_RESET_N`).

*Test Coverage*: Command sequences of 3 to 5 commands that hold each of EXECUTE, SEC_WIPE_DMEM and SEC_WIPE_IMEM at least once on every seed, with drawn gaps, each command issued at STATUS IDLE. Two programs of a and b instructions for `INSN_CNT`. The done line with the enable set and clear, and with `INTR_TEST.done`. The busy-write leg: OTBN held busy (STATUS 0x01) by an RND read that gets no acknowledge while the TRNG reset is held; k writes of `software_errs_fatal` = 1 while busy; the same write while idle after the chain returns. The busy-write leg also writes `CMD` = SEC_WIPE_DMEM j times while `STATUS` is 0x01. The ignored-command leg writes one unrecognised `CMD` value at IDLE. Error injection: a `CTRL` write while busy, a `CMD` write while busy and an unrecognised `CMD` value at IDLE. Extends `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`, which runs EXECUTE of an ADD, XOR or AND program and grades `ERR_BITS` and a DMEM result. This entry grades only the wipe commands, the status sequence, `INSN_CNT`, the busy-write rule of `CTRL` and the ignored `CMD` writes. The owner of the open test keeps the ADD, XOR and AND results. The done-line check extends `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`, which raises OTBN done with `INTR_TEST` and brackets it with a write-one clear. This entry owns the OTBN line cells of the extension leg of `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`: the enable clear, the rise, the enable flip and the write-one clear with the enable clear, for the real completion event and for `INTR_TEST`. The enable-set `INTR_TEST` cell is a control, and the owner of the open test keeps its `INTR_TEST` walk. Functional coverage: `sep_otbn_op_cg` (`cp_cmd`, `x_cmd_first_busy`, `cp_cmd_ignored`, `cp_insn_cnt`, `cp_done_event`, `cp_post_reset_arc`), Phase 1 `sep_otbn_execute_cg`, `sep_otbn_err_lock_cg` (`cp_ctrl_write_legality`), `sep_crypto_intr_cg` (`x_line_gate`, `cp_hw_raise` otbn_done).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Select the internal source in `SEP_CPU_CTRL.EXT_TRNG_SRC_SEL`. Bring up ESRC, CSRNG and EDN. Set `SW_RESET_N` to the baseline of the common rules with the bits of KMAC, AES, HMAC and ABR clear. Log the baseline.
. Draw the plan with `SepSeededRng`. Log `PLAN seed=<n>` with the sequence, the gaps, the operation, the operands, a, b, the order of the enable runs, k, the unrecognised `CMD` value and j.
. Reset OTBN (bit 1 of `SW_RESET_N` low, then high). Wait for `STATUS` = IDLE (a bounded wait). Log every `STATUS` value read.
. Read `INTR_STATE.done` and log it as `OBS-OTBN-INITDONE`. The initial wipe is not a command, so the leaf does not grade the value. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done` and read it as 0.
. Command leg. Steps 6 to 9 run once for each command of the drawn sequence. Wait the drawn gap with no bus access.
. For EXECUTE, load the compute program into IMEM, stage the operands in DMEM and pre-seed the result word with the complement of the golden. Do this before every EXECUTE, because a wipe clears the memories.
. Read `INTR_STATE.done` and expect 0. Read `STATUS` and expect IDLE. Write `CMD` with the command.
. Poll `STATUS` until it reads IDLE (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the check). Record every value read. Log the clock count from the `CMD` write to IDLE.
. Read `INTR_STATE.done`, `ERR_BITS` and `FATAL_ALERT_CAUSE`. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done` and read it as 0. After the last command take t_cmd as the longest logged count.
. `INSN_CNT` leg. Steps 10 to 12 run for the program P(a), then for P(b). P(k) is the compute program with k extra independent ADD instructions. Reset OTBN, wait for `STATUS` = IDLE and clear `INTR_STATE.done` as in steps 3 and 4. Read `INSN_CNT` and expect 0 (the reset value in `otbn.adoc`).
. Load P(k) into IMEM. Stage the operands in DMEM and pre-seed the result word with the complement of the golden. Write `CMD` = EXECUTE. Wait for `STATUS` = IDLE (a bounded wait).
. Read `INSN_CNT` and the DMEM result word.
. Done-line leg. Steps 13 to 16 run for two EXECUTE runs in the drawn order, one with `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 1 and one with it = 0. Reset OTBN, wait for `STATUS` = IDLE and write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done` as in steps 3 and 4, so that the `done` of the earlier run does not remain. Read `INTR_STATE.done` and vector bit 29 as 0. Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` with the value of the run and read `INTR_STATE.done` as 0.
. Load the compute program and the operands. Write `CMD` = EXECUTE. Sample vector bit 29 of `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` at every clock from the `CMD` write. Wait for `STATUS` = IDLE and `INTR_STATE.done` = 1 (a bounded wait).
. With the enable clear, keep bit 29 under watch for a window of a multiple of t_cmd (fixed by the leaf and logged). Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 1. Wait for bit 29 = 1 (a bounded wait). With the enable set, expect bit 29 = 1 with the state bit.
. Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done`. Read it as 0 and read bit 29 as 0.
. `INTR_TEST` leg. Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 0, then write `INTR_TEST.done` = 1. Read `INTR_STATE.done` as 1 and keep bit 29 under watch for the window. Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 1 and wait for bit 29 = 1. Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 0 and wait for bit 29 = 0 (a bounded wait). Write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done` with the enable clear, then read `INTR_STATE.done` as 0 and bit 29 as 0. Write `INTR_ENABLE.done` = 1, write `INTR_TEST.done` = 1, wait for bit 29 = 1, then write 1 to `INTR_STATE.done` and read bit 29 as 0.
